`ARCHITECTURE.md`:

```md
# Architecture

Tuxgate is a Cargo workspace of six crates. One of them is a binary; the rest are libraries with a single direction of dependency.

```
                      tuxgate-cli  (binary: tuxgate)
                           │
        ┌──────────────────┼───────────────────┬──────────────────┐
        ▼                  ▼                   ▼                  ▼
 tuxgate-reporting   tuxgate-bridge   tuxgate-compatibility   tuxgate-diagnostics
        │                  │                   │                  │
        └──────────────────┴─────────┬─────────┴──────────────────┘
                                     ▼
                               tuxgate-core
```

`tuxgate-reporting` also depends on the three domain crates, because a report assembles their results. No domain crate depends on another domain crate.

## Design rules

1. **Read-only by default.** Probes read files and run query commands. The only code path that changes anything is `tuxgate windows reboot`, and only after the user types a confirmation.
2. **No privileges.** Nothing runs as root, nothing is installed setuid, there is no daemon and no polkit rule. What cannot be read without privileges is reported as `unverifiable`.
3. **No network.** No crate in the dependency tree can open a socket. `cargo-deny` enforces this with a ban list (see `deny.toml`).
4. **Honest statuses.** Every result uses the five-value `Status` enum below. Local detection of a security feature is never presented as acceptance by an anti-cheat.
5. **Testable anywhere.** All system access goes through `SystemContext`, which takes a root directory and a command runner. The same code inspects `/`, an offline tree, or a test fixture.
6. **No Riot material.** The repository contains no Riot file, logo or asset, and the code never reads Riot credentials.

## The status model

Defined in `tuxgate-core::finding`:

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `detected` | Looked for and found |
| `not_detected` | Looked for; absent or disabled |
| `unverifiable` | The check could not be completed (permission, missing tool, interface not exposed) |
| `not_applicable` | The check makes no sense on this system |
| `blocked_external` | Decided by something outside this machine and this project, typically a vendor requirement |

`detected` is a statement of fact, not of quality: a detected hypervisor is bad news for anti-cheat. A `Finding` carries an identifier, a category, a status, an optional value, the evidence (files read, commands run) and notes.

## Crates

### tuxgate-core

Shared vocabulary and services.

| Module | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `finding` | `Status`, `Category`, `Finding`, `Evidence`, the attestation disclaimer |
| `context` | `SystemContext`: rooted file access, command execution with a time limit, environment; `CommandRunner` with the real, null and (in tests) scripted implementations |
| `probe` | The `Probe` trait and `ProbeRegistry` (module management: registration, ordering, disabling) |
| `config` | TOML configuration with strict parsing and validation |
| `paths` | XDG directories |
| `efi` | Read-only decoding of efivarfs variables |
| `text` | Neutralising control characters in text that comes from the system |
| `tools` | The list of optional external programs and the Arch packages that provide them (dependency management) |
| `time` | UTC date formatting without a date-time dependency |
| `error` | `CoreError` |
| `testing` | Fixtures, scripted runner and four ready-made machines; compiled only for tests |

### tuxgate-diagnostics

One probe per subject, each implementing `Probe`: distribution, kernel, CPU, graphics, Vulkan, Wine, Proton, firmware and Secure Boot, TPM, IOMMU, kernel hardening (lockdown, module signature enforcement, kexec, LSMs), virtualisation, boot manager, optional tools.

`readiness` derives, from those findings, a view of the system against the attested-boot model used by Easy Anti-Cheat Linux v2. It is informational: the view is labelled as local detection, and it says nothing about Riot Vanguard.

### tuxgate-compatibility

- `data/games.toml` and `data/sources.toml`, embedded at build time. Every status in the database cites a source with a date and a reliability grade. Statuses are documented from sources, not tested by the project.
- Validation of the database (unknown sources, missing evidence, bad dates) runs as unit tests, so a bad edit fails CI.
- A local history of the user's own test results, with regression detection.

### tuxgate-bridge

- Detects an existing Windows installation from three independent, read-only sources: UEFI boot entries, the Windows boot manager on the EFI System Partition, and systemd-boot's entry list.
- Builds a reboot plan and, after confirmation, executes it. Two methods are supported: `systemctl reboot --boot-loader-entry=` for systemd-boot, and a one-time UEFI `BootNext` set through `efibootmgr`. Neither changes the permanent boot order or writes to the ESP.
- Has no partitioning, formatting, boot-manager installation or firmware-setting code.

### tuxgate-reporting

Assembles a `Report` from the other crates, renders it as text, Markdown or JSON, and anonymises it. Reports are written locally and never transmitted.

### tuxgate-cli

Argument parsing (clap), terminal rendering and exit codes. Contains no system logic of its own.

## Deviation from the original brief

The brief listed a `research` module. Research is documentation, not code, so it lives in `RESEARCH.md` and in the machine-readable `data/sources.toml`, which the compatibility database cites. A crate would have had nothing to compile.

## Privileged operations

There is exactly one, and Tuxgate does not perform it itself:

| Operation | When | How | What it changes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Set UEFI `BootNext` | `tuxgate windows reboot`, EFI method, after typed confirmation | `/usr/bin/pkexec /usr/bin/efibootmgr --bootnext XXXX` (or `sudo` when pkexec is absent), resolved in system directories only, never through `PATH` | One firmware variable that the firmware clears after the next boot |

The systemd-boot method needs no elevation from Tuxgate: `systemctl` asks logind, which applies the system's polkit policy.

## Data flow of `tuxgate doctor`

1. Load configuration (`~/.config/tuxgate/config.toml`, optional).
2. Build a `SystemContext` for the host, or for `--root DIR`.
3. Run the probe registry, skipping disabled probes.
4. Derive the readiness view and detect Windows.
5. Render as text or JSON.

Nothing is written to disk and nothing leaves the machine.

## Testing strategy

| Level | Where | What |
|---|---|---|
| Unit | each crate's `#[cfg(test)]` modules | Parsers, classification, configuration, error handling, anonymisation |
| Scenario | `tuxgate-core::testing::scenarios` | Four simulated machines exercised by every crate |
| CLI | `crates/tuxgate-cli/tests` | The built binary against scenarios through `--root` |
| Packaging | CI, Arch container | `makepkg`, `namcap`, install, run, remove, residue check |
| Hardware | `docs/TESTING.md` | Manual checklist; not automated and not claimed as done |

A passing build or test suite says nothing about whether any game runs.

```

`CHANGELOG.md`:

```md
# Changelog

All notable changes to Tuxgate are recorded here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and the project uses [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html). While the version is 0.x, minor versions may change interfaces and file formats.

## [Unreleased]

## [0.1.0] - 2026-11-28

First release.

### Added

- `tuxgate doctor`: fourteen read-only probes covering distribution, kernel and CachyOS kernel variant, CPU, graphics adapters and NVIDIA driver, Vulkan, Wine, Steam and Proton, UEFI and Secure Boot, TPM, IOMMU, kernel lockdown, module signature enforcement, kexec, BPF LSM, virtualisation, boot manager and optional tools.
- A five-value status model: detected, not detected, unverifiable, not applicable, blocked (external).
- An informational view of the system against the attested-boot model documented for Easy Anti-Cheat Linux v2.
- `tuxgate status`, a short overview.
- `tuxgate games` and `tuxgate check <game>`: a compatibility database of six Riot titles, each status citing graded sources.
- `tuxgate games record` and `tuxgate games history`: a local test history with regression detection.
- `tuxgate report`: local reports in text, Markdown and JSON, anonymised by default.
- `tuxgate windows detect`: read-only detection of an existing Windows installation.
- `tuxgate windows reboot`: a one-time restart into Windows through systemd-boot or UEFI `BootNext`, after a typed confirmation.
- `tuxgate sources`, `tuxgate probes`, shell completions and a manual page.
- `--json` output for every command, and `--root` to inspect an offline system tree.
- An Arch and CachyOS package recipe (`packaging/arch/PKGBUILD`).
- `RESEARCH.md`: a feasibility study of Riot Vanguard on Linux based on 35 graded sources.

### Security

- No network access: the dependency tree contains no networking crate, enforced by `deny.toml`.
- The commands of `windows reboot` are resolved in system directories and run by absolute path; `PATH` is ignored for them.
- Text read from the system (file contents, directory names, firmware boot entry names) has control characters neutralised before it is displayed or written to a report.
- `unsafe` code is forbidden across the workspace.

### Known limitations

- Not yet validated on real hardware by the maintainers.
- No game has been tested; statuses are documented from sources.

```

`CONTRIBUTING.md`:

```md
# Contributing

Thank you for considering a contribution. Tuxgate is small on purpose; please read the scope section before opening a pull request.

## Scope

Tuxgate diagnoses, documents and helps with dual boot. It stays strictly on the right side of anti-cheat.

**Welcome:**

- New or better diagnostics, as long as they are read-only and need no privileges.
- Corrections and updates to the compatibility database, with sources.
- Support for more boot managers in `windows detect` and `windows reboot`.
- Test results from real hardware (see `docs/TESTING.md`).
- Documentation, translations of the README, packaging for other distributions.

**Declined, whatever the reason given:**

- Anything that hides a virtual machine, fakes TPM, Secure Boot, IOMMU or an attestation, loads or emulates a Windows kernel driver, or patches, unpacks or redistributes a game publisher's files.
- "Workarounds" for an anti-cheat refusal, including links to them in documentation.
- Networking code: telemetry, update checks, uploads.
- Anything that needs Tuxgate to run as root, install a service or change boot configuration permanently.

If you are unsure whether an idea fits, open an issue first.

## Development setup

You need Rust 1.85 or newer. The code builds and its tests run on Linux, macOS and Windows, because all system access goes through `SystemContext` and the tests use simulated machines.

```sh
cargo fmt --all --check
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings
cargo test --workspace
```

All three must pass. CI runs them, plus `cargo deny check` and a package build in an Arch container.

To try the binary against a simulated machine, write a small tree and point `--root` at it; or run it on your own system, which is the point of the tool:

```sh
cargo run -p tuxgate-cli -- doctor --verbose
```

## Code rules

- `unsafe` is forbidden. `unwrap()` is linted against; use `expect("why")` in tests and real error handling elsewhere.
- A probe must never fail and never guess. What cannot be determined is an `Unverifiable` finding with a note saying why.
- A missing file usually means "not present"; a permission error means "cannot tell". Keep the two apart (`FsError`).
- Every security-related finding ends with `.not_an_attestation()`.
- Read system files through `SystemContext`, never `std::fs` directly, or the code cannot be tested.
- Commands that change state or run elevated are resolved with `find_system_program`, never through `PATH`.
- New behaviour comes with tests. For a probe, add the relevant files to a scenario in `tuxgate-core/src/testing.rs` or build a `Fixture` in the test.
- Do not add a dependency without a strong reason. `deny.toml` lists crates that will be refused.

## Updating the compatibility database

The database is `data/games.toml`; its sources are in `data/sources.toml`. The rules are enforced by unit tests in `tuxgate-compatibility`:

1. Every status cites at least one source that exists in `sources.toml`.
2. `blocked` requires a primary or reputable secondary source (grade A, A- or B). Forum posts are not enough to say a vendor blocks a game.
3. `basis = "documented"` requires such a source too. Otherwise use `"inferred"` or `"community"`.
4. A `blocked` entry lists at least one legitimate way to play.
5. Never write a status as tested. The project tests no game; statuses are documented.
6. Bump `updated` in `games.toml`, and `accessed` in `sources.toml` if you re-read the sources.

Grades are defined in `RESEARCH.md`, section 2. When a source cannot be read directly (login wall, bot protection), grade it `A-` and say so.

If your change alters a conclusion of `RESEARCH.md`, update that document in the same pull request.

## Commits and pull requests

- One logical change per commit, with a message that says what changed and why. Prefixes such as `feat(bridge):`, `fix(cli):`, `docs:` are used but not enforced.
- Describe how you tested. If you tested on hardware, say which machine, boot manager and kernel.
- Add an entry to `CHANGELOG.md` under "Unreleased".

## Reporting bugs

Attach the output of `tuxgate report --format markdown`. It is anonymised by default; read it before posting.

Security problems go through private reporting; see `SECURITY.md`.

## Licence

By contributing, you agree that your contribution is licensed under GPL-3.0-or-later, the licence of the project.

```

`INSTALLATION.md`:

```md
# Installation

Tuxgate targets CachyOS and Arch Linux on x86_64.

> The only installation method that exists today is building the package from the source tree, described below. There is no `pacman -S tuxgate` and no AUR package yet. See [docs/DISTRIBUTION.md](docs/DISTRIBUTION.md).

## Requirements

| Needed for | Package |
|---|---|
| Building | `base-devel`, `git`, `rust` (or `rustup` with the stable toolchain); Rust 1.85 or newer |
| Running | `glibc`, `gcc-libs` |

Everything else is optional. Tuxgate reports "unverifiable" instead of failing when a tool is missing.

| Optional package | Enables |
|---|---|
| `systemd` | Listing boot entries (`bootctl`), restarting (`systemctl`), hypervisor detection |
| `efibootmgr` | Restarting into Windows on systems that do not use systemd-boot |
| `polkit` | The administrator prompt for that one `efibootmgr` call |
| `util-linux` | Looking for Windows partitions (`lsblk`) |
| `vulkan-tools` | Confirming that a Vulkan driver actually loads |
| `wine` | Reporting the Wine version |

## Build and install

```sh
git clone https://github.com/LoanPleurmeauBonnin/tuxgate.git
cd tuxgate
./packaging/arch/make-source.sh
cd packaging/arch && makepkg -si
```

What happens:

1. `make-source.sh` archives the current commit next to the PKGBUILD. Uncommitted changes are not included.
2. `cargo fetch --locked` downloads the Rust crates pinned in `Cargo.lock`, each verified by checksum. Nothing else is downloaded.
3. The program is compiled in release mode and the test suite runs (`check()`).
4. pacman installs the package. No install script runs.

To build without installing, use `makepkg` and install later with `sudo pacman -U tuxgate-*.pkg.tar.zst`.

## Verify

```sh
tuxgate --version
tuxgate status
tuxgate doctor
```

None of these needs `sudo`. If you find yourself wanting to run Tuxgate as root, don't: it is designed not to need it.

## Update

```sh
cd tuxgate
git pull
./packaging/arch/make-source.sh
cd packaging/arch && makepkg -si
```

pacman upgrades the installed package in place.

## Remove

```sh
sudo pacman -R tuxgate
```

This removes every file the package installed. Files Tuxgate created in your home directory are yours and are left alone, as with any pacman package:

```sh
rm -r ~/.config/tuxgate ~/.local/share/tuxgate
```

Those directories only exist if you wrote a configuration or recorded a test.

## Configuration

Optional. Create `~/.config/tuxgate/config.toml` (or `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/tuxgate/config.toml`). An example with every key and its default is installed at `/usr/share/doc/tuxgate/config.example.toml`:

```toml
[output]
color = "auto"          # "auto", "always" or "never"

[report]
anonymize = true        # strip home paths, UUIDs and MAC addresses from reports

[probes]
disabled = []           # for example ["wine", "vulkan"]; see `tuxgate probes`
command_timeout_secs = 5
```

A misspelt key is an error, not a silent no-op.

## Files

| Path | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `~/.config/tuxgate/config.toml` | Your configuration (optional) |
| `~/.local/share/tuxgate/history.toml` | Your recorded tests (`tuxgate games record`) |

Tuxgate writes nowhere else, except the report file you name with `tuxgate report --output`.

## Shell completion

The package installs completions for bash, zsh and fish. For another setup:

```sh
tuxgate completions bash   # or zsh, fish, elvish, powershell
```

## Other distributions

```sh
cargo build --release
./target/release/tuxgate doctor
```

The diagnostics are distribution-neutral. The package recipe is Arch-specific.

## Inspecting another system

`--root DIR` inspects a system tree mounted at `DIR` (a chroot, a mounted disk) instead of the running system. No command is executed in this mode, so results that need one are "unverifiable".

```sh
tuxgate --root /mnt doctor
```

```

`README.fr.md`:

```md
# Tuxgate

*English: [README.md](README.md). Le reste de la documentation est en anglais.*

Un compagnon non officiel pour les jeux Riot Games sous Linux. Tuxgate diagnostique votre système, indique quels jeux peuvent y fonctionner et pourquoi, et vous aide à redémarrer sous Windows quand un jeu l'exige.

> **Tuxgate ne fait pas fonctionner les jeux protégés par Vanguard sous Linux, et rien ne le permet aujourd'hui.**
> Riot Vanguard n'existe pas pour Linux. Tuxgate n'installe, n'émule, ne contourne et ne trompe aucun anti-cheat, et ne le fera jamais.

Tuxgate est un projet indépendant. Il n'est ni affilié à Riot Games ou à CachyOS, ni approuvé ou soutenu par eux. Il n'utilise aucun logo, fichier ou contenu de Riot.

## Ce qu'il fait

| Commande | Rôle |
|---|---|
| `tuxgate doctor` | Diagnostic complet : distribution, noyau, carte graphique et pilotes, Vulkan, Wine, Proton, Secure Boot, TPM, IOMMU, durcissement du noyau, virtualisation, chargeur d'amorçage |
| `tuxgate status` | La même chose en huit lignes |
| `tuxgate games` | Les jeux Riot et leur état documenté sous Linux |
| `tuxgate check valorant` | Pourquoi un jeu est bloqué ou non, avec les sources et les alternatives légitimes |
| `tuxgate report` | Un rapport local en texte, Markdown ou JSON, anonymisé par défaut |
| `tuxgate windows detect` | Recherche une installation Windows existante, en lecture seule |
| `tuxgate windows reboot` | Redémarre une fois sous Windows, après affichage du plan et saisie d'une confirmation |

S'y ajoutent `tuxgate games record` et `tuxgate games history` pour conserver vos propres essais et repérer les régressions, `tuxgate sources`, `tuxgate probes`, et l'option `--json` sur chaque commande.

## Ce qu'il ne fait pas

- Il n'installe ni n'active Vanguard. Cette commande n'existe pas.
- Il ne masque pas une machine virtuelle, ne simule ni TPM ni Secure Boot, et ne modifie aucun fichier de Riot.
- Il n'envoie rien nulle part. Le programme ne contient aucun code réseau.
- Il n'a pas besoin des droits root, n'installe aucun service et ne change jamais durablement votre configuration de démarrage.

## L'état réel

| Jeu | Sous Linux aujourd'hui | Raison |
|---|---|---|
| VALORANT | Bloqué | Exige Riot Vanguard, qui n'existe que pour Windows |
| League of Legends | Bloqué | Vanguard obligatoire depuis 2024 ; Riot a indiqué ne pas prendre Linux en charge |
| Teamfight Tactics (PC) | Bloqué | Passe par le client League of Legends |
| 2XKO | Bloqué | Exige Vanguard |
| Legends of Runeterra | Retours communautaires partagés | Aucune exigence Vanguard trouvée ; non pris en charge par Riot ; régression signalée depuis octobre 2026 |

Ces états sont **documentés à partir de sources, et non testés par ce projet**. Chacun cite ses sources : lancez `tuxgate check <jeu>`.

La seule façon de jouer à un jeu protégé par Vanguard sur une machine qui tourne sous Linux est le double démarrage avec Windows. Tuxgate rend cela moins pénible ; il ne le remplace pas.

Le raisonnement complet, appuyé sur 35 sources classées par fiabilité, se trouve dans [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md).

## « Détection locale » ne veut pas dire « validation »

Tuxgate indique si Secure Boot est actif, si un TPM 2.0 existe, si l'IOMMU fonctionne, etc. Ce sont des observations faites sur votre machine. Ce n'est **pas** une attestation, et aucun anti-cheat ne les reconnaît. Chaque ligne concernée le rappelle.

Chaque résultat porte l'un de ces cinq états :

| État | Signification |
|---|---|
| detected | Recherché et trouvé |
| not detected | Recherché ; absent ou désactivé |
| unverifiable | Impossible à vérifier (droits, outil manquant) |
| not applicable | Sans objet sur ce système |
| blocked (external) | Décidé par un éditeur, pas par cette machine |

« Detected » est un constat, pas un compliment : un hyperviseur détecté est une mauvaise nouvelle.

### La vue « démarrage attesté »

En octobre 2026, Epic a annoncé Easy Anti-Cheat Linux v2, qui n'accepte un système Linux que si sa chaîne de démarrage est vérifiable, et CachyOS fournit un noyau signé à cet effet. `tuxgate doctor` montre quelles propriétés de ce modèle sont observables sur votre machine. C'est une information. Ce n'est pas une validation par Epic, et cela n'a aucun effet sur Riot Vanguard.

## Installation

Tuxgate est développé pour CachyOS et Arch Linux.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/LoanPleurmeauBonnin/tuxgate.git
cd tuxgate
./packaging/arch/make-source.sh
cd packaging/arch && makepkg -si
```

Il n'existe pour l'instant ni paquet AUR ni dépôt pacman. Voir [INSTALLATION.md](INSTALLATION.md) pour le détail, les mises à jour et la désinstallation, et [docs/DISTRIBUTION.md](docs/DISTRIBUTION.md) pour ce qui est prévu.

## Confidentialité et sécurité

- Aucune télémétrie, aucun accès réseau, aucun compte, aucun identifiant Riot lu ou stocké.
- Les rapports restent sur votre disque et sont anonymisés par défaut : chemins personnels, UUID et adresses MAC sont retirés. Le nom d'hôte, les numéros de série et les identifiants de disque ne sont jamais collectés.
- Une seule opération peut demander les droits d'administration : la sélection de l'entrée de démarrage ponctuelle lors de `tuxgate windows reboot`, sur les systèmes sans systemd-boot. La commande exacte est affichée avant, et rien ne se passe tant que vous n'avez pas saisi `reboot to windows`.

Détails : [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md).

## Limites connues

- **Pas encore validé sur du matériel réel par les mainteneurs.** La suite de tests s'exécute sur des machines simulées ; le paquet est construit, installé, mis à jour et désinstallé par la CI dans des conteneurs Arch Linux et CachyOS. Voir [docs/TESTING.md](docs/TESTING.md).
- Aucun jeu n'a été testé. Un état peut changer à chaque mise à jour du client.
- La détection de Windows nécessite l'UEFI. En BIOS hérité, le résultat est « unverifiable ».
- `windows reboot` gère systemd-boot et `BootNext` UEFI. Si Windows n'a pas d'entrée dans le firmware et que vous n'utilisez pas systemd-boot, choisissez-le dans votre menu de démarrage.
- Seule l'architecture x86_64 est empaquetée.

## Feuille de route

- Validation sur du matériel CachyOS réel avec les quatre chargeurs d'amorçage.
- Première version étiquetée et paquets AUR `tuxgate` et `tuxgate-git`.
- Lecture des registres PCR du TPM et du journal de démarrage, à titre d'information.
- Mise à jour communautaire de la base de jeux, avec les mêmes règles de sourçage.

Dépend de Riot, et non de ce projet : toute prise en charge de Vanguard sous Linux. Si Riot l'annonce un jour, Tuxgate en documentera les exigences et les vérifiera. Il ne peut pas la créer.

## Licence

GPL-3.0-or-later. Voir [LICENSE](LICENSE).

VALORANT, League of Legends, Teamfight Tactics, Legends of Runeterra, 2XKO, Wild Rift et Riot Vanguard sont des marques de Riot Games, Inc. Elles ne sont citées ici que pour décrire la compatibilité.

```

`README.md`:

```md
# Tuxgate

*Français : [README.fr.md](README.fr.md)*

An unofficial companion for Riot Games titles on Linux. Tuxgate diagnoses your system, tells you which titles can run on it and why, and helps you restart into Windows when a game needs it.

> **Tuxgate does not make Vanguard-protected games run on Linux, and nothing can today.**
> Riot Vanguard has no Linux version. Tuxgate does not install, emulate, bypass, spoof or interact with any anti-cheat, and never will.

Tuxgate is an independent project. It is not affiliated with, endorsed by or sponsored by Riot Games or CachyOS. It uses no Riot logo, asset or file.

## What it does

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `tuxgate doctor` | Full diagnostic: distribution, kernel, GPU and drivers, Vulkan, Wine, Proton, Secure Boot, TPM, IOMMU, kernel hardening, virtualisation, boot manager |
| `tuxgate status` | The same in eight lines |
| `tuxgate games` | Riot titles and their documented status on Linux |
| `tuxgate check valorant` | Why a title is blocked or not, with graded sources and legitimate alternatives |
| `tuxgate report` | A local report in text, Markdown or JSON, anonymised by default |
| `tuxgate windows detect` | Finds an existing Windows installation, read-only |
| `tuxgate windows reboot` | Restarts into Windows once, after showing the plan and asking you to type a confirmation |

Also: `tuxgate games record` and `tuxgate games history` to keep your own test results and spot regressions, `tuxgate sources`, `tuxgate probes`, and `--json` on every command.

## What it does not do

- It does not install or enable Vanguard. There is no such command.
- It does not hide a virtual machine, fake a TPM or Secure Boot state, or patch any Riot file.
- It does not send anything anywhere. There is no networking code in the program.
- It needs no root access, installs no service, and never changes your boot configuration permanently.

## The honest picture

| Title | On Linux today | Why |
|---|---|---|
| VALORANT | Blocked | Requires Riot Vanguard, which exists only for Windows |
| League of Legends | Blocked | Vanguard mandatory since 2024; Riot has stated it does not support Linux |
| Teamfight Tactics (PC) | Blocked | Played through the League client |
| 2XKO | Blocked | Requires Vanguard |
| Legends of Runeterra | Mixed community reports | No Vanguard requirement found; unsupported by Riot; a regression is reported since October 2026 |

These statuses are **documented from sources, not tested by this project**. Each one cites its sources: run `tuxgate check <title>`.

The only way to play a Vanguard-protected title on a machine that runs Linux is to dual boot Windows. Tuxgate makes that less painful; it does not replace it.

The full reasoning, with 35 graded sources, is in [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md).

## Why "local detection" is not "validation"

Tuxgate reports whether Secure Boot is on, whether a TPM 2.0 exists, whether the IOMMU is active, and so on. These are observations made on your machine. They are **not** an attestation, and no anti-cheat recognises them. Every such line in the output says so.

Every result uses one of five statuses:

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| detected | Looked for and found |
| not detected | Looked for; absent or disabled |
| unverifiable | Could not be checked (permission, missing tool) |
| not applicable | Makes no sense on this system |
| blocked (external) | Decided by a vendor, not by this machine |

"Detected" is a fact, not a compliment: a detected hypervisor is bad news.

### The attested-boot view

In October 2026 Epic announced Easy Anti-Cheat Linux v2, which accepts a Linux system only when its boot chain can be verified, and CachyOS ships a signed kernel for it. `tuxgate doctor` shows which properties of that model are observable on your machine. This is informational. It is not a validation by Epic, and it has no bearing on Riot Vanguard.

## Installation

Tuxgate is developed for CachyOS and Arch Linux.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/LoanPleurmeauBonnin/tuxgate.git
cd tuxgate
./packaging/arch/make-source.sh
cd packaging/arch && makepkg -si
```

There is no AUR package and no pacman repository yet. See [INSTALLATION.md](INSTALLATION.md) for details, updates and removal, and [docs/DISTRIBUTION.md](docs/DISTRIBUTION.md) for what is planned.

On other distributions, `cargo build --release` produces `target/release/tuxgate`; the diagnostics work, the package does not apply.

## Example

Output abridged and wrapped for reading.

```text
$ tuxgate check valorant
VALORANT
  Status:      blocked by an external requirement
  Basis:       documented by sources
  Anti-cheat:  Riot Vanguard (Windows kernel driver)

  Cannot run on Linux. It requires Riot Vanguard, which exists only for Windows.

Legitimate ways to play today
  - Dual boot: play on Windows installed on the same machine. See `tuxgate windows detect`.

On this system
  [   blocked    ] VALORANT cannot run on this system. No setting on this machine
                   changes that: the requirement is set by Riot Games.
  [   detected   ] Windows installation found: firmware entry Boot0000 "Windows Boot Manager"
      `tuxgate windows reboot` can restart into Windows once.
```

## Privacy and security

- No telemetry, no network access, no account, no Riot credentials read or stored.
- Reports stay on your disk and are anonymised by default: home paths, UUIDs and MAC addresses are removed. Host name, serial numbers and disk identifiers are never collected in the first place.
- One operation can ask for administrator rights: setting the firmware's one-time boot entry during `tuxgate windows reboot`, on systems without systemd-boot. The exact command is shown first and nothing happens until you type `reboot to windows`.

Details: [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md).

## Known limitations

- **Not validated on real hardware by the maintainers yet.** The test suite runs against simulated machines; the package is built, installed, upgraded and removed in Arch Linux and CachyOS containers by CI. See [docs/TESTING.md](docs/TESTING.md) for the manual checklist and what has and has not been run.
- No game was tested. A status can change with any client update.
- Windows detection needs UEFI. On legacy BIOS systems it reports "unverifiable".
- `windows reboot` supports systemd-boot and UEFI `BootNext`. If Windows has no firmware entry and you do not use systemd-boot, choose it from your boot menu.
- If the EFI System Partition is readable by root only, some boot checks report "unverifiable". Tuxgate will not elevate itself to find out.
- Only x86_64 is packaged.

## Roadmap

Near term:

- Validation on real CachyOS hardware across the four boot managers, with results recorded in `docs/TESTING.md`.
- A first tagged release and the `tuxgate` and `tuxgate-git` AUR packages.
- Reading TPM PCR values and the boot event log, for the owner's information.

Later:

- A community-maintained update channel for the games database, with the same sourcing rules.
- Readiness checks for other attested-boot anti-cheat deployments as distributions document them.
- Packages for other distributions.

Depends on Riot, not on this project:

- Any Linux support for Vanguard. If Riot ever announces it, Tuxgate will document the requirements and check them. It cannot create that support.

| Document | Content |
|---|---|
| [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md) | Feasibility study and sources |
| [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md) | Crates, design rules, status model |
| [INSTALLATION.md](INSTALLATION.md) | Build, install, update, remove, configure |
| [SECURITY.md](SECURITY.md) | Threat model, privileged operations, reporting a vulnerability |
| [CONTRIBUTING.md](CONTRIBUTING.md) | Scope, development, how to update the database |
| [docs/TESTING.md](docs/TESTING.md) | Test strategy and hardware checklist |
| [docs/DISTRIBUTION.md](docs/DISTRIBUTION.md) | AUR and signed repository study |
| [CHANGELOG.md](CHANGELOG.md) | Changes |

`RESEARCH.md`:

```md
# Research and feasibility study: Riot Vanguard on Linux

Status of this document: research snapshot, **28 November 2026**.
This project is independent. It is not affiliated with, endorsed by, or sponsored by Riot Games, CachyOS, Epic Games, Valve or Microsoft.

## 1. Verdict in one page

**Riot Vanguard does not run on Linux, and nothing this project can build will change that.**

- Riot has published no Linux build of Vanguard and has announced no plan for one. Its only published position on Linux (April 2024) is a refusal, and no later official statement was found. [S1]
- Vanguard's protection depends on a Windows kernel-mode driver and on Windows platform security features (TPM 2.0, Secure Boot, HVCI/VBS, IOMMU). [S2] [S3] [S4]
- Wine and Proton do not support kernel-space anti-cheat. Valve says so in its own partner documentation. [S7]
- Riot explicitly refuses virtual machines. [S5]
- Any technique that would make the games start anyway (spoofing the environment, hiding a VM, emulating the driver, patching Riot's files) is a circumvention of an anti-cheat. This project will not implement, document or link to such techniques.

What has changed since 2024, and matters for the future:

- On 14 October 2026 Epic announced **Easy Anti-Cheat Linux v2**, an anti-cheat for Linux built on attested boot: Secure Boot, a kernel signed by a key the vendor trusts, TPM 2.0 measured boot, kernel lockdown, signed modules only, IOMMU. [S9] [S10]
- CachyOS shipped a dedicated signed kernel (`linux-cachyos-eac`) and tooling for it in November 2026. [S11]

In 2024 Riot's stated blocker was that Linux did not let it "attest boot state or kernel modules". [S1] A vendor-attested Linux boot chain now exists in production for another anti-cheat. That removes part of the technical argument. It does **not** mean Vanguard will follow: the decision, the server-side verification and the client all belong to Riot.

**What this project can honestly deliver:** system diagnostics, a sourced compatibility database, help with dual boot, local reports, and a maintained record of the facts. That is the scope of Tuxgate.

## 2. Method and reliability scale

Sources were read on 28 November 2026. Each claim below cites a source from the register in section 3.

| Grade | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **A** | Primary source (the vendor's or project's own page), read directly for this study |
| **A-** | Primary source that exists but could not be read directly (login wall or bot protection); content known through search excerpts or secondary coverage |
| **B** | Reputable secondary source (established press, Wikipedia) |
| **C** | Community source, individual project, or commercial blog; treat as a lead, not a fact |

A statement marked *(background)* comes from general engineering knowledge and was not re-verified against a source for this study.

A theoretical possibility is never presented as a working feature. Where something is unknown, this document says "unknown".

## 3. Source register

| S30 | League of Linux — leagueoflinux.org | archived mid-2024, LoR page maintained | C | Community status per game |
| S31 | Maksim-Nikolaev/lor-proton (GitHub) | 2026-10 or later | C | Legends of Runeterra regression report |
| S32 | s-uryansh/vanguard-linux-poc (GitHub) | 2026 | C | Third-party attestation proof of concept |
| S33 | KitGuru / Neowin, League of Legends leaving GeForce NOW | 2024 | B | Cloud gaming and Vanguard |
| S34 | Wikipedia, "Riot Vanguard" | seen 2026-11 | B | Vanguard On-Demand release date |
| S35 | Riot Support, "Vanguard Error Codes (2XKO)" — support-2xko.riotgames.com | undated, seen 2026-11 | A- | 2XKO uses Vanguard (title seen in search results; page not read) |

## 4. What Riot has published about Vanguard

- **A kernel-mode driver plus a client.** Riot explains that cheats increasingly run in the kernel, above user-mode anti-cheat, where they can tamper with the system calls an anti-cheat relies on. Its answer is its own kernel driver. [S2]
- **Purpose of the driver.** Not to collect more data, but to confirm that the game runs "in an environment we can trust". [S1]
- **Loaded at boot.** The driver loads at Windows startup so Riot can attest that nothing was tampered with between boot and game launch. It is also positioned early in boot to block known signed-but-vulnerable drivers. [S1]
- **TPM 2.0.** Required for League of Legends with Vanguard. Riot uses it for certificate validation and as a hardware identifier that is hard to forge, which supports hardware bans. [S1]
- **Current requirement list.** Riot's support page says Vanguard "may require certain Windows security features": TPM 2.0, Secure Boot, Memory Integrity (HVCI / VBS), IOMMU. [S3]
- **On-demand mode (June 2026).** An optional mode in which Vanguard starts with the game instead of with Windows. It is offered only on devices with Windows 11 25H2 or newer, Secure Boot, TPM 2.0, IOMMU, VBS and HVCI. [S4] [S34]
- **Virtual machines.** Refused. The support articles for VAN 9100 and VAN 138 tell the player to use a standard Windows PC. [S5] Riot's reasoning: a cheat could run on the host and inspect the guest where Vanguard cannot see it. [S1]

The trend from 2024 to 2026 is clear: Vanguard leans **more** on hardware-rooted Windows security, not less.

## 5. The nine questions

### Q1. Why does Vanguard not work on Linux?

Three independent reasons, any one of which is sufficient.

1. **There is no Linux Vanguard.** Riot ships a Windows kernel driver and a Windows client. No Linux component exists. [S1] [S3]
2. **Riot has declined to support Linux.** In April 2024 Riot wrote that it has never officially supported Linux, that Linux did not give it enough ability to attest boot state or kernel modules, that distribution differences compound the difficulty, and that with roughly 800 daily Linux players on League the risk was "not worth the payoff". [S1]
3. **Compatibility layers cannot carry it.** Wine and Proton do not run Windows kernel-space anti-cheat drivers. [S7]

### Q2. Which parts of the architecture depend on Windows?

| Part | Windows dependency | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Kernel driver | Written against the Windows kernel driver model; must be signed through Microsoft's Hardware Dev Center to load on Windows 10 1607+ | [S2] [S23] |
| Early-boot positioning | Relies on Windows boot ordering to load before other drivers | [S1] |
| Trust in the environment | Built on Secure Boot, Trusted Boot, Measured Boot and the TPM as Windows exposes them | [S24] |
| Memory integrity | HVCI moves kernel code-integrity decisions into the Windows hypervisor | [S21] [S22] |
| DMA protection | IOMMU as enforced by Windows VBS / Kernel DMA Protection | [S3] [S21] |
| Hardware identity | TPM 2.0 endorsement used as a ban anchor | [S1] |
| Client and game | Windows user-mode binaries | *(background)* |

The server side matters as much as the client: Riot's servers decide which environments are acceptable. Even a perfect client-side implementation by a third party would be an environment Riot's servers have no reason to trust.

### Q3. Which parts could legitimately be studied or replaced?

- **Studied:** everything Riot, Microsoft, Epic, Valve and the Linux kernel have published. That is what this document does.
- **Replaced:** nothing in Vanguard. Replacing or emulating any Vanguard component means presenting Riot's servers with something that claims to be Vanguard and is not. That is circumvention, whatever the intent.
- **Built independently:** tooling that measures the local machine and reports the result to its owner. It can tell a user whether their Linux system has the security properties an attestation-based anti-cheat would ask for. It cannot make any anti-cheat accept the machine.

### Q4. Which Linux interfaces could play an equivalent role?

These are the Linux counterparts of what Vanguard uses on Windows. This table describes equivalence of **function**, not a working substitute.

| Need | Windows | Linux counterpart | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Verified boot chain | Secure Boot + Trusted Boot | UEFI Secure Boot with a signed boot manager and signed kernel (`sbctl`, shim, signed UKIs) | [S12] [S24] |
| Boot measurement | Measured Boot into TPM PCRs | Boot manager and kernel measurements into TPM PCRs; `/sys/class/tpm/tpm0/pcr-sha256/N` | [S19] [S13] |
| Remote attestation | TPM-signed boot log sent to a server | TPM2 quote over PCRs plus event log; IMA measurement list in PCR 10 | [S20] [S24] |
| Kernel code integrity | HVCI | Kernel lockdown (integrity mode) plus enforced module signatures | [S16] [S17] |
| Only trusted kernel code | Microsoft-signed drivers only | `CONFIG_MODULE_SIG_FORCE` or `module.sig_enforce=1`; keys in `.builtin_trusted_keys` | [S16] |
| Runtime observation | Kernel driver callbacks | eBPF and BPF LSM, without a custom kernel module | [S9] [S10] |
| DMA protection | Kernel DMA Protection | IOMMU (`intel_iommu=on`, `iommu.passthrough=0`) | [S11] |
| Hardware identity | TPM endorsement key | TPM endorsement key (same hardware) | [S19] |

Two caveats on CachyOS specifically: systemd-boot and GRUB measure into TPM PCRs, while rEFInd and Limine do not pass the PCR0 reconstruction test according to the CachyOS wiki. [S13]

**The structural difference** is who holds the root of trust. On Windows, Microsoft signs the kernel and every driver. On Linux the machine owner can build and sign their own kernel. A lockdown flag that the owner can compile out proves nothing to a remote party. An anti-cheat therefore has to trust a *specific kernel binary signed by a specific party*, verified through the TPM, not merely "Secure Boot is on".

### Q5. Why are Wine and Proton not enough?

- Wine reimplements the Windows user-mode API. It does not provide a Windows kernel into which `vgk`-style drivers can load with real authority. *(background)*
- Valve's own documentation: Proton supports some anti-cheat middleware in user space (Easy Anti-Cheat, BattlEye), each requiring the game developer to opt in, and "Kernel-space solutions are not currently supported and are not recommended." [S7]
- The EAC and BattlEye support that exists under Proton is a cooperative arrangement: the anti-cheat vendor ships a Linux-side module and each game developer enables it. [S7] [S8] Riot is both vendor and developer for Vanguard and has done neither.
- Even if Wine could load the driver, the driver would be inspecting an emulated Windows, not the real kernel beneath it. That is exactly the blind spot Riot refuses in virtual machines. [S1]

### Q6. Why is virtualisation a problem?

- **Riot refuses it by policy.** VAN 9100 and VAN 138 are the errors a virtualised environment produces. [S5]
- **The reasoning is sound from Riot's side.** Code on the host can read and modify guest memory invisibly to anything inside the guest. [S1]
- **Getting past the check means hiding the hypervisor**, which is deceiving the anti-cheat. This project will never do that, and accounts used that way are exposed to bans.
- **Cloud gaming was affected too.** League of Legends was withdrawn from GeForce NOW in 2024 because Vanguard does not support virtual machines. [S33] Whether any Riot title is available on a cloud service today was not established by this study; check the service's own list.

### Q7. What would have to change on Riot's side?

All of the following, and only Riot can do any of them:

1. Decide to support Linux at all.
2. Ship a Linux client-side component (a native module, or a Proton-compatible one in the manner of EAC and BattlEye).
3. Define an attestation policy for Linux: which distributions' signing keys, which kernel configuration, which boot managers.
4. Accept those attestations on its servers.
5. Work with distributions so that signed kernels and boot chains are available, as Epic is doing. [S9] [S10]

### Q8. What can be built independently of Riot?

- A **diagnostic engine**: distribution, kernel, GPU and drivers, Vulkan, Wine, Proton, Secure Boot, TPM, IOMMU, lockdown, module signature enforcement, virtualisation, boot manager.
- A **readiness view** of the security properties that attestation-based anti-cheat requires on Linux, clearly labelled as local detection and not as any vendor's validation.
- A **compatibility database** of Riot titles with a status, a reason, a source and a date for each.
- A **dual-boot helper**: detect an existing Windows installation and, after explicit confirmation, reboot into it once.
- **Local, anonymisable reports** a user can share when asking for help.
- A **maintained factual record**, so the community stops rediscovering the same dead ends.

### Q9. What is the most realistic path forward?

1. **Today:** dual boot is the only way to play Vanguard-protected titles on a machine that otherwise runs Linux. Make that path safe and painless.
2. **Now and continuing:** make the facts easy to find and keep them current. Show users what their system looks like against the attested-boot model.
3. **If Riot moves:** the EAC Linux v2 model is the most plausible shape for Linux support, since it answers Riot's own 2024 objection. A user whose system already satisfies that model would be best placed. This project can track readiness; it cannot create support.
4. **Advocacy:** demand is a legitimate signal. Riot cited player numbers in its refusal. [S1] Petitions and feedback through official channels are the appropriate route.

## 6. The EAC Linux v2 precedent

What is established:

- Epic announced a rebuilt Linux anti-cheat on 14 October 2026, to be piloted with Fortnite. It drops the kernel module and relies on existing Linux security features together with eBPF. [S9] [S10]
- Requirements, as implemented on CachyOS: Secure Boot with a kernel signed by a key Epic trusts; TPM 2.0 measured boot (fTPM and PTT accepted); kernel lockdown in integrity mode; only signed modules; IOMMU on; kexec disabled; BPF LSM active. [S11]
- CachyOS kernel command line for this mode:
  `lockdown=integrity lsm=landlock,lockdown,yama,integrity,bpf iommu.passthrough=0 intel_iommu=on module.sig_enforce=1 sysctl.kernel.kexec_load_disabled=1` [S11]
- The kernel must be the distribution's signed build. CachyOS signs `linux-cachyos-eac` and its systemd-boot with the "CachyOS EAC CA"; re-signing them with the user's own key makes EAC stop recognising them. [S11] [S12]
- **Out-of-tree and DKMS modules do not load** in that boot entry. NVIDIA, ZFS and r8125 are shipped as prebuilt signed packages. [S11]
- Steam Deck is not supported yet. [S10] [S11]
- The design has drawn criticism from open-source advocates for restricting which kernels a player may run. [S10]

What this means for Riot titles:

- It shows that vendor-verified boot state on Linux is achievable in practice, and that a distribution can deliver it.
- It costs the user real freedom on that boot entry: no self-built kernel, no arbitrary modules.
- It is **not evidence that Riot will adopt the same approach**. Riot has said nothing on the subject since Epic's announcement that this study could find.

## 7. Related third-party work

- **vanguard-linux-poc** [S32]: an individual's proof of concept of an attestation path (eBPF monitors, TPM2 quote, Secure Boot check, a mock verifier), described by its author as proposed to Riot. Its README states that Valorant does not run on Linux and does not claim otherwise. It shows no licence in the content read, has a handful of commits, and is unrelated to Riot's servers. It is noted here as a design reference only; this project does not depend on it or reuse its code.
- **League of Linux** [S30]: the long-running community hub, largely archived since Vanguard reached League in 2024.

## 8. Riot titles: documented status

Statuses below are **documented from sources**, not tested by this project.

| Title | Anti-cheat | Status on Linux | Basis | Grade |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VALORANT | Vanguard | Blocked | Vanguard required [S3]; listed "Denied" [S28] | A / C |
| League of Legends | Vanguard (since 2024) | Blocked | Riot's own statement [S1] | A |
| Teamfight Tactics (PC) | Vanguard, through the League client | Blocked on desktop | Shares the League client *(background)*; community hub marks it as limited [S30] | C |
| 2XKO | Vanguard | Blocked | Riot publishes Vanguard error codes for 2XKO [S35]; listed "Broken" [S29] | B |
| Legends of Runeterra | No Vanguard requirement found | Reported to run through Wine/Proton; a regression is reported since Riot Client v142 (October 2026) | Community reports only [S30] [S31] | C |
| Wild Rift | n/a | Not applicable (mobile title) | — | — |

Open points: whether Legends of Runeterra uses any anti-cheat was not confirmed from a primary source; the reported regression was not reproduced.

## 9. Naming and trademarks

Riot's "Legal Jibber Jabber" policy (last updated August 2018) states that without a written licence a project may not use Riot's logos or trademarks, may not register domain names or social accounts using Riot's names, and that Riot may require any project to stop at any time. It asks community projects to carry a notice that Riot does not endorse or sponsor them. [S6]

The policy is written for fan content that reuses Riot's game assets, and does not address a diagnostic tool that uses none. Referring to Riot's products by name in order to describe compatibility is ordinary descriptive use. Using "Riot" as the leading word of the project's own name is a different matter and is the form most likely to cause confusion with official software. This is not legal advice.

**Decision (28 November 2026):** the project was first drafted as "Riot Linux Companion" and renamed **Tuxgate** before any publication. Riot's names appear only descriptively, to say which games the tool is about.

Whatever the name, the project: uses no Riot logo or artwork, redistributes no Riot file, and states its independence prominently.

## 10. Classification

**Feasible today**

- Hardware and software diagnostics on Linux, without privileges.
- Reporting Secure Boot, TPM, IOMMU, lockdown and module-signing state as *local detections*.
- A sourced compatibility database and local test history.
- Detecting a Windows installation and rebooting into it once, with confirmation.
- Packaging for Arch and CachyOS.

**Can be explored without circumventing anything**

- A "readiness" view against the attested-boot model that EAC Linux v2 uses.
- Reading TPM PCR values and the boot event log for the owner's own information.
- Tracking official statements from Riot and other vendors.

**Requires Riot**

- Any Linux build of Vanguard or of a Vanguard-protected game.
- Any server-side acceptance of a Linux environment.
- Opt-in under Proton, in the way EAC and BattlEye games do it.

**Impossible or not demonstrated**

- Running the Vanguard Windows kernel driver on Linux, in Wine or in Proton.
- Playing Vanguard-protected titles in a virtual machine within Riot's rules.
- A third-party "compatible" Vanguard implementation: it could not be validated and would be a circumvention.

## 11. Limits of this study

- Epic's announcement [S9] and the Arch Wiki pages [S27] could not be read directly; they are graded A-.
- No Riot statement about Linux later than April 2024 was found. Absence of a statement is not a statement.
- Game statuses in section 8 were not tested. A status can change with any client update.
- Nothing here was verified on real CachyOS hardware by the author of this document.

## 12. Re-check list

Review these when updating this document: the Riot support pages [S3] [S4] [S5]; Riot's dev blog for any Linux statement; Epic's EAC Linux v2 rollout and the list of supported distributions; the CachyOS EAC guide [S11]; Valve's Proton anti-cheat documentation [S7].

```

`SECURITY.md`:

```md
# Security

Tuxgate runs on people's main machines and touches boot configuration. This document states what it does, what it refuses to do, and how to report a problem.

## Commitments

These are enforced by the code and by tests, not only by intent.

| Commitment | How it is enforced |
|---|---|
| No network access | No networking crate is in the dependency tree. `deny.toml` bans the crates that could add one; CI fails if one appears. |
| No privileges needed | The binary is not setuid; the package installs no service, no polkit rule and no install script. A test rejects those strings in the PKGBUILD. |
| No circumvention | No code hides a virtual machine, fakes a TPM, Secure Boot or IOMMU state, loads a Windows driver, or reads, patches or redistributes a Riot file. A test rejects any subcommand named after installing, enabling, bypassing, spoofing or patching. |
| No silent system change | The only state-changing path is `tuxgate windows reboot`, which requires a phrase typed at a terminal. There is no flag to skip it. |
| No permanent boot change | Both reboot methods affect the next boot only. Nothing writes to the EFI System Partition or the boot order. |
| No credentials | Tuxgate never reads Riot Client files, tokens or account data. |
| No `unsafe` | `unsafe_code = "forbid"` across the workspace. |

## Privileged operations

There is exactly one situation in which administrator rights are requested.

**When:** `tuxgate windows reboot`, on a system where Windows has a firmware boot entry and the running boot manager is not systemd-boot.

**What runs:** `/usr/bin/pkexec /usr/bin/efibootmgr --bootnext XXXX` (or `sudo` in place of `pkexec` when `pkexec` is absent), then `/usr/bin/systemctl reboot`. Programs are run by absolute path from system directories; your `PATH` is not consulted.

**What it changes:** the UEFI `BootNext` variable, which the firmware uses once and clears.

**Safeguards:**

1. The exact commands are printed before anything runs. `--dry-run` stops there.
2. Confirmation requires typing `reboot to windows` at an interactive terminal. Input that is piped or redirected is refused.
3. If the elevation is refused or fails, the restart is not attempted.
4. If the restart fails after `BootNext` was set, the error says so and gives the command to undo it (`sudo efibootmgr --delete-bootnext`).
5. Tuxgate never handles your password. `pkexec` or `sudo` does.

With systemd-boot, the single command is `systemctl reboot --boot-loader-entry=<id>`. Tuxgate asks for no elevation; logind applies your system's own authorisation policy.

## Data handling

**Read:** files under `/etc`, `/proc`, `/sys` and `/boot` that are readable by your user; the output of the query commands listed in `tuxgate doctor --verbose`.

**Never read:** host name, serial numbers, MAC addresses, disk or partition identifiers, the full kernel command line as a finding, browser or game account data.

**Written:**

| File | When |
|---|---|
| `~/.local/share/tuxgate/history.toml` | Only when you run `tuxgate games record` |
| The path given to `tuxgate report --output` | Only then; created with mode 0600; never overwritten without `--force` |

**Transmitted:** nothing, ever. Sharing a report is something you do yourself, after reading it.

### Anonymisation

On by default for `tuxgate report`. It replaces your home directory with `~`, any other `/home/<name>` with `/home/<user>`, UUIDs with `<uuid>` (well-known UEFI vendor GUIDs excepted) and MAC addresses with `<mac>`.

Limits you should know about:

- A report still describes your hardware: CPU model, GPU model, kernel version, firmware vendor. That can narrow down a machine.
- UEFI boot entry descriptions are kept as the firmware stores them. If you named an entry after yourself, it appears.
- `tuxgate doctor` output is **not** anonymised; it is meant for your terminal. Use `tuxgate report` for anything you intend to share.

Read a report before posting it.

## Threat model

| Threat | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| A malicious or compromised dependency | 42 pinned crates, all from crates.io, checksummed by `Cargo.lock`; `cargo deny` checks advisories, licences, sources and the ban list in CI |
| A tampered package recipe | The PKGBUILD is short, downloads nothing but pinned crates, and is covered by tests |
| Hostile data in system files or firmware variables | Parsers are bounds-checked and tested against truncated and malformed input; `unsafe` is forbidden; captured command output is capped at 4 MiB per stream |
| Terminal escape sequences or forged lines smuggled through a file, a directory name, a firmware boot entry name or a history note | Every string taken from the system passes through a filter that replaces control characters and bidirectional overrides before it is stored, printed or written to a report |
| A hostile program earlier in `PATH` named like a tool Tuxgate calls | The commands of `windows reboot` (`pkexec`, `sudo`, `efibootmgr`, `systemctl`) are resolved in `/usr/bin`, `/usr/sbin`, `/bin` and `/sbin` only and run by absolute path; `PATH` is ignored for them, and a test covers it. Diagnostic commands do use `PATH`, but run with your own privileges and change nothing, so substituting one gains an attacker nothing they did not already have. |
| Being talked into a reboot | A typed phrase at a terminal; no bypass flag; no way to trigger it from a pipe |
| Misleading output taken as proof of anti-cheat compatibility | Every security finding carries a disclaimer; statuses distinguish detection from validation; tests assert no title is ever presented as verified working |

Out of scope: an attacker who already has your user account or root.

## What Tuxgate will never accept

Contributions or requests for any of the following will be declined, whatever the stated purpose:

- hiding virtualisation or emulation from an anti-cheat;
- faking TPM, Secure Boot, IOMMU or any attestation;
- loading or emulating Vanguard or any Windows kernel driver;
- patching, unpacking or redistributing Riot's files;
- anything presented as a "workaround" for an anti-cheat refusal.

## Reporting a vulnerability

Please report privately through the repository's **Security → Report a vulnerability** page (GitHub private vulnerability reporting), rather than in a public issue.

Include the version (`tuxgate --version`), the command, and what you observed. You should get a first answer within a week. There is no bounty.

Bugs that are not security issues go to the public issue tracker.

## Supported versions

Only the latest release receives fixes. The project is at version 0.x: interfaces and file formats may still change.

```

`crates/tuxgate-bridge/src/efi_boot.rs`:

```rs
//! Read-only decoding of UEFI boot entries (`Boot####`, `BootOrder`,
//! `BootCurrent`, `BootNext`) from efivarfs.

use serde::Serialize;
use tuxgate_core::efi::{self, EFIVARS_DIR, EFI_GLOBAL_GUID};
use tuxgate_core::text::sanitize;
use tuxgate_core::{FsError, SystemContext};

/// Path of the Windows boot manager on an EFI System Partition.
pub const WINDOWS_LOADER: &str = "\\EFI\\Microsoft\\Boot\\bootmgfw.efi";

/// One firmware boot entry.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct EfiBootEntry {
    /// The `####` of `Boot####`.
    pub number: u16,
    pub description: String,
    /// File the entry starts, when the entry carries a file path.
    pub file_path: Option<String>,
    /// False when the entry is disabled in the firmware.
    pub active: bool,
}

impl EfiBootEntry {
    /// The entry number as firmware tools print it, for example `0003`.
    pub fn label(&self) -> String {
        format!("{:04X}", self.number)
    }

    /// True when the entry starts the Windows boot manager.
    pub fn is_windows(&self) -> bool {
        let starts_windows_loader = self
            .file_path
            .as_deref()
            .is_some_and(|path| path.eq_ignore_ascii_case(WINDOWS_LOADER));
        starts_windows_loader
            || self
                .description
                .to_lowercase()
                .contains("windows boot manager")
    }
}

/// Decodes an `EFI_LOAD_OPTION` (the variable data, without the efivarfs
/// attribute prefix). Returns `None` when the data is malformed.
pub fn parse_load_option(number: u16, data: &[u8]) -> Option<EfiBootEntry> {
    let attributes = u32::from_le_bytes(data.get(0..4)?.try_into().ok()?);
    let path_length = usize::from(u16::from_le_bytes(data.get(4..6)?.try_into().ok()?));

    // The description is a NUL-terminated UTF-16 string.
    let rest = data.get(6..)?;
    let description_units = rest.chunks_exact(2).position(|pair| pair == [0, 0])?;
    let description_end = description_units * 2;
    // The description is whatever the firmware stores; neutralise it.
    let description = sanitize(&efi::utf16le_to_string(&rest[..description_end]));

    let path_start = description_end + 2;
    let device_path = rest.get(path_start..path_start.checked_add(path_length)?)?;

    Some(EfiBootEntry {
        number,
        description,
        file_path: file_path_of(device_path),
        active: attributes & 1 == 1,
    })
}

/// Walks a device path and returns its file path node, if any.
fn file_path_of(mut device_path: &[u8]) -> Option<String> {
    const MEDIA_DEVICE_PATH: u8 = 0x04;
    const MEDIA_FILE_PATH: u8 = 0x04;
    const END_OF_PATH: u8 = 0x7f;

    while device_path.len() >= 4 {
        let node_type = device_path[0];
        let subtype = device_path[1];
        let length = usize::from(u16::from_le_bytes([device_path[2], device_path[3]]));
        if length < 4 || length > device_path.len() {
            return None;
        }
        if node_type == END_OF_PATH {
            return None;
        }
        if node_type == MEDIA_DEVICE_PATH && subtype == MEDIA_FILE_PATH {
            let path = sanitize(&efi::utf16le_to_string(&device_path[4..length]));
            return (!path.is_empty()).then_some(path);
        }
        device_path = &device_path[length..];
    }
    None
}

/// Extracts the entry number from an efivarfs file name such as
/// `Boot0003-8be4df61-93ca-11d2-aa0d-00e098032b8c`.
fn boot_number(file_name: &str) -> Option<u16> {
    let suffix = format!("-{EFI_GLOBAL_GUID}");
    let name = file_name.strip_suffix(suffix.as_str())?;
    let digits = name.strip_prefix("Boot")?;
    (digits.len() == 4 && digits.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit()))
        .then(|| u16::from_str_radix(digits, 16).ok())
        .flatten()
}

/// Lists the firmware boot entries, ordered by number. Entries that cannot
/// be read or decoded are skipped.
pub fn list_boot_entries(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Result<Vec<EfiBootEntry>, FsError> {
    let mut entries: Vec<EfiBootEntry> = ctx
        .list_dir(EFIVARS_DIR)?
        .iter()
        .filter_map(|name| boot_number(name))
        .filter_map(|number| {
            let data = efi::read_var(ctx, &format!("Boot{number:04X}"), EFI_GLOBAL_GUID).ok()?;
            parse_load_option(number, &data)
        })
        .collect();
    entries.sort_by_key(|e| e.number);
    Ok(entries)
}

fn read_u16_list(ctx: &SystemContext, name: &str) -> Option<Vec<u16>> {
    let data = efi::read_var(ctx, name, EFI_GLOBAL_GUID).ok()?;
    Some(
        data.chunks_exact(2)
            .map(|pair| u16::from_le_bytes([pair[0], pair[1]]))
            .collect(),
    )
}

/// The firmware boot order.
pub fn boot_order(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Option<Vec<u16>> {
    read_u16_list(ctx, "BootOrder")
}

/// The entry the firmware used for the current boot.
pub fn boot_current(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Option<u16> {
    read_u16_list(ctx, "BootCurrent")?.first().copied()
}

/// The entry already selected for the next boot, if any.
pub fn boot_next(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Option<u16> {
    read_u16_list(ctx, "BootNext")?.first().copied()
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::{efi_load_option, efi_var_u16_list, scenarios, Fixture};

    fn option_data(description: &str, path: Option<&str>) -> Vec<u8> {
        efi_load_option(description, path)[4..].to_vec()
    }

    #[test]
    fn decodes_a_windows_entry() {
        let data = option_data("Windows Boot Manager", Some(WINDOWS_LOADER));
        let entry = parse_load_option(0, &data).expect("entry");
        assert_eq!(entry.description, "Windows Boot Manager");
        assert_eq!(entry.file_path.as_deref(), Some(WINDOWS_LOADER));
        assert!(entry.active);
        assert!(entry.is_windows());
        assert_eq!(entry.label(), "0000");
    }

    #[test]
    fn recognises_windows_by_loader_path_even_when_renamed() {
        let data = option_data("My other OS", Some("\\efi\\microsoft\\boot\\BOOTMGFW.EFI"));
        assert!(parse_load_option(7, &data).expect("entry").is_windows());

        let linux = option_data(
            "Linux Boot Manager",
            Some("\\EFI\\systemd\\systemd-bootx64.efi"),
        );
        assert!(!parse_load_option(1, &linux).expect("entry").is_windows());
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_entry_without_file_path_is_decoded() {
        let data = option_data("UEFI: PXE IPv4", None);
        let entry = parse_load_option(0x10, &data).expect("entry");
        assert_eq!(entry.file_path, None);
        assert_eq!(entry.label(), "0010");
    }

    #[test]
    fn hostile_descriptions_are_neutralised() {
        let data = option_data("Windows\x1b[2J Boot Manager", Some("\\EFI\\x\x1b[31m.efi"));
        let entry = parse_load_option(0, &data).expect("entry");
        assert!(!entry.description.chars().any(char::is_control));
        assert!(!entry
            .file_path
            .as_deref()
            .unwrap_or_default()
            .chars()
            .any(char::is_control));
    }

    #[test]
    fn malformed_data_is_rejected_without_panicking() {
        assert_eq!(parse_load_option(0, &[]), None);
        assert_eq!(parse_load_option(0, &[1, 0, 0, 0, 4]), None);
        // No NUL terminator on the description.
        assert_eq!(
            parse_load_option(0, &[1, 0, 0, 0, 4, 0, 0x41, 0x00, 0x42, 0x00]),
            None
        );
        // Declared path length runs past the end of the data.
        let mut truncated = option_data("X", Some("\\a"));
        truncated.truncate(truncated.len() - 3);
        assert_eq!(parse_load_option(0, &truncated), None);
        // A device path node claiming a zero length must not loop forever.
        assert_eq!(file_path_of(&[0x04, 0x04, 0x00, 0x00, 0xff, 0xff]), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn extracts_boot_numbers_from_file_names() {
        let guid = EFI_GLOBAL_GUID;
        assert_eq!(boot_number(&format!("Boot0003-{guid}")), Some(3));
        assert_eq!(boot_number(&format!("Boot00AF-{guid}")), Some(0xAF));
        assert_eq!(boot_number(&format!("BootOrder-{guid}")), None);
        assert_eq!(boot_number(&format!("BootCurrent-{guid}")), None);
        assert_eq!(
            boot_number("Boot0003-00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000"),
            None
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn lists_entries_and_order_from_a_scenario() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let ctx = scenario.context();
        let entries = list_boot_entries(&ctx).expect("entries");
        assert_eq!(entries.len(), 2);
        assert!(entries[0].is_windows());
        assert_eq!(entries[1].description, "Linux Boot Manager");
        assert_eq!(boot_order(&ctx), Some(vec![1, 0]));
        assert_eq!(boot_current(&ctx), Some(1));
        assert_eq!(boot_next(&ctx), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn reports_a_pending_boot_next() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new().bytes(
            &efi::var_path("BootNext", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID),
            &efi_var_u16_list(&[4]),
        );
        assert_eq!(boot_next(&fixture.context()), Some(4));
    }

    #[test]
    fn missing_efivars_is_an_error_not_an_empty_list() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        assert_eq!(
            list_boot_entries(&fixture.context()),
            Err(FsError::NotFound)
        );
    }
}
