`crates/tuxgate-cli/src/main.rs`:

```rs
//! `tuxgate`: an unofficial companion for Riot Games titles on Linux.
//!
//! This binary only parses arguments and prints. The logic lives in the
//! library crates.

mod app;
mod cli;
mod commands;

use std::process::ExitCode;

use clap::Parser;

use crate::app::{App, CliResult};
use crate::cli::{Cli, Command, GamesAction, WindowsAction};

fn run(cli: Cli) -> CliResult {
    // These two print static text and need no system access.
    match &cli.command {
        Command::Completions { shell } => return commands::completions(*shell),
        Command::Man => return commands::man(),
        _ => {}
    }

    let app = App::new(&cli)?;
    match cli.command {
        Command::Doctor { verbose } => commands::doctor(&app, verbose),
        Command::Status => commands::status(&app),
        Command::Games { action } => match action.unwrap_or(GamesAction::List) {
            GamesAction::List => commands::games_list(&app),
            GamesAction::History { game } => commands::games_history(&app, game.as_deref()),
            GamesAction::Record {
                game,
                result,
                runner,
                note,
            } => commands::games_record(&app, &game, result, runner, note),
        },
        Command::Check { game } => commands::check(&app, &game),
        Command::Report {
            format,
            output,
            no_anonymize,
            anonymize,
            force,
        } => commands::report(
            &app,
            format,
            output.as_deref(),
            no_anonymize,
            anonymize,
            force,
        ),
        Command::Windows { action } => match action {
            WindowsAction::Detect => commands::windows_detect(&app),
            WindowsAction::Reboot { dry_run } => commands::windows_reboot(&app, dry_run),
        },
        Command::Sources => commands::sources(&app),
        Command::Probes => commands::probes(&app),
        Command::Completions { .. } | Command::Man => unreachable!("handled above"),
    }
}

fn main() -> ExitCode {
    // Usage errors exit with status 2 through clap.
    let cli = Cli::parse();
    match run(cli) {
        Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,
        Err(error) => {
            eprintln!("tuxgate: error: {error}");
            ExitCode::FAILURE
        }
    }
}

```

`crates/tuxgate-cli/tests/cli.rs`:

```rs
//! End-to-end tests of the `tuxgate` binary.
//!
//! Each test runs the real executable against a simulated machine through
//! `--root`, with the user directories redirected to a temporary location.
//! No test runs `windows reboot` against the host.

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::process::{Command, Output};
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};

use tuxgate_core::testing::{scenarios, Scenario};

static COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);

/// A private HOME for one test, removed on drop.
struct Sandbox {
    dir: PathBuf,
}

impl Sandbox {
    fn new() -> Self {
        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
            "tuxgate-cli-test-{}-{}",
            std::process::id(),
            COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed)
        ));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect("sandbox");
        Self { dir }
    }

    fn command(&self) -> Command {
        let mut command = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_tuxgate"));
        command
            .env("HOME", &self.dir)
            .env("USERPROFILE", &self.dir)
            .env("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", self.dir.join("config"))
            .env("XDG_DATA_HOME", self.dir.join("data"))
            .env_remove("NO_COLOR")
            .stdin(std::process::Stdio::null());
        command
    }

    fn run(&self, args: &[&str]) -> Output {
        self.command().args(args).output().expect("run tuxgate")
    }

    fn run_on(&self, scenario: &Scenario, args: &[&str]) -> Output {
        self.command()
            .arg("--root")
            .arg(scenario.fixture.root())
            .args(args)
            .output()
            .expect("run tuxgate")
    }

    fn path(&self, name: &str) -> PathBuf {
        self.dir.join(name)
    }
}

impl Drop for Sandbox {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.dir);
    }
}

fn stdout(output: &Output) -> String {
    String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).into_owned()
}

fn stderr(output: &Output) -> String {
    String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).into_owned()
}

fn json(output: &Output) -> serde_json::Value {
    serde_json::from_slice(&output.stdout).unwrap_or_else(|e| {
        panic!("invalid JSON ({e}):\n{}", stdout(output));
    })
}

fn assert_success(output: &Output) {
    assert!(
        output.status.success(),
        "exit {:?}\nstdout:\n{}\nstderr:\n{}",
        output.status.code(),
        stdout(output),
        stderr(output)
    );
}

fn assert_failure(output: &Output, code: i32) {
    assert_eq!(
        output.status.code(),
        Some(code),
        "stdout:\n{}\nstderr:\n{}",
        stdout(output),
        stderr(output)
    );
}

#[test]
fn help_and_version() {
    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();

    let help = sandbox.run(&["--help"]);
    assert_success(&help);
    let text = stdout(&help);
    assert!(text.contains("does NOT install, emulate, bypass"));
    assert!(text.contains("not affiliated with Riot Games"));
    for command in ["doctor", "status", "games", "check", "report", "windows"] {
        assert!(text.contains(command), "help lacks {command}");
    }

    let version = sandbox.run(&["--version"]);
    assert_success(&version);
    assert!(stdout(&version).starts_with("tuxgate "));
}

#[test]
fn unknown_commands_are_usage_errors() {
    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();
    assert_failure(&sandbox.run(&["vanguard", "install"]), 2);
    assert_failure(&sandbox.run(&["install"]), 2);
    assert_failure(&sandbox.run(&[]), 2);
}

#[test]
fn doctor_reports_a_cachyos_desktop() {
    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();
    let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
    let output = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["doctor"]);
    assert_success(&output);
    let text = stdout(&output);
    for expected in [
        "Distribution: CachyOS (rolling)",
        "Kernel: 6.17.9-2-cachyos",
        "CachyOS kernel: linux-cachyos",
        "Graphics adapter: NVIDIA [10de:2684], driver: nvidia",
        "Secure Boot: enabled",
        "TPM: TPM 2.0",
        "Boot manager: systemd-boot 258.2-1-arch",
        "Offline tree inspected: no command was run.",
        "Local detection only. This is not an attestation",
        "Windows installation found",
        "VALORANT: blocked by an external requirement",
        "not affiliated with",
    ] {
        assert!(text.contains(expected), "missing {expected:?} in:\n{text}");
    }
    // Output is piped, so it must not be coloured.
    assert!(!text.contains('\x1b'));
    // Evidence only appears with --verbose.
    assert!(!text.contains("read: /etc/os-release"));

    let verbose = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["doctor", "--verbose"]);
    assert_success(&verbose);
    assert!(stdout(&verbose).contains("read: /etc/os-release"));
}

#[test]
fn doctor_json_is_machine_readable() {
    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();
    let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_attested();
    let output = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["doctor", "--json"]);
    assert_success(&output);
    let value = json(&output);
    assert_eq!(value["schema_version"], 1);
    assert_eq!(value["offline"], true);
    assert_eq!(value["anonymized"], false);
    assert_eq!(value["readiness"]["observed"], 8);
    let lockdown = value["findings"]
        .as_array()
        .and_then(|f| f.iter().find(|f| f["id"] == "security.lockdown"))
        .expect("lockdown finding");
    assert_eq!(lockdown["status"], "detected");
    assert_eq!(lockdown["value"], "integrity");
}

#[test]
fn check_valorant_explains_the_block_and_the_alternative() {
    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();
    let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
    let output = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["check", "valorant"]);
    assert_success(&output);
    let text = stdout(&output);
    for expected in [
        "VALORANT",
        "Status:      blocked by an external requirement",
        "Anti-cheat:  Riot Vanguard",
        "Legitimate ways to play today",
        "Dual boot",
        "[S3] grade A, 2026-05-31",
        "https://support.riotgames.com/",
        "cannot run on this system",
        "set by Riot Games",
        "Windows installation found",
        "tuxgate windows reboot",
        "it has no effect on Vanguard",
    ] {
        assert!(text.contains(expected), "missing {expected:?} in:\n{text}");
    }

    // Aliases resolve to the same title.
    let alias = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["check", "val", "--json"]);
    assert_success(&alias);
    let value = json(&alias);
    assert_eq!(value["game"]["id"], "valorant");
    assert_eq!(value["system_status"], "blocked_external");
    assert!(value["sources"].as_array().is_some_and(|s| s.len() >= 3));
}

#[test]
fn report_previews_by_default_and_writes_only_on_request() {
    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();
    let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();

    let preview = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["report", "--format", "markdown"]);
    assert_success(&preview);
    assert!(stdout(&preview).starts_with("# Tuxgate "));
    assert!(stdout(&preview).contains("- Anonymised: yes"));

    let target = sandbox.path("report.md");
    let target_text = target.to_string_lossy().into_owned();
    let written = sandbox.run_on(
        &scenario,
        &["report", "--format", "markdown", "--output", &target_text],
    );
    assert_success(&written);
    assert!(stdout(&written).is_empty(), "the report goes to the file");
    assert!(stderr(&written).contains("Report written to"));
    assert!(stderr(&written).contains("has not sent it anywhere"));
    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(&target).expect("report file");
    assert!(content.contains("## Security features"));

    // A second run must not overwrite silently.
    let again = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["report", "--output", &target_text]);
    assert_failure(&again, 1);
    assert!(stderr(&again).contains("already exists"));
    assert_eq!(
        std::fs::read_to_string(&target).expect("unchanged"),
        content
    );

    let forced = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["report", "--output", &target_text, "--force"]);
    assert_success(&forced);
    assert!(std::fs::read_to_string(&target)
        .expect("overwritten")
        .starts_with("Tuxgate "));
}

    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();
    let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
    let raw = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["report", "--no-anonymize", "--json"]);
    assert_success(&raw);
    assert_eq!(json(&raw)["anonymized"], false);

    assert_eq!(json(&default)["anonymized"], true);

    assert_success(&output);

    assert!(text.contains("Firmware entry Boot0000: Windows Boot Manager"));

#[test]
fn records_tests_and_reports_regressions() {
    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();
    let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();

    let empty = sandbox.run(&["games", "history"]);
    assert_success(&empty);
    assert!(stdout(&empty).contains("No test has been recorded yet."));

    let first = sandbox.run_on(
        &scenario,
        &[
            "games",
            "record",
            "lor",
            "--result",
            "works",
            "--runner",
            "Proton 10.0",
        ],
    );
    assert_success(&first);
    assert!(stdout(&first).contains("Legends of Runeterra is \"works\""));

    let second = sandbox.run_on(
        &scenario,
        &[
            "games",
            "record",
            "lor",
            "--result",
            "broken",
            "--note",
            "update loop",
        ],
    );
    assert_success(&second);
    assert!(stdout(&second).contains("Regression: legends-of-runeterra: worked on"));

    let history = sandbox.run(&["games", "history", "lor"]);
    assert_success(&history);
    let text = stdout(&history);
    assert!(text.contains("[Proton 10.0]"));
    assert!(text.contains("kernel 6.17.9-2-cachyos"));
    assert!(text.contains("update loop"));
    assert!(text.contains("Regressions"));

    let file = sandbox.path("data").join("tuxgate").join("history.toml");
    assert!(file.is_file(), "history is stored under XDG_DATA_HOME");

    let as_json = json(&sandbox.run(&["games", "history", "--json"]));
    assert_eq!(as_json["entries"].as_array().map(Vec::len), Some(2));
    assert_eq!(as_json["regressions"].as_array().map(Vec::len), Some(1));
}

    assert_success(&output);

}

#[test]
fn configuration_is_honoured_and_validated() {
    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();
    let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();

    let config = sandbox.path("custom.toml");
    std::fs::write(&config, "[probes]\ndisabled = [\"tools\", \"wine\"]\n").expect("config");
    let config_text = config.to_string_lossy().into_owned();

    let output = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["--config", &config_text, "probes"]);
    assert_success(&output);
    let text = stdout(&output);
    assert!(text
        .lines()
        .any(|l| l.contains("wine") && l.contains("disabled")));
    assert!(text
        .lines()
        .any(|l| l.contains("kernel") && l.contains("enabled")));

    let doctor = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["--config", &config_text, "doctor"]);
    assert_success(&doctor);
    assert!(!stdout(&doctor).contains("Optional tools"));

    std::fs::write(&config, "[probes]\ndisabled = [\"nonsense\"]\n").expect("config");
    let invalid = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["--config", &config_text, "doctor"]);
    assert_failure(&invalid, 1);
    assert!(stderr(&invalid).contains("unknown probe 'nonsense'"));

    std::fs::write(&config, "[report]\nanonymise = false\n").expect("config");
    let typo = sandbox.run_on(&scenario, &["--config", &config_text, "status"]);
    assert_failure(&typo, 1);
    assert!(stderr(&typo).contains("invalid configuration"));

    let missing = sandbox.run(&["--config", "does-not-exist.toml", "status"]);
    assert_failure(&missing, 1);
    assert!(stderr(&missing).contains("does not exist"));
}

#[test]
fn root_must_be_a_directory() {
    let sandbox = Sandbox::new();
    let missing = Path::new("definitely-not-a-directory");
    let output = sandbox
        .command()
        .arg("--root")
        .arg(missing)
        .arg("status")
        .output()
        .expect("run");
    assert_failure(&output, 1);
    assert!(stderr(&output).contains("is not a directory"));
}

    assert!(stdout(&sources).contains("Grades: A primary"));

// ------------------------------------------------------------ packaging
//
// These keep the package recipe, the documentation and the code in step.

fn repo_file(relative: &str) -> String {
    let path = Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR"))
        .join("..")
        .join("..")
        .join(relative);
    std::fs::read_to_string(&path).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{}: {e}", path.display()))
}

#[test]
fn pkgbuild_version_matches_the_workspace() {
    let pkgbuild = repo_file("packaging/arch/PKGBUILD");
    let expected = format!("pkgver={}", env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"));
    assert!(
        pkgbuild.lines().any(|line| line == expected),
        "PKGBUILD must contain the line {expected}"
    );
}

#[test]
fn pkgbuild_declares_every_optional_tool() {
    let pkgbuild = repo_file("packaging/arch/PKGBUILD");
    for tool in tuxgate_core::tools::EXTERNAL_TOOLS {
        assert!(
            pkgbuild.contains(&format!("'{}: ", tool.package)),
            "package {} (for {}) is missing from optdepends",
            tool.package,
            tool.program
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn pkgbuild_installs_nothing_privileged() {
    let pkgbuild = repo_file("packaging/arch/PKGBUILD");
    for forbidden in [
        "install=",
        "setuid",
        "chmod 4",
        "-m4755",
        "systemd/system",
        "polkit-1",
        "/etc/",
        "curl ",
        "wget ",
    ] {
        assert!(
            !pkgbuild
                .lines()
                .filter(|line| !line.trim_start().starts_with('#'))
                .any(|line| line.contains(forbidden)),
            "PKGBUILD must not contain {forbidden:?}"
        );
    }
}

#[test]
fn example_configuration_matches_the_built_in_defaults() {
    let example = repo_file("docs/config.example.toml");
    assert_eq!(example, tuxgate_core::Config::example());
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-compatibility/src/history.rs`:

```rs
//! The user's own test results, kept locally.
//!
//! The database says what sources document. The history says what the user
//! observed on their machine. Comparing entries over time reveals
//! regressions; comparing the latest entry with the database reveals where
//! the documentation and reality have drifted apart.
//!
//! The file never leaves the machine.

use std::path::Path;

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use tuxgate_core::time::is_iso_date;

use crate::{CompatError, Game, GameStatus};

/// Schema version of the history file.
pub const HISTORY_SCHEMA_VERSION: u32 = 1;

/// Longest accepted free-text note.
pub const MAX_NOTE_LENGTH: usize = 500;

/// What the user observed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum TestResult {
    /// The game started and was playable.
    Works,
    /// The game failed for a reason that is not an anti-cheat refusal.
    Broken,
    /// The anti-cheat or the vendor refused the system.
    Blocked,
}

impl TestResult {
    /// Short human label.
    pub fn label(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            TestResult::Works => "works",
            TestResult::Broken => "broken",
            TestResult::Blocked => "blocked",
        }
    }
}

/// One recorded observation.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct HistoryEntry {
    /// Game identifier in the database.
    pub game: String,
    /// `YYYY-MM-DD`.
    pub date: String,
    pub result: TestResult,
    /// Kernel release at the time, when known.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub kernel: Option<String>,
    /// How the game was run, for example a Proton build. Free text.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub runner: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub note: Option<String>,
    /// Version of Tuxgate that wrote the entry.
    pub tuxgate_version: String,
}

/// A change from working to not working.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct Regression {
    pub game: String,
    pub last_working: HistoryEntry,
    pub first_failing: HistoryEntry,
}

/// A disagreement between the user's latest observation and the database.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct Divergence {
    pub game: String,
    pub message: String,
}

/// The local history file.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct History {
    #[serde(default = "default_schema")]
    pub schema_version: u32,
    #[serde(default, rename = "entry")]
    pub entries: Vec<HistoryEntry>,
}

fn default_schema() -> u32 {
    HISTORY_SCHEMA_VERSION
}

fn io_error(path: &Path, source: std::io::Error) -> CompatError {
    CompatError::Io {
        path: path.to_path_buf(),
        source,
    }
}

impl History {
    /// An empty history.
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self {
            schema_version: HISTORY_SCHEMA_VERSION,
            entries: Vec::new(),
        }
    }

    /// Parses history text.
    pub fn parse(text: &str) -> Result<Self, CompatError> {
        let history: History = toml::from_str(text).map_err(|e| CompatError::Parse {
            what: "history.toml",
            message: e.to_string(),
        })?;
        if history.schema_version != HISTORY_SCHEMA_VERSION {
            return Err(CompatError::Schema {
                what: "history.toml",
                found: history.schema_version,
                expected: HISTORY_SCHEMA_VERSION,
            });
        }
        for entry in &history.entries {
            validate_entry(entry)?;
        }
        Ok(history)
    }

    /// Loads the history, returning an empty one when the file is absent.
    pub fn load(path: &Path) -> Result<Self, CompatError> {
        match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {
            Ok(text) => Self::parse(&text),
            Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(Self::new()),
            Err(e) => Err(io_error(path, e)),
        }
    }

    /// Writes the history, creating its directory. The file is written
    /// next to the destination and renamed over it, so an interrupted write
    /// cannot leave a truncated history.
    pub fn save(&self, path: &Path) -> Result<(), CompatError> {
        let text = toml::to_string_pretty(self).map_err(|e| CompatError::History(e.to_string()))?;
        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e| io_error(parent, e))?;
        }
        let temporary = path.with_extension("toml.tmp");
        std::fs::write(&temporary, text).map_err(|e| io_error(&temporary, e))?;
        std::fs::rename(&temporary, path).map_err(|e| io_error(path, e))
    }

    /// Appends an observation after validating it.
    pub fn record(&mut self, entry: HistoryEntry) -> Result<(), CompatError> {
        validate_entry(&entry)?;
        self.entries.push(entry);
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Entries for one game, oldest first.
    pub fn for_game(&self, game_id: &str) -> Vec<&HistoryEntry> {
        self.entries.iter().filter(|e| e.game == game_id).collect()
    }

    /// The most recent entry for a game.
    pub fn latest(&self, game_id: &str) -> Option<&HistoryEntry> {
        self.entries.iter().rev().find(|e| e.game == game_id)
    }

    /// The current regression for a game: it worked at some point and the
    /// latest observation says it no longer does.
    pub fn regression(&self, game_id: &str) -> Option<Regression> {
        let entries = self.for_game(game_id);
        let last_working = entries
            .iter()
            .rposition(|e| e.result == TestResult::Works)?;
        // Nothing after the last success means there is no regression, or
        // that it has been fixed since.
        let first_failing = entries.get(last_working + 1)?;
        Some(Regression {
            game: game_id.to_string(),
            last_working: entries[last_working].clone(),
            first_failing: (*first_failing).clone(),
        })
    }

    /// Current regressions across all games, in order of first appearance.
    pub fn regressions(&self) -> Vec<Regression> {
        let mut games: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
        for entry in &self.entries {
            if !games.contains(&entry.game.as_str()) {
                games.push(&entry.game);
            }
        }
        games
            .into_iter()
            .filter_map(|game| self.regression(game))
            .collect()
    }

    /// Compares the latest observation for a game with the database.
    pub fn divergence(&self, game: &Game) -> Option<Divergence> {
        let latest = self.latest(&game.id)?;
        let message = match (game.status, latest.result) {
            (GameStatus::Blocked, TestResult::Works) => format!(
                "Your record of {} says the game works, but the database documents it as \
                 blocked. If your record is accurate and involved no circumvention, the \
                 database is out of date: please report it.",
                latest.date
            ),
            (GameStatus::ReportedWorking, TestResult::Broken | TestResult::Blocked) => format!(
                "Your record of {} says the game is {}, but the database lists it as reported \
                 working. This may be a regression worth reporting.",
                latest.date,
                latest.result.label()
            ),
            (GameStatus::ReportedBroken, TestResult::Works) => format!(
                "Your record of {} says the game works, but the database lists it as reported \
                 broken. The database may be out of date.",
                latest.date
            ),
            _ => return None,
        };
        Some(Divergence {
            game: game.id.clone(),
            message,
        })
    }
}

fn validate_entry(entry: &HistoryEntry) -> Result<(), CompatError> {
    if entry.game.trim().is_empty() {
        return Err(CompatError::History("an entry has no game".to_string()));
    }
    if !is_iso_date(&entry.date) {
        return Err(CompatError::History(format!(
            "'{}' is not a YYYY-MM-DD date",
            entry.date
        )));
    }
    if entry
        .note
        .as_deref()
        .is_some_and(|note| note.chars().count() > MAX_NOTE_LENGTH)
    {
        return Err(CompatError::History(format!(
            "the note is longer than {MAX_NOTE_LENGTH} characters"
        )));
    }
    // The file can be edited by hand, and its text is printed to the
    // terminal: refuse anything that could carry an escape sequence.
    let texts = [
        Some(entry.game.as_str()),
        entry.kernel.as_deref(),
        entry.runner.as_deref(),
        entry.note.as_deref(),
        Some(entry.tuxgate_version.as_str()),
    ];
    if texts
        .into_iter()
        .flatten()
        .any(|text| tuxgate_core::text::sanitize(text) != text)
    {
        return Err(CompatError::History(
            "an entry contains control characters".to_string(),
        ));
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::Compatibility;

    fn entry(game: &str, date: &str, result: TestResult) -> HistoryEntry {
        HistoryEntry {
            game: game.to_string(),
            date: date.to_string(),
            result,
            kernel: Some("6.17.9-2-cachyos".to_string()),
            runner: None,
            note: None,
            tuxgate_version: "0.1.0".to_string(),
        }
    }

    fn history(entries: &[(&str, &str, TestResult)]) -> History {
        let mut history = History::new();
        for (game, date, result) in entries {
            history.record(entry(game, date, *result)).expect("record");
        }
        history
    }

    #[test]
    fn detects_a_regression() {
        let history = history(&[
            ("legends-of-runeterra", "2026-09-01", TestResult::Works),
            ("legends-of-runeterra", "2026-10-20", TestResult::Broken),
            ("legends-of-runeterra", "2026-10-25", TestResult::Broken),
        ]);
        let regression = history
            .regression("legends-of-runeterra")
            .expect("regression");
        assert_eq!(regression.last_working.date, "2026-09-01");
        assert_eq!(regression.first_failing.date, "2026-10-20");
        assert_eq!(history.regressions().len(), 1);
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_fixed_regression_is_not_reported() {
        let history = history(&[
            ("g", "2026-09-01", TestResult::Works),
            ("g", "2026-10-20", TestResult::Broken),
            ("g", "2026-11-02", TestResult::Works),
        ]);
        assert_eq!(history.regression("g"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn never_working_is_not_a_regression() {
        let history = history(&[
            ("valorant", "2026-09-01", TestResult::Blocked),
            ("valorant", "2026-10-01", TestResult::Blocked),
        ]);
        assert_eq!(history.regression("valorant"), None);
        assert_eq!(history.regression("unrecorded"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn regressions_are_tracked_per_game() {
        let history = history(&[
            ("a", "2026-01-01", TestResult::Works),
            ("b", "2026-01-02", TestResult::Works),
            ("a", "2026-02-01", TestResult::Blocked),
            ("b", "2026-02-02", TestResult::Works),
        ]);
        let regressions = history.regressions();
        assert_eq!(regressions.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(regressions[0].game, "a");
        assert_eq!(
            history.latest("b").map(|e| e.date.as_str()),
            Some("2026-02-02")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn divergence_with_the_database() {
        let db = Compatibility::builtin().expect("database");
        let valorant = db.find("valorant").expect("valorant");

        let agrees = history(&[("valorant", "2026-11-01", TestResult::Blocked)]);
        assert_eq!(agrees.divergence(valorant), None);

        let disagrees = history(&[("valorant", "2026-11-01", TestResult::Works)]);
        let divergence = disagrees.divergence(valorant).expect("divergence");
        assert!(divergence.message.contains("no circumvention"));

        let empty = History::new();
        assert_eq!(empty.divergence(valorant), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_invalid_entries() {
        let mut history = History::new();
        assert!(history
            .record(entry("g", "01/02/2026", TestResult::Works))
            .is_err());
        assert!(history
            .record(entry(" ", "2026-01-02", TestResult::Works))
            .is_err());
        let mut long = entry("g", "2026-01-02", TestResult::Works);
        long.note = Some("x".repeat(MAX_NOTE_LENGTH + 1));
        assert!(history.record(long).is_err());
        let mut escape = entry("g", "2026-01-02", TestResult::Works);
        escape.runner = Some("Proton\x1b[2J".to_string());
        assert!(history.record(escape).is_err());
        let mut newline = entry("g", "2026-01-02", TestResult::Works);
        newline.note = Some("first\nsecond".to_string());
        assert!(history.record(newline).is_err());
        assert!(history.entries.is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn saves_and_loads_round_trip() {
        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
            "tuxgate-history-test-{}-{}",
            std::process::id(),
            std::time::SystemTime::now()
                .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
                .map(|d| d.subsec_nanos())
                .unwrap_or(0)
        ));
        let path = dir.join("nested").join("history.toml");

        assert_eq!(History::load(&path).expect("missing file"), History::new());

        let mut original = history(&[("g", "2026-01-02", TestResult::Works)]);
        let mut second = entry("g", "2026-02-03", TestResult::Broken);
        second.runner = Some("Proton 10.0".to_string());
        second.note = Some("black screen after login".to_string());
        original.record(second).expect("record");
        original.save(&path).expect("save");

        let loaded = History::load(&path).expect("load");
        assert_eq!(loaded, original);
        assert!(!path.with_extension("toml.tmp").exists());

        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_corrupt_or_future_files() {
        assert!(matches!(
            History::parse("schema_version = 9\n"),
            Err(CompatError::Schema { found: 9, .. })
        ));
        assert!(matches!(
            History::parse("[[entry]]\ngame = \"g\"\n"),
            Err(CompatError::Parse { .. })
        ));
        assert!(History::parse("").expect("empty").entries.is_empty());
    }
}

```

`crates/tuxgate-compatibility/src/lib.rs`:

```rs
//! Compatibility database of Riot Games titles on Linux.
//!
//! The database and the source register are TOML files embedded at build
//! time. Every status is documented from sources and cites them; nothing in
//! the database was tested by this project. Validation runs in the unit
//! tests, so an inconsistent edit fails the build pipeline.

pub mod history;

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
use tuxgate_core::time::is_iso_date;
use tuxgate_core::Status;

const GAMES_TOML: &str = include_str!("../../../data/games.toml");
const SOURCES_TOML: &str = include_str!("../../../data/sources.toml");

/// Schema version this build understands, for both data files.
pub const SCHEMA_VERSION: u32 = 1;

/// Errors raised by the compatibility crate.
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum CompatError {
    #[error("cannot parse {what}: {message}")]
    Parse { what: &'static str, message: String },

    #[error("{what} uses schema version {found}, this build understands version {expected}")]
    Schema {
        what: &'static str,
        found: u32,
        expected: u32,
    },

    #[error("the compatibility data is inconsistent:\n  - {}", .0.join("\n  - "))]
    Invalid(Vec<String>),

    #[error("unknown game '{query}' (known games: {known})")]
    UnknownGame { query: String, known: String },

    #[error("cannot access {path}: {source}")]
    Io {
        path: std::path::PathBuf,
        #[source]
        source: std::io::Error,
    },

    #[error("{0}")]
    History(String),
}

/// How reliable a source is. See `RESEARCH.md`, section 2.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Grade {
    /// Primary source, read directly.
    #[serde(rename = "A")]
    Primary,
    /// Primary source that could not be read directly.
    #[serde(rename = "A-")]
    PrimaryUnread,
    /// Reputable secondary source.
    #[serde(rename = "B")]
    Secondary,
    /// Community source.
    #[serde(rename = "C")]
    Community,
}

impl Grade {
    /// The letter used in the documentation.
    pub fn label(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Grade::Primary => "A",
            Grade::PrimaryUnread => "A-",
            Grade::Secondary => "B",
            Grade::Community => "C",
        }
    }

    /// True for sources strong enough to support a "blocked" status.
    pub fn is_authoritative(self) -> bool {
        !matches!(self, Grade::Community)
    }
}

/// One entry of the source register.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Source {
    pub id: String,
    pub publisher: String,
    pub title: String,
    pub url: String,
    /// Publication or last-update date, when the source shows one.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub date: Option<String>,
    pub grade: Grade,
}

#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct SourcesFile {
    schema_version: u32,
    accessed: String,
    #[serde(rename = "source")]
    sources: Vec<Source>,
}

/// Where the title runs.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum Platform {
    Pc,
    Mobile,
}

/// Documented state of a title on Linux.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum GameStatus {
    /// An external requirement prevents the game from running.
    Blocked,
    /// Community reports say it runs; not verified by this project.
    ReportedWorking,
    /// Community reports say it does not run.
    ReportedBroken,
    /// Community reports disagree or changed recently.
    MixedReports,
    /// Not a desktop title.
    NotApplicable,
    /// No usable information.
    Unknown,
}

impl GameStatus {
    /// Short human label.
    pub fn label(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            GameStatus::Blocked => "blocked by an external requirement",
            GameStatus::ReportedWorking => "reported working (not verified)",
            GameStatus::ReportedBroken => "reported broken (not verified)",
            GameStatus::MixedReports => "mixed reports (not verified)",
            GameStatus::NotApplicable => "not applicable",
            GameStatus::Unknown => "unknown",
        }
    }

    /// The generic status this corresponds to. Community reports map to
    /// "unverifiable" because this project has verified none of them.
    pub fn as_status(self) -> Status {
        match self {
            GameStatus::Blocked => Status::BlockedExternal,
            GameStatus::NotApplicable => Status::NotApplicable,
            GameStatus::ReportedWorking
            | GameStatus::ReportedBroken
            | GameStatus::MixedReports
            | GameStatus::Unknown => Status::Unverifiable,
        }
    }
}

/// What a status rests on.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum Basis {
    /// Stated by a primary or reputable secondary source.
    Documented,
    /// Follows from documented facts but is not stated as such.
    Inferred,
    /// Community reports only.
    Community,
}

impl Basis {
    /// Short human label.
    pub fn label(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Basis::Documented => "documented by sources",
            Basis::Inferred => "inferred from documented facts",
            Basis::Community => "community reports only",
        }
    }
}

/// A claim and the source it comes from.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct EvidenceRef {
    /// Identifier in the source register.
    pub source: String,
    pub claim: String,
}

/// One title.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Game {
    pub id: String,
    pub name: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub aliases: Vec<String>,
    pub platform: Platform,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub anti_cheat: Option<String>,
    pub status: GameStatus,
    pub basis: Basis,
    pub summary: String,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub details: Vec<String>,
    /// Legitimate ways to play today.
    #[serde(default)]
    pub ways_to_play: Vec<String>,
    #[serde(default)]
    pub evidence: Vec<EvidenceRef>,
}

#[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
struct GamesFile {
    schema_version: u32,
    updated: String,
    #[serde(rename = "game")]
    games: Vec<Game>,
}

/// The games database together with its source register.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Compatibility {
    /// Date of the last database update.
    pub updated: String,
    /// Date the sources were last consulted.
    pub sources_accessed: String,
    pub games: Vec<Game>,
    pub sources: Vec<Source>,
}

fn is_slug(text: &str) -> bool {
    !text.is_empty()
        && text
            .chars()
            .all(|c| c.is_ascii_lowercase() || c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '-')
        && !text.starts_with('-')
        && !text.ends_with('-')
}

impl Compatibility {
    /// Parses and validates both data files.
    pub fn parse(games_toml: &str, sources_toml: &str) -> Result<Self, CompatError> {
        let games: GamesFile = toml::from_str(games_toml).map_err(|e| CompatError::Parse {
            what: "games.toml",
            message: e.to_string(),
        })?;
        let sources: SourcesFile =
            toml::from_str(sources_toml).map_err(|e| CompatError::Parse {
                what: "sources.toml",
                message: e.to_string(),
            })?;
        for (what, found) in [
            ("games.toml", games.schema_version),
            ("sources.toml", sources.schema_version),
        ] {
            if found != SCHEMA_VERSION {
                return Err(CompatError::Schema {
                    what,
                    found,
                    expected: SCHEMA_VERSION,
                });
            }
        }

        let compatibility = Self {
            updated: games.updated,
            sources_accessed: sources.accessed,
            games: games.games,
            sources: sources.sources,
        };
        let problems = compatibility.problems();
        if problems.is_empty() {
            Ok(compatibility)
        } else {
            Err(CompatError::Invalid(problems))
        }
    }

    /// The database shipped with this build.
    pub fn builtin() -> Result<Self, CompatError> {
        Self::parse(GAMES_TOML, SOURCES_TOML)
    }

    /// Looks a source up by identifier.
    pub fn source(&self, id: &str) -> Option<&Source> {
        self.sources.iter().find(|s| s.id == id)
    }

    /// Finds a game by identifier, alias or name, ignoring case.
    pub fn find(&self, query: &str) -> Option<&Game> {
        let query = query.trim().to_lowercase();
        self.games.iter().find(|game| {
            game.id == query
                || game.name.to_lowercase() == query
                || game
                    .aliases
                    .iter()
                    .any(|alias| alias.to_lowercase() == query)
        })
    }

    /// Like [`Compatibility::find`], with an error listing the known games.
    pub fn require(&self, query: &str) -> Result<&Game, CompatError> {
        self.find(query).ok_or_else(|| CompatError::UnknownGame {
            query: query.to_string(),
            known: self
                .games
                .iter()
                .map(|g| g.id.as_str())
                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                .join(", "),
        })
    }

    /// Every inconsistency in the data. Empty when the data is sound.
    pub fn problems(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        let mut problems = Vec::new();

        if !is_iso_date(&self.updated) {
            problems.push(format!(
                "games.toml: updated '{}' is not a date",
                self.updated
            ));
        }
        if !is_iso_date(&self.sources_accessed) {
            problems.push(format!(
                "sources.toml: accessed '{}' is not a date",
                self.sources_accessed
            ));
        }

        let mut seen_sources = Vec::new();
        for source in &self.sources {
            if seen_sources.contains(&&source.id) {
                problems.push(format!("source {} is defined twice", source.id));
            }
            seen_sources.push(&source.id);
            if !source.url.starts_with("https://") {
                problems.push(format!("source {} has a non-https URL", source.id));
            }
            if let Some(date) = &source.date {
                if !is_iso_date(date) {
                    problems.push(format!("source {} has an invalid date '{date}'", source.id));
                }
            }
            if source.title.trim().is_empty() || source.publisher.trim().is_empty() {
                problems.push(format!("source {} lacks a title or publisher", source.id));
            }
        }

        let mut seen_names: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
        for game in &self.games {
            if !is_slug(&game.id) {
                problems.push(format!("game id '{}' is not a lowercase slug", game.id));
            }
            for name in std::iter::once(&game.id).chain(&game.aliases) {
                let lowered = name.to_lowercase();
                if seen_names.contains(&lowered) {
                    problems.push(format!("'{name}' identifies more than one game"));
                }
                seen_names.push(lowered);
            }
            if game.summary.trim().is_empty() {
                problems.push(format!("game {} has no summary", game.id));
            }

            let grades: Vec<Grade> = game
                .evidence
                .iter()
                .filter_map(|evidence| match self.source(&evidence.source) {
                    Some(source) => Some(source.grade),
                    None => {
                        problems.push(format!(
                            "game {} cites unknown source {}",
                            game.id, evidence.source
                        ));
                        None
                    }
                })
                .collect();

            if game.evidence.iter().any(|e| e.claim.trim().is_empty()) {
                problems.push(format!("game {} has evidence without a claim", game.id));
            }
            if game.status != GameStatus::NotApplicable
                && game.status != GameStatus::Unknown
                && game.evidence.is_empty()
            {
                problems.push(format!("game {} has a status but cites no source", game.id));
            }
            // A claim that a vendor blocks a game must rest on more than
            // forum posts.
            if game.status == GameStatus::Blocked {
                if !grades.iter().any(|g| g.is_authoritative()) {
                    problems.push(format!(
                        "game {} is 'blocked' without a primary or secondary source",
                        game.id
                    ));
                }
                if game.basis == Basis::Community {
                    problems.push(format!(
                        "game {} is 'blocked' on community reports only",
                        game.id
                    ));
                }
                if game.ways_to_play.is_empty() {
                    problems.push(format!(
                        "game {} is 'blocked' but lists no legitimate way to play",
                        game.id
                    ));
                }
            }
            // The converse: nothing may be presented as documented when only
            // community sources support it.
            if game.basis == Basis::Documented
                && !grades.is_empty()
                && !grades.iter().any(|g| g.is_authoritative())
            {
                problems.push(format!(
                    "game {} claims a documented basis with community sources only",
                    game.id
                ));
            }
        }
        problems
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    fn builtin() -> Compatibility {
        Compatibility::builtin().expect("the shipped database must be valid")
    }

    const MINIMAL_SOURCES: &str = r#"
schema_version = 1
accessed = "2026-11-28"

[[source]]
id = "P1"
publisher = "Vendor"
title = "Official page"
url = "https://example.org/official"
date = "2026-01-01"
grade = "A"

[[source]]
id = "C1"
publisher = "Forum"
title = "A thread"
url = "https://example.org/thread"
grade = "C"
"#;

    fn games(body: &str) -> String {
        format!("schema_version = 1\nupdated = \"2026-11-28\"\n{body}")
    }

    fn problems_of(body: &str) -> Vec<String> {
        match Compatibility::parse(&games(body), MINIMAL_SOURCES) {
            Ok(_) => Vec::new(),
            Err(CompatError::Invalid(problems)) => problems,
            Err(other) => panic!("unexpected error: {other}"),
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn shipped_database_is_valid() {
        let db = builtin();
        assert!(db.problems().is_empty());
        assert!(db.games.len() >= 6);
        assert!(db.sources.len() >= 30);
    }

    #[test]
    fn vanguard_titles_are_blocked_never_working() {
        let db = builtin();
        for id in ["valorant", "league-of-legends", "teamfight-tactics", "2xko"] {
            let game = db.find(id).expect(id);
            assert_eq!(game.status, GameStatus::Blocked, "{id}");
            assert_eq!(game.status.as_status(), Status::BlockedExternal);
            assert!(
                game.anti_cheat
                    .as_deref()
                    .is_some_and(|a| a.contains("Vanguard")),
                "{id} must name Vanguard"
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn no_game_is_presented_as_verified_working() {
        // The project has tested nothing, so no status may map to a
        // positive "detected".
        for game in &builtin().games {
            assert_ne!(game.status.as_status(), Status::Detected, "{}", game.id);
        }
        assert!(GameStatus::ReportedWorking.label().contains("not verified"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn finds_games_by_id_alias_and_name() {
        let db = builtin();
        assert_eq!(db.find("valorant").map(|g| g.id.as_str()), Some("valorant"));
        assert_eq!(
            db.find("LoL").map(|g| g.id.as_str()),
            Some("league-of-legends")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            db.find(" TFT ").map(|g| g.id.as_str()),
            Some("teamfight-tactics")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            db.find("Legends of Runeterra").map(|g| g.id.as_str()),
            Some("legends-of-runeterra")
        );
        assert!(db.find("minecraft").is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_game_error_lists_known_games() {
        let error = builtin().require("minecraft").expect_err("unknown");
        let message = error.to_string();
        assert!(message.contains("minecraft"));
        assert!(message.contains("valorant"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_cited_source_exists_and_is_https() {
        let db = builtin();
        for game in &db.games {
            for evidence in &game.evidence {
                let source = db.source(&evidence.source).expect("cited source");
                assert!(source.url.starts_with("https://"));
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_unknown_sources() {
        let problems = problems_of(
            r#"
[[game]]
id = "g"
name = "G"
platform = "pc"
status = "reported-working"
basis = "community"
summary = "s"
[[game.evidence]]
source = "NOPE"
claim = "c"
"#,
        );
        assert!(problems.iter().any(|p| p.contains("unknown source NOPE")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_blocked_on_community_evidence() {
        let problems = problems_of(
            r#"
[[game]]
id = "g"
name = "G"
platform = "pc"
status = "blocked"
basis = "documented"
summary = "s"
ways_to_play = ["dual boot"]
[[game.evidence]]
source = "C1"
claim = "someone said so"
"#,
        );
        assert!(problems
            .iter()
            .any(|p| p.contains("without a primary or secondary source")));
        assert!(problems
            .iter()
            .any(|p| p.contains("documented basis with community sources only")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_status_without_evidence_and_duplicate_names() {
        let problems = problems_of(
            r#"
[[game]]
id = "g"
name = "G"
aliases = ["x"]
platform = "pc"
status = "reported-broken"
basis = "community"
summary = "s"

[[game]]
id = "x"
name = "X"
platform = "mobile"
status = "not-applicable"
basis = "documented"
summary = "s"
"#,
        );
        assert!(problems.iter().any(|p| p.contains("cites no source")));
        assert!(problems.iter().any(|p| p.contains("more than one game")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_bad_ids_and_dates() {
        let text = "schema_version = 1\nupdated = \"yesterday\"\n[[game]]\nid = \"Bad Id\"\nname = \"B\"\nplatform = \"pc\"\nstatus = \"unknown\"\nbasis = \"community\"\nsummary = \"s\"\n";
        let Err(CompatError::Invalid(problems)) = Compatibility::parse(text, MINIMAL_SOURCES)
        else {
            panic!("expected invalid data");
        };
        assert!(problems.iter().any(|p| p.contains("not a date")));
        assert!(problems.iter().any(|p| p.contains("not a lowercase slug")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_unknown_fields_and_schema_versions() {
        let unknown_field = games("[[game]]\nid = \"g\"\nname = \"G\"\nplatform = \"pc\"\nstatus = \"unknown\"\nbasis = \"community\"\nsummary = \"s\"\ntested = true\n");
        assert!(matches!(
            Compatibility::parse(&unknown_field, MINIMAL_SOURCES),
            Err(CompatError::Parse {
                what: "games.toml",
                ..
            })
        ));

        let future = "schema_version = 2\nupdated = \"2026-11-28\"\ngame = []\n";
        assert!(matches!(
            Compatibility::parse(future, MINIMAL_SOURCES),
            Err(CompatError::Schema { found: 2, .. })
        ));
    }

    #[test]
    fn grades_round_trip() {
        for (text, grade) in [
            ("A", Grade::Primary),
            ("A-", Grade::PrimaryUnread),
            ("B", Grade::Secondary),
            ("C", Grade::Community),
        ] {
            assert_eq!(grade.label(), text);
        }
        assert!(Grade::PrimaryUnread.is_authoritative());
        assert!(!Grade::Community.is_authoritative());
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/config.rs`:

```rs
//! User configuration, stored as TOML.
//!
//! A missing file means defaults. An invalid file is an error: silently
//! ignoring a typo would leave the user believing a setting is active.

use std::path::Path;
use std::time::Duration;

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

use crate::error::CoreError;

/// When to colour terminal output.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
pub enum ColorMode {
    /// Colour when writing to a terminal and `NO_COLOR` is unset.
    #[default]
    Auto,
    Always,
    Never,
}

/// `[output]` section.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct OutputConfig {
    pub color: ColorMode,
}

/// `[report]` section.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct ReportConfig {
    /// Remove user names, home paths and hardware identifiers from reports.
    pub anonymize: bool,
}

impl Default for ReportConfig {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self { anonymize: true }
    }
}

/// `[probes]` section.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct ProbesConfig {
    /// Identifiers of probes to skip.
    pub disabled: Vec<String>,
    /// Time limit for each diagnostic command, in seconds (1 to 60).
    pub command_timeout_secs: u64,
}

impl Default for ProbesConfig {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self {
            disabled: Vec::new(),
            command_timeout_secs: 5,
        }
    }
}

/// The whole configuration file.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(default, deny_unknown_fields)]
pub struct Config {
    pub output: OutputConfig,
    pub report: ReportConfig,
    pub probes: ProbesConfig,
}

impl Config {
    /// Parses and validates configuration text. `origin` is only used in
    /// error messages.
    pub fn parse(text: &str, origin: &Path) -> Result<Self, CoreError> {
        let config: Config = toml::from_str(text).map_err(|e| CoreError::Config {
            path: origin.to_path_buf(),
            message: e.to_string(),
        })?;
        config.validate(origin)?;
        Ok(config)
    }

    /// Loads the configuration, returning defaults when the file is absent.
    pub fn load(path: &Path) -> Result<Self, CoreError> {
        match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {
            Ok(text) => Self::parse(&text, path),
            Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(Self::default()),
            Err(source) => Err(CoreError::Io {
                path: path.to_path_buf(),
                source,
            }),
        }
    }

    fn validate(&self, origin: &Path) -> Result<(), CoreError> {
        if !(1..=60).contains(&self.probes.command_timeout_secs) {
            return Err(CoreError::Config {
                path: origin.to_path_buf(),
                message: format!(
                    "probes.command_timeout_secs must be between 1 and 60, got {}",
                    self.probes.command_timeout_secs
                ),
            });
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// The command time limit as a duration.
    pub fn command_timeout(&self) -> Duration {
        Duration::from_secs(self.probes.command_timeout_secs)
    }

    /// The default configuration as commented TOML, for documentation.
    pub fn example() -> &'static str {
        "\
# Tuxgate configuration. Every key is optional; the values below are the defaults.

[output]
# \"auto\", \"always\" or \"never\"
color = \"auto\"

[report]
# Strip user names, home paths and hardware identifiers from reports.
anonymize = true

[probes]
# Probe identifiers to skip, for example [\"wine\", \"vulkan\"].
disabled = []
# Time limit for each diagnostic command, in seconds (1 to 60).
command_timeout_secs = 5
"
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use std::path::PathBuf;

    fn origin() -> PathBuf {
        PathBuf::from("config.toml")
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_text_gives_defaults() {
        let config = Config::parse("", &origin()).expect("parse");
        assert_eq!(config, Config::default());
        assert!(config.report.anonymize);
        assert_eq!(config.command_timeout(), Duration::from_secs(5));
    }

    #[test]
    fn example_matches_defaults() {
        let config = Config::parse(Config::example(), &origin()).expect("parse");
        assert_eq!(config, Config::default());
    }

    #[test]
    fn partial_sections_keep_other_defaults() {
        let config = Config::parse(
            "[probes]\ndisabled = [\"wine\"]\n\n[output]\ncolor = \"never\"\n",
            &origin(),
        )
        .expect("parse");
        assert_eq!(config.probes.disabled, vec!["wine".to_string()]);
        assert_eq!(config.probes.command_timeout_secs, 5);
        assert_eq!(config.output.color, ColorMode::Never);
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_keys_are_rejected() {
        let error = Config::parse("[report]\nanonymise = false\n", &origin()).expect_err("typo");
        assert!(matches!(error, CoreError::Config { .. }));
        assert!(error.to_string().contains("anonymise"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn out_of_range_timeout_is_rejected() {
        for value in ["0", "61"] {
            let text = format!("[probes]\ncommand_timeout_secs = {value}\n");
            assert!(matches!(
                Config::parse(&text, &origin()),
                Err(CoreError::Config { .. })
            ));
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn missing_file_gives_defaults() {
        let path = std::env::temp_dir().join("tuxgate-config-that-does-not-exist.toml");
        assert_eq!(Config::load(&path).expect("load"), Config::default());
    }
}

```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/context.rs`:

```rs
//! Access to the system under inspection.
//!
//! Every probe reads files and runs commands through a [`SystemContext`].
//! The context has a root directory, so the same code inspects the live
//! system (`/`), an offline tree (a mounted root) or a test fixture.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;
use std::io::Read;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::process::{Command, Stdio};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

/// Default limit for a diagnostic command.
pub const DEFAULT_COMMAND_TIMEOUT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(5);

/// Most output kept from one stream of a diagnostic command. A program that
/// writes more than this blocks on its pipe and is stopped by the timeout.
const MAX_COMMAND_OUTPUT: u64 = 4 * 1024 * 1024;

/// Search path used when `PATH` is not set.
const DEFAULT_PATH: &str = "/usr/local/sbin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/usr/sbin:/bin:/sbin";

/// Directories owned by the system, searched for programs that must not be
/// substitutable through the user's `PATH`.
pub const SYSTEM_PROGRAM_DIRS: [&str; 4] = ["/usr/bin", "/usr/sbin", "/bin", "/sbin"];

/// Why a file could not be read.
///
/// The distinction matters: a missing file usually means "not present",
/// while a permission error means "cannot tell".
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum FsError {
    NotFound,
    PermissionDenied,
    Other(String),
}

impl FsError {
    fn from_io(error: &std::io::Error) -> Self {
        match error.kind() {
            std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => FsError::NotFound,
            std::io::ErrorKind::PermissionDenied => FsError::PermissionDenied,
            _ => FsError::Other(error.to_string()),
        }
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Display for FsError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        match self {
            FsError::NotFound => f.write_str("not found"),
            FsError::PermissionDenied => f.write_str("permission denied"),
            FsError::Other(message) => f.write_str(message),
        }
    }
}

/// Kind of a file system entry.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum EntryKind {
    File,
    Dir,
}

/// Captured result of a command.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct CommandOutput {
    /// Exit code, or `None` when the process was killed by a signal.
    pub code: Option<i32>,
    pub stdout: String,
    pub stderr: String,
}

impl CommandOutput {
    /// True when the command exited with status 0.
    pub fn success(&self) -> bool {
        self.code == Some(0)
    }
}

/// Why a command produced no output.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum CommandError {
    /// The program is not installed.
    NotFound,
    /// The program did not finish in time and was killed.
    Timeout,
    /// Commands are not executed in this context (offline inspection).
    Disabled,
    /// The program could not be started for another reason.
    Failed(String),
}

impl std::fmt::Display for CommandError {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        match self {
            CommandError::NotFound => f.write_str("program not found"),
            CommandError::Timeout => f.write_str("timed out"),
            CommandError::Disabled => f.write_str("commands are disabled in offline mode"),
            CommandError::Failed(message) => f.write_str(message),
        }
    }
}

/// Runs external programs. Implemented by the real runner, by a runner that
/// refuses everything, and by a scripted runner in tests.
pub trait CommandRunner: Send + Sync {
    /// Runs a program, capturing its output, with no terminal attached.
    fn run(
        &self,
        program: &str,
        args: &[&str],
        timeout: Duration,
    ) -> Result<CommandOutput, CommandError>;

    /// Runs a program attached to the user's terminal, with no time limit.
    /// Used only for actions the user has just confirmed, where a password
    /// prompt may appear. Returns the exit code.
    fn run_interactive(&self, program: &str, args: &[&str]) -> Result<Option<i32>, CommandError>;
}

/// Executes programs on the host.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct SystemRunner;

fn spawn_error(error: &std::io::Error) -> CommandError {
    if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound {
        CommandError::NotFound
    } else {
        CommandError::Failed(error.to_string())
    }
}

fn drain<R: Read + Send + 'static>(reader: Option<R>) -> std::thread::JoinHandle<String> {
    std::thread::spawn(move || {
        let mut bytes = Vec::new();
        if let Some(reader) = reader {
            // Bounded, so a misbehaving program cannot exhaust memory. A read
            // error only truncates diagnostic output.
            let _ = reader.take(MAX_COMMAND_OUTPUT).read_to_end(&mut bytes);
        }
        String::from_utf8_lossy(&bytes).into_owned()
    })
}

impl CommandRunner for SystemRunner {
    fn run(
        &self,
        program: &str,
        args: &[&str],
        timeout: Duration,
    ) -> Result<CommandOutput, CommandError> {
        let mut child = Command::new(program)
            .args(args)
            // Stable, parseable output regardless of the user's locale.
            .env("LC_ALL", "C")
            .stdin(Stdio::null())
            .stdout(Stdio::piped())
            .stderr(Stdio::piped())
            .spawn()
            .map_err(|e| spawn_error(&e))?;

        // Drain both pipes on their own threads so a chatty program cannot
        // block on a full pipe while we wait for it.
        let stdout = drain(child.stdout.take());
        let stderr = drain(child.stderr.take());

        let deadline = Instant::now() + timeout;
        let status = loop {
            match child.try_wait() {
                Ok(Some(status)) => break status,
                Ok(None) => {
                    if Instant::now() >= deadline {
                        let _ = child.kill();
                        let _ = child.wait();
                        return Err(CommandError::Timeout);
                    }
                    std::thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(20));
                }
                Err(e) => return Err(CommandError::Failed(e.to_string())),
            }
        };

        Ok(CommandOutput {
            code: status.code(),
            stdout: stdout.join().unwrap_or_default(),
            stderr: stderr.join().unwrap_or_default(),
        })
    }

    fn run_interactive(&self, program: &str, args: &[&str]) -> Result<Option<i32>, CommandError> {
        let status = Command::new(program)
            .args(args)
            .status()
            .map_err(|e| spawn_error(&e))?;
        Ok(status.code())
    }
}

/// Refuses to run anything. Used when inspecting an offline tree, where
/// running host commands would describe the wrong system.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct NullRunner;

impl CommandRunner for NullRunner {
    fn run(&self, _: &str, _: &[&str], _: Duration) -> Result<CommandOutput, CommandError> {
        Err(CommandError::Disabled)
    }

    fn run_interactive(&self, _: &str, _: &[&str]) -> Result<Option<i32>, CommandError> {
        Err(CommandError::Disabled)
    }
}

/// Maps an absolute path of an inspected system onto a root directory.
///
/// Linux uses `:` in some sysfs names (PCI addresses such as
/// `0000:01:00.0`), which Windows forbids in file names. On a Windows
/// development host the character is replaced, so fixtures behave the same
/// everywhere. This never applies to a real Linux system.
pub fn resolve_under(root: &Path, absolute: &str) -> PathBuf {
    let relative = absolute.trim_start_matches('/');
    if relative.is_empty() {
        return root.to_path_buf();
    }
    if cfg!(windows) {
        root.join(relative.replace(':', "_"))
    } else {
        root.join(relative)
    }
}

/// The system being inspected: a file tree, a way to run commands, and an
/// environment.
pub struct SystemContext {
    root: PathBuf,
    runner: Box<dyn CommandRunner>,
    env: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    command_timeout: Duration,
    offline: bool,
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for SystemContext {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("SystemContext")
            .field("root", &self.root)
            .field("offline", &self.offline)
            .field("command_timeout", &self.command_timeout)
            .finish_non_exhaustive()
    }
}

impl SystemContext {
    /// The running system.
    pub fn host() -> Self {
        let env = std::env::vars_os()
            .filter_map(|(k, v)| Some((k.into_string().ok()?, v.into_string().ok()?)))
            .collect();
        Self {
            root: PathBuf::from("/"),
            runner: Box::new(SystemRunner),
            env,
            command_timeout: DEFAULT_COMMAND_TIMEOUT,
            offline: false,
        }
    }

    /// A system tree mounted at `root`. No command is executed and no
    /// environment variable is assumed.
    pub fn offline(root: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> Self {
        Self {
            root: root.into(),
            runner: Box::new(NullRunner),
            env: BTreeMap::new(),
            command_timeout: DEFAULT_COMMAND_TIMEOUT,
            offline: true,
        }
    }

    /// A fully specified context. Mostly useful in tests.
    pub fn new(
        root: impl Into<PathBuf>,
        runner: Box<dyn CommandRunner>,
        env: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    ) -> Self {
        Self {
            root: root.into(),
            runner,
            env,
            command_timeout: DEFAULT_COMMAND_TIMEOUT,
            offline: false,
        }
    }

    /// Changes the time limit applied to diagnostic commands.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn with_command_timeout(mut self, timeout: Duration) -> Self {
        self.command_timeout = timeout;
        self
    }

    /// True when inspecting a tree that is not the running system.
    pub fn is_offline(&self) -> bool {
        self.offline
    }

    /// Maps an absolute path of the inspected system to a real path.
    pub fn resolve(&self, absolute: &str) -> PathBuf {
        resolve_under(&self.root, absolute)
    }

    /// Reads a file as raw bytes.
    pub fn read_bytes(&self, absolute: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, FsError> {
        std::fs::read(self.resolve(absolute)).map_err(|e| FsError::from_io(&e))
    }

    /// Reads a file as text, replacing invalid UTF-8.
    pub fn read_to_string(&self, absolute: &str) -> Result<String, FsError> {
        self.read_bytes(absolute)
            .map(|bytes| String::from_utf8_lossy(&bytes).into_owned())
    }

    /// Reads a single-value file such as those in `/proc/sys` and `/sys`.
    pub fn read_trimmed(&self, absolute: &str) -> Result<String, FsError> {
        self.read_to_string(absolute).map(|s| s.trim().to_string())
    }

    /// Returns the kind of an entry, or why it cannot be examined.
    pub fn stat(&self, absolute: &str) -> Result<EntryKind, FsError> {
        let metadata =
            std::fs::metadata(self.resolve(absolute)).map_err(|e| FsError::from_io(&e))?;
        Ok(if metadata.is_dir() {
            EntryKind::Dir
        } else {
            EntryKind::File
        })
    }

    /// True when the entry exists. A permission error counts as "no"; use
    /// [`SystemContext::stat`] when the difference matters.
    pub fn exists(&self, absolute: &str) -> bool {
        self.stat(absolute).is_ok()
    }

    /// True when the entry exists and is a directory.
    pub fn is_dir(&self, absolute: &str) -> bool {
        self.stat(absolute) == Ok(EntryKind::Dir)
    }

    /// True when the entry exists and is not a directory.
    pub fn is_file(&self, absolute: &str) -> bool {
        self.stat(absolute) == Ok(EntryKind::File)
    }

    /// Lists the entry names of a directory, sorted.
    pub fn list_dir(&self, absolute: &str) -> Result<Vec<String>, FsError> {
        let entries =
            std::fs::read_dir(self.resolve(absolute)).map_err(|e| FsError::from_io(&e))?;
        let mut names: Vec<String> = entries
            .filter_map(Result::ok)
            .map(|entry| entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().into_owned())
            .collect();
        names.sort();
        Ok(names)
    }

    /// Reads an environment variable of the inspected session.
    pub fn env(&self, key: &str) -> Option<&str> {
        self.env
            .get(key)
            .map(String::as_str)
            .filter(|v| !v.is_empty())
    }

    /// Runs a diagnostic command and captures its output.
    pub fn run(&self, program: &str, args: &[&str]) -> Result<CommandOutput, CommandError> {
        self.runner.run(program, args, self.command_timeout)
    }

    /// Runs a command attached to the terminal. See
    /// [`CommandRunner::run_interactive`].
    pub fn run_interactive(
        &self,
        program: &str,
        args: &[&str],
    ) -> Result<Option<i32>, CommandError> {
        self.runner.run_interactive(program, args)
    }

    /// Looks a program up in `PATH`, returning its absolute path within the
    /// inspected system.
    pub fn find_program(&self, name: &str) -> Option<String> {
        let path = self.env("PATH").unwrap_or(DEFAULT_PATH);
        path.split(':')
            .filter(|dir| dir.starts_with('/'))
            .map(|dir| format!("{}/{}", dir.trim_end_matches('/'), name))
            .find(|candidate| self.is_file(candidate))
    }

    /// Looks a program up in the fixed system directories only, ignoring
    /// `PATH`. Use this for anything that runs with elevated rights or
    /// changes system state, so that a directory writable by the user's
    /// session cannot substitute the program.
    pub fn find_system_program(&self, name: &str) -> Option<String> {
        SYSTEM_PROGRAM_DIRS
            .iter()
            .map(|dir| format!("{dir}/{name}"))
            .find(|candidate| self.is_file(candidate))
    }

    /// The root of the inspected tree.
    pub fn root(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.root
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::testing::{Fixture, MockRunner};

    #[test]
    fn resolves_paths_under_the_root() {
        let ctx = SystemContext::offline("/mnt/target");
        assert_eq!(
            ctx.resolve("/etc/os-release"),
            PathBuf::from("/mnt/target").join("etc/os-release")
        );
        assert_eq!(ctx.resolve("/"), PathBuf::from("/mnt/target"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn missing_files_are_not_found() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let ctx = fixture.context();
        assert_eq!(ctx.read_to_string("/nope"), Err(FsError::NotFound));
        assert!(!ctx.exists("/nope"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn reads_and_lists_fixture_content() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new()
            .file("/proc/sys/kernel/osrelease", "6.17.9-2-cachyos\n")
            .file("/sys/class/tpm/tpm0/tpm_version_major", "2\n")
            .dir("/sys/kernel/iommu_groups/0");
        let ctx = fixture.context();
        assert_eq!(
            ctx.read_trimmed("/proc/sys/kernel/osrelease").as_deref(),
            Ok("6.17.9-2-cachyos")
        );
        assert_eq!(ctx.list_dir("/sys/class/tpm"), Ok(vec!["tpm0".to_string()]));
        assert!(ctx.is_dir("/sys/kernel/iommu_groups/0"));
        assert!(ctx.is_file("/sys/class/tpm/tpm0/tpm_version_major"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn offline_context_refuses_commands() {
        let ctx = SystemContext::offline("/mnt/target");
        assert!(ctx.is_offline());
        assert_eq!(ctx.run("uname", &["-r"]), Err(CommandError::Disabled));
        assert_eq!(
            ctx.run_interactive("systemctl", &["reboot"]),
            Err(CommandError::Disabled)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn finds_programs_through_path() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new()
            .file("/usr/bin/bootctl", "")
            .dir("/usr/bin/not-a-program");
        let ctx = fixture.context_with(MockRunner::new(), &[("PATH", "/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin")]);
        assert_eq!(
            ctx.find_program("bootctl").as_deref(),
            Some("/usr/bin/bootctl")
        );
        assert_eq!(ctx.find_program("efibootmgr"), None);
        assert_eq!(ctx.find_program("not-a-program"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn system_program_lookup_ignores_path() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new()
            .file("/home/u/bin/efibootmgr", "")
            .file("/usr/sbin/efibootmgr", "")
            .file("/home/u/bin/onlyhere", "");
        let ctx = fixture.context_with(MockRunner::new(), &[("PATH", "/home/u/bin")]);
        assert_eq!(
            ctx.find_program("efibootmgr").as_deref(),
            Some("/home/u/bin/efibootmgr")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            ctx.find_system_program("efibootmgr").as_deref(),
            Some("/usr/sbin/efibootmgr")
        );
        assert_eq!(ctx.find_system_program("onlyhere"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_environment_variables_are_ignored() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let ctx = fixture.context_with(MockRunner::new(), &[("HOME", "")]);
        assert_eq!(ctx.env("HOME"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn system_runner_reports_missing_programs() {
        let result = SystemRunner.run(
            "tuxgate-test-program-that-does-not-exist",
            &[],
            Duration::from_secs(1),
        );
        assert_eq!(result, Err(CommandError::NotFound));
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/efi.rs`:

```rs
//! Read-only access to UEFI variables through efivarfs.
//!
//! Each file under `/sys/firmware/efi/efivars` is named `Name-GUID` and holds
//! four bytes of attributes, little endian, followed by the variable data.

use crate::context::{FsError, SystemContext};

/// Directory where efivarfs is mounted.
pub const EFIVARS_DIR: &str = "/sys/firmware/efi/efivars";

/// Directory that exists when the system was booted through UEFI.
pub const EFI_DIR: &str = "/sys/firmware/efi";

/// GUID of the variables defined by the UEFI specification.
pub const EFI_GLOBAL_GUID: &str = "8be4df61-93ca-11d2-aa0d-00e098032b8c";

/// GUID of the systemd Boot Loader Interface variables.
pub const LOADER_GUID: &str = "4a67b082-0a4c-41cf-b6c7-440b29bb8c4f";

/// Path of a variable file.
pub fn var_path(name: &str, guid: &str) -> String {
    format!("{EFIVARS_DIR}/{name}-{guid}")
}

/// Reads the data of a variable, without its attribute prefix.
pub fn read_var(ctx: &SystemContext, name: &str, guid: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, FsError> {
    let bytes = ctx.read_bytes(&var_path(name, guid))?;
    if bytes.len() < 4 {
        return Err(FsError::Other(format!(
            "variable {name} is truncated ({} bytes)",
            bytes.len()
        )));
    }
    Ok(bytes[4..].to_vec())
}

/// Reads a variable holding a single byte, such as `SecureBoot`.
pub fn read_u8(ctx: &SystemContext, name: &str, guid: &str) -> Result<u8, FsError> {
    let data = read_var(ctx, name, guid)?;
    data.first()
        .copied()
        .ok_or_else(|| FsError::Other(format!("variable {name} is empty")))
}

/// Decodes a UTF-16LE string, stopping at the first NUL.
pub fn utf16le_to_string(bytes: &[u8]) -> String {
    let units: Vec<u16> = bytes
        .chunks_exact(2)
        .map(|pair| u16::from_le_bytes([pair[0], pair[1]]))
        .take_while(|unit| *unit != 0)
        .collect();
    String::from_utf16_lossy(&units)
}

/// True when the system exposes UEFI runtime information.
pub fn booted_with_uefi(ctx: &SystemContext) -> bool {
    ctx.is_dir(EFI_DIR)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::testing::{efi_var_u8, efi_var_utf16, Fixture};

    #[test]
    fn reads_a_byte_variable() {
        let fixture =
            Fixture::new().bytes(&var_path("SecureBoot", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID), &efi_var_u8(1));
        let ctx = fixture.context();
        assert_eq!(read_u8(&ctx, "SecureBoot", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID), Ok(1));
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_truncated_variables() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new().bytes(&var_path("SecureBoot", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID), &[0x06, 0x00]);
        let ctx = fixture.context();
        assert!(matches!(
            read_var(&ctx, "SecureBoot", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID),
            Err(FsError::Other(_))
        ));
    }

    #[test]
    fn missing_variable_is_not_found() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let ctx = fixture.context();
        assert_eq!(
            read_var(&ctx, "SecureBoot", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID),
            Err(FsError::NotFound)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn decodes_utf16_strings() {
        let raw = efi_var_utf16("systemd-boot 258.2-1-arch");
        assert_eq!(utf16le_to_string(&raw[4..]), "systemd-boot 258.2-1-arch");
        assert_eq!(
            utf16le_to_string(&[0x41, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x42, 0x00]),
            "A"
        );
        assert_eq!(utf16le_to_string(&[]), "");
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/error.rs`:

```rs
//! Error type for the core crate.

use std::path::PathBuf;

/// Errors raised by core services.
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum CoreError {
    /// A configuration file exists but cannot be parsed or is invalid.
    #[error("invalid configuration in {path}: {message}")]
    Config { path: PathBuf, message: String },

    /// A file could not be read or written.
    #[error("cannot access {path}: {source}")]
    Io {
        path: PathBuf,
        #[source]
        source: std::io::Error,
    },

    /// Two probes were registered under the same identifier.
    #[error("probe '{0}' is registered twice")]
    DuplicateProbe(String),

    /// A probe identifier is not known to the registry.
    #[error("unknown probe '{name}' (known probes: {known})")]
    UnknownProbe { name: String, known: String },

    /// The user directories cannot be determined (no HOME).
    #[error("cannot determine the user directories: HOME is not set")]
    NoHome,
}
```
