`crates/tuxgate-core/src/finding.rs`:

```rs
//! The result model: what a probe found, and how sure we are.

use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};

use crate::text::sanitize;

/// Shown with every security-related finding.
///
/// Detecting a TPM, Secure Boot or an IOMMU locally says nothing about
/// whether a remote anti-cheat would accept the machine.
pub const ATTESTATION_DISCLAIMER: &str = "Local detection only. This is not an attestation and is \
     not recognised by Riot Vanguard or any other anti-cheat.";

/// Outcome of a single check.
///
/// `Detected` and `NotDetected` are statements of fact, not of quality:
/// detecting a hypervisor is a `Detected` finding even though it is bad news
/// for anti-cheat compatibility.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum Status {
    /// The thing was looked for and found.
    Detected,
    /// The thing was looked for and is absent or disabled.
    NotDetected,
    /// The check could not be completed (missing permission, missing tool,
    /// interface not exposed by this kernel).
    Unverifiable,
    /// The check makes no sense on this system.
    NotApplicable,
    /// Something outside this machine and outside this project decides the
    /// outcome, typically a vendor requirement.
    BlockedExternal,
}

impl Status {
    /// Every status, in display order.
    pub const ALL: [Status; 5] = [
        Status::Detected,
        Status::NotDetected,
        Status::Unverifiable,
        Status::NotApplicable,
        Status::BlockedExternal,
    ];

    /// Short human label.
    pub fn label(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Status::Detected => "detected",
            Status::NotDetected => "not detected",
            Status::Unverifiable => "unverifiable",
            Status::NotApplicable => "not applicable",
            Status::BlockedExternal => "blocked (external)",
        }
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Display for Status {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(self.label())
    }
}

/// Area a finding belongs to.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum Category {
    System,
    Graphics,
    Gaming,
    Security,
    Boot,
    Tools,
}

impl Category {
    /// Every category, in display order.
    pub const ALL: [Category; 6] = [
        Category::System,
        Category::Graphics,
        Category::Gaming,
        Category::Security,
        Category::Boot,
        Category::Tools,
    ];

    /// Section heading.
    pub fn title(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Category::System => "System",
            Category::Graphics => "Graphics",
            Category::Gaming => "Gaming environment",
            Category::Security => "Security features",
            Category::Boot => "Boot",
            Category::Tools => "Optional tools",
        }
    }
}

/// Where a piece of evidence came from.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum EvidenceKind {
    /// A file or directory that was read.
    File,
    /// A command that was executed.
    Command,
    /// A conclusion drawn from other findings.
    Derived,
}

/// What a finding is based on, so a reader can reproduce it.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Evidence {
    pub kind: EvidenceKind,
    pub source: String,
}

/// One observation about the system.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Finding {
    /// Stable identifier, for example `security.secure_boot`.
    pub id: String,
    pub category: Category,
    /// Human title, for example "Secure Boot".
    pub title: String,
    pub status: Status,
    /// The observed value, when there is one.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub value: Option<String>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
    pub evidence: Vec<Evidence>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
    pub notes: Vec<String>,
}

impl Finding {
    /// Creates a finding with no value, evidence or notes.
    pub fn new(
        id: impl Into<String>,
        category: Category,
        title: impl Into<String>,
        status: Status,
    ) -> Self {
        Self {
            id: id.into(),
            category,
            title: title.into(),
            status,
            value: None,
            evidence: Vec::new(),
            notes: Vec::new(),
        }
    }

    /// Sets the observed value.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn value(mut self, value: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.value = Some(sanitize(&value.into()));
        self
    }

    /// Records a file that was read.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn file(mut self, path: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.evidence.push(Evidence {
            kind: EvidenceKind::File,
            source: sanitize(&path.into()),
        });
        self
    }

    /// Records a command that was executed.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn command(mut self, command: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.evidence.push(Evidence {
            kind: EvidenceKind::Command,
            source: sanitize(&command.into()),
        });
        self
    }

    /// Records that the finding was derived from other information.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn derived(mut self, from: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.evidence.push(Evidence {
            kind: EvidenceKind::Derived,
            source: sanitize(&from.into()),
        });
        self
    }

    /// Adds an explanatory note.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn note(mut self, note: impl Into<String>) -> Self {
        self.notes.push(sanitize(&note.into()));
        self
    }

    /// Adds the standard "this is not an attestation" note.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn not_an_attestation(self) -> Self {
        self.note(ATTESTATION_DISCLAIMER)
    }
}

/// Finds a finding by identifier.
pub fn find<'a>(findings: &'a [Finding], id: &str) -> Option<&'a Finding> {
    findings.iter().find(|f| f.id == id)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn status_serialises_as_snake_case() {
        let json = serde_json::to_string(&Status::BlockedExternal).expect("serialise");
        assert_eq!(json, "\"blocked_external\"");
        let back: Status = serde_json::from_str("\"not_detected\"").expect("deserialise");
        assert_eq!(back, Status::NotDetected);
    }

    #[test]
    fn every_status_has_a_distinct_label() {
        let mut labels: Vec<_> = Status::ALL.iter().map(|s| s.label()).collect();
        labels.sort_unstable();
        labels.dedup();
        assert_eq!(labels.len(), Status::ALL.len());
    }

    #[test]
    fn builder_collects_evidence_and_notes() {
        let finding = Finding::new("a.b", Category::Security, "Thing", Status::Detected)
            .value("on")
            .file("/sys/thing")
            .command("thing --status")
            .not_an_attestation();
        assert_eq!(finding.value.as_deref(), Some("on"));
        assert_eq!(finding.evidence.len(), 2);
        assert_eq!(finding.evidence[0].kind, EvidenceKind::File);
        assert_eq!(finding.notes, vec![ATTESTATION_DISCLAIMER.to_string()]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn builder_neutralises_control_characters() {
        let finding = Finding::new("a.b", Category::Gaming, "Proton", Status::Detected)
            .value("evil\x1b[2J")
            .file("/home/u/\x1b[31mdir")
            .command("tool\n--flag")
            .derived("x\ry")
            .note("line\nforged line");
        let all = [
            finding.value.clone().unwrap_or_default(),
            finding.evidence[0].source.clone(),
            finding.evidence[1].source.clone(),
            finding.evidence[2].source.clone(),
            finding.notes[0].clone(),
        ];
        for text in all {
            assert!(!text.chars().any(char::is_control), "{text:?}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_fields_are_omitted_from_json() {
        let finding = Finding::new("a.b", Category::System, "Thing", Status::NotApplicable);
        let json = serde_json::to_value(&finding).expect("serialise");
        assert!(json.get("value").is_none());
        assert!(json.get("evidence").is_none());
        assert!(json.get("notes").is_none());
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/paths.rs`:

```rs
//! Per-user directories, following the XDG Base Directory specification.

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use crate::error::CoreError;
use crate::APP_DIR;

/// Where Tuxgate keeps its files for the current user.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Paths {
    /// Configuration, normally `~/.config/tuxgate`.
    pub config_dir: PathBuf,
    /// Data such as the local test history, normally `~/.local/share/tuxgate`.
    pub data_dir: PathBuf,
}

fn absolute(value: Option<String>) -> Option<PathBuf> {
    // The specification says relative paths must be ignored.
    value
        .filter(|v| !v.is_empty())
        .map(PathBuf::from)
        .filter(|p| p.is_absolute())
}

impl Paths {
    /// Computes the directories from an environment lookup function.
    pub fn from_env(env: impl Fn(&str) -> Option<String>) -> Result<Self, CoreError> {
        let home = absolute(env("HOME")).or_else(|| absolute(env("USERPROFILE")));
        let config_base = absolute(env("XDG_CONFIG_HOME"))
            .or_else(|| home.as_ref().map(|h| h.join(".config")))
            .ok_or(CoreError::NoHome)?;
        let data_base = absolute(env("XDG_DATA_HOME"))
            .or_else(|| home.as_ref().map(|h| h.join(".local").join("share")))
            .ok_or(CoreError::NoHome)?;
        Ok(Self {
            config_dir: config_base.join(APP_DIR),
            data_dir: data_base.join(APP_DIR),
        })
    }

    /// Computes the directories from the process environment.
    pub fn from_process_env() -> Result<Self, CoreError> {
        Self::from_env(|key| std::env::var(key).ok())
    }

    /// The configuration file.
    pub fn config_file(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.config_dir.join("config.toml")
    }

    /// The local test history.
    pub fn history_file(&self) -> PathBuf {
        self.data_dir.join("history.toml")
    }
}

/// Creates a directory and its parents.
pub fn ensure_dir(path: &Path) -> Result<(), CoreError> {
    std::fs::create_dir_all(path).map_err(|source| CoreError::Io {
        path: path.to_path_buf(),
        source,
    })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use std::collections::HashMap;

    fn lookup(pairs: &[(&str, &str)]) -> impl Fn(&str) -> Option<String> {
        let map: HashMap<String, String> = pairs
            .iter()
            .map(|(k, v)| ((*k).to_string(), (*v).to_string()))
            .collect();
        move |key| map.get(key).cloned()
    }

    // An absolute path on the platform running the tests.
    fn abs(tail: &str) -> String {
        if cfg!(windows) {
            format!("C:\\{}", tail.replace('/', "\\"))
        } else {
            format!("/{tail}")
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn falls_back_to_home() {
        let home = abs("home/ada");
        let paths = Paths::from_env(lookup(&[("HOME", &home)])).expect("paths");
        assert_eq!(
            paths.config_dir,
            PathBuf::from(&home).join(".config").join("tuxgate")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.data_dir,
            PathBuf::from(&home)
                .join(".local")
                .join("share")
                .join("tuxgate")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn honours_xdg_variables() {
        let home = abs("home/ada");
        let config = abs("cfg");
        let data = abs("data");
        let paths = Paths::from_env(lookup(&[
            ("HOME", &home),
            ("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", &config),
            ("XDG_DATA_HOME", &data),
        ]))
        .expect("paths");
        assert_eq!(
            paths.config_file(),
            PathBuf::from(&config).join("tuxgate").join("config.toml")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            paths.history_file(),
            PathBuf::from(&data).join("tuxgate").join("history.toml")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn ignores_relative_xdg_variables() {
        let home = abs("home/ada");
        let paths = Paths::from_env(lookup(&[
            ("HOME", &home),
            ("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "relative/dir"),
        ]))
        .expect("paths");
        assert_eq!(
            paths.config_dir,
            PathBuf::from(&home).join(".config").join("tuxgate")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn fails_without_home() {
        assert!(matches!(
            Paths::from_env(lookup(&[])),
            Err(CoreError::NoHome)
        ));
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/probe.rs`:

```rs
//! The probe interface and its registry.
//!
//! A probe inspects one aspect of the system and returns findings. Probes
//! are read-only by contract: they may read files and run query commands,
//! never change anything.

use crate::context::SystemContext;
use crate::error::CoreError;
use crate::finding::Finding;

/// One diagnostic module.
pub trait Probe: Send + Sync {
    /// Stable identifier, used in configuration to disable the probe.
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str;

    /// One-line description of what the probe looks at.
    fn description(&self) -> &'static str;

    /// Inspects the system. Must not fail: anything that cannot be
    /// determined is reported as an `Unverifiable` finding.
    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding>;
}

/// An ordered set of probes.
#[derive(Default)]
pub struct ProbeRegistry {
    probes: Vec<Box<dyn Probe>>,
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for ProbeRegistry {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.debug_struct("ProbeRegistry")
            .field("probes", &self.ids())
            .finish()
    }
}

impl ProbeRegistry {
    /// Creates an empty registry.
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }

    /// Adds a probe. Identifiers must be unique.
    pub fn register(&mut self, probe: Box<dyn Probe>) -> Result<(), CoreError> {
        if self.probes.iter().any(|p| p.id() == probe.id()) {
            return Err(CoreError::DuplicateProbe(probe.id().to_string()));
        }
        self.probes.push(probe);
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Identifiers of the registered probes, in execution order.
    pub fn ids(&self) -> Vec<&'static str> {
        self.probes.iter().map(|p| p.id()).collect()
    }

    /// Identifier and description of each probe.
    pub fn describe(&self) -> Vec<(&'static str, &'static str)> {
        self.probes
            .iter()
            .map(|p| (p.id(), p.description()))
            .collect()
    }

    /// Checks that every name in `disabled` is a registered probe.
    pub fn validate_disabled(&self, disabled: &[String]) -> Result<(), CoreError> {
        let ids = self.ids();
        match disabled.iter().find(|name| !ids.contains(&name.as_str())) {
            Some(name) => Err(CoreError::UnknownProbe {
                name: name.clone(),
                known: ids.join(", "),
            }),
            None => Ok(()),
        }
    }

    /// Runs every probe not listed in `disabled` and collects the findings.
    pub fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext, disabled: &[String]) -> Vec<Finding> {
        self.probes
            .iter()
            .filter(|p| !disabled.iter().any(|d| d == p.id()))
            .flat_map(|p| p.run(ctx))
            .collect()
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::finding::{Category, Status};
    use crate::testing::Fixture;

    struct Fixed(&'static str);

    impl Probe for Fixed {
        fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
            self.0
        }
        fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
            "test probe"
        }
        fn run(&self, _: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
            vec![Finding::new(
                format!("{}.thing", self.0),
                Category::System,
                "Thing",
                Status::Detected,
            )]
        }
    }

    fn registry() -> ProbeRegistry {
        let mut registry = ProbeRegistry::new();
        registry.register(Box::new(Fixed("alpha"))).expect("alpha");
        registry.register(Box::new(Fixed("beta"))).expect("beta");
        registry
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_duplicate_identifiers() {
        let mut registry = registry();
        assert!(matches!(
            registry.register(Box::new(Fixed("alpha"))),
            Err(CoreError::DuplicateProbe(name)) if name == "alpha"
        ));
    }

    #[test]
    fn runs_probes_in_order() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let findings = registry().run(&fixture.context(), &[]);
        let ids: Vec<_> = findings.iter().map(|f| f.id.as_str()).collect();
        assert_eq!(ids, ["alpha.thing", "beta.thing"]);
    }

    #[test]
    fn skips_disabled_probes() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let findings = registry().run(&fixture.context(), &["alpha".to_string()]);
        assert_eq!(findings.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(findings[0].id, "beta.thing");
    }

    #[test]
    fn rejects_unknown_disabled_names() {
        let registry = registry();
        assert!(registry.validate_disabled(&["beta".to_string()]).is_ok());
        let error = registry
            .validate_disabled(&["gamma".to_string()])
            .expect_err("unknown");
        assert!(error.to_string().contains("alpha, beta"));
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/testing.rs`:

```rs
//! Test support: throw-away system trees, a scripted command runner and
//! ready-made scenarios. Compiled only for tests or with the `testing`
//! feature; never part of the released binary.

use std::collections::{BTreeMap, HashMap};
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicU64, Ordering};
use std::sync::{Arc, Mutex};
use std::time::Duration;

use crate::context::{
    resolve_under, CommandError, CommandOutput, CommandRunner, NullRunner, SystemContext,
};
use crate::efi::{var_path, EFI_GLOBAL_GUID, LOADER_GUID};

static COUNTER: AtomicU64 = AtomicU64::new(0);

/// A temporary directory laid out like a Linux root. Removed on drop.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Fixture {
    root: PathBuf,
}

impl Default for Fixture {
    fn default() -> Self {
        Self::new()
    }
}

impl Fixture {
    /// Creates an empty tree.
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        let unique = format!(
            "tuxgate-fixture-{}-{}-{}",
            std::process::id(),
            COUNTER.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed),
            std::time::SystemTime::now()
                .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
                .map(|d| d.subsec_nanos())
                .unwrap_or(0)
        );
        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(unique);
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).expect("create fixture root");
        Self { root }
    }

    /// Writes a text file at an absolute path of the simulated system.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn file(self, absolute: &str, content: &str) -> Self {
        self.bytes(absolute, content.as_bytes())
    }

    /// Writes a binary file.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn bytes(self, absolute: &str, content: &[u8]) -> Self {
        let path = resolve_under(&self.root, absolute);
        if let Some(parent) = path.parent() {
            std::fs::create_dir_all(parent).expect("create fixture directory");
        }
        std::fs::write(&path, content).expect("write fixture file");
        self
    }

    /// Creates an empty directory.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn dir(self, absolute: &str) -> Self {
        std::fs::create_dir_all(resolve_under(&self.root, absolute))
            .expect("create fixture directory");
        self
    }

    /// The real directory backing the tree.
    pub fn root(&self) -> &Path {
        &self.root
    }

    /// A context over this tree that refuses every command and has an empty
    /// environment.
    pub fn context(&self) -> SystemContext {
        SystemContext::new(&self.root, Box::new(NullRunner), BTreeMap::new())
    }

    /// A context over this tree with a scripted runner and environment.
    pub fn context_with(&self, runner: MockRunner, env: &[(&str, &str)]) -> SystemContext {
        let env = env
            .iter()
            .map(|(k, v)| ((*k).to_string(), (*v).to_string()))
            .collect();
        SystemContext::new(&self.root, Box::new(runner), env)
    }
}

impl Drop for Fixture {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.root);
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Default)]
struct MockState {
    captured: HashMap<String, Result<CommandOutput, CommandError>>,
    interactive: HashMap<String, Result<Option<i32>, CommandError>>,
    calls: Vec<String>,
}

/// A command runner that answers from a script and records what was asked.
/// Unscripted commands behave as if the program were not installed.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone)]
pub struct MockRunner {
    state: Arc<Mutex<MockState>>,
}

fn command_line(program: &str, args: &[&str]) -> String {
    std::iter::once(program)
        .chain(args.iter().copied())
        .collect::<Vec<_>>()
        .join(" ")
}

impl MockRunner {
    /// Creates a runner that knows no command.
    pub fn new() -> Self {
        Self::default()
    }

    fn lock(&self) -> std::sync::MutexGuard<'_, MockState> {
        self.state.lock().expect("mock runner lock")
    }

    /// Scripts a successful command with the given standard output.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn on(self, line: &str, stdout: &str) -> Self {
        self.on_exit(line, 0, stdout, "")
    }

    /// Scripts a command with an explicit exit code and outputs.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn on_exit(self, line: &str, code: i32, stdout: &str, stderr: &str) -> Self {
        self.lock().captured.insert(
            line.to_string(),
            Ok(CommandOutput {
                code: Some(code),
                stdout: stdout.to_string(),
                stderr: stderr.to_string(),
            }),
        );
        self
    }

    /// Scripts a command that cannot be run.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn on_error(self, line: &str, error: CommandError) -> Self {
        self.lock().captured.insert(line.to_string(), Err(error));
        self
    }

    /// Scripts the exit code of an interactive command.
    #[must_use]
    pub fn on_interactive(self, line: &str, code: i32) -> Self {
        self.lock()
            .interactive
            .insert(line.to_string(), Ok(Some(code)));
        self
    }

    /// Every command line requested so far, in order. Interactive commands
    /// are prefixed with `interactive: `.
    pub fn calls(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        self.lock().calls.clone()
    }
}

impl CommandRunner for MockRunner {
    fn run(
        &self,
        program: &str,
        args: &[&str],
        _: Duration,
    ) -> Result<CommandOutput, CommandError> {
        let line = command_line(program, args);
        let mut state = self.lock();
        state.calls.push(line.clone());
        state
            .captured
            .get(&line)
            .cloned()
            .unwrap_or(Err(CommandError::NotFound))
    }

    fn run_interactive(&self, program: &str, args: &[&str]) -> Result<Option<i32>, CommandError> {
        let line = command_line(program, args);
        let mut state = self.lock();
        state.calls.push(format!("interactive: {line}"));
        state
            .interactive
            .get(&line)
            .cloned()
            .unwrap_or(Err(CommandError::NotFound))
    }
}

/// Standard non-volatile, boot-service, runtime attribute prefix.
const EFI_ATTRIBUTES: [u8; 4] = [0x07, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00];

fn utf16le(text: &str) -> Vec<u8> {
    text.encode_utf16()
        .chain(std::iter::once(0))
        .flat_map(u16::to_le_bytes)
        .collect()
}

/// Contents of an efivarfs file holding one byte.
pub fn efi_var_u8(value: u8) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut bytes = EFI_ATTRIBUTES.to_vec();
    bytes.push(value);
    bytes
}

/// Contents of an efivarfs file holding a NUL-terminated UTF-16 string.
pub fn efi_var_utf16(text: &str) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut bytes = EFI_ATTRIBUTES.to_vec();
    bytes.extend(utf16le(text));
    bytes
}

/// Contents of an efivarfs file holding a list of 16-bit values, as used by
/// `BootOrder`, `BootCurrent` and `BootNext`.
pub fn efi_var_u16_list(values: &[u16]) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut bytes = EFI_ATTRIBUTES.to_vec();
    bytes.extend(values.iter().flat_map(|v| v.to_le_bytes()));
    bytes
}

/// Contents of an efivarfs `Boot####` file: an `EFI_LOAD_OPTION` with a
/// description and, optionally, a file path device node.
pub fn efi_load_option(description: &str, file_path: Option<&str>) -> Vec<u8> {
    let mut device_path = Vec::new();
    if let Some(path) = file_path {
        let data = utf16le(path);
        let length = u16::try_from(data.len() + 4).expect("device path node length");
        device_path.extend([0x04, 0x04]);
        device_path.extend(length.to_le_bytes());
        device_path.extend(data);
    }
    device_path.extend([0x7f, 0xff, 0x04, 0x00]);

    let mut bytes = EFI_ATTRIBUTES.to_vec();
    bytes.extend(1u32.to_le_bytes()); // LOAD_OPTION_ACTIVE
    bytes.extend(
        u16::try_from(device_path.len())
            .expect("device path length")
            .to_le_bytes(),
    );
    bytes.extend(utf16le(description));
    bytes.extend(device_path);
    bytes
}

/// A complete simulated machine: files, command answers and environment.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Scenario {
    pub fixture: Fixture,
    pub runner: MockRunner,
    pub env: Vec<(&'static str, &'static str)>,
}

impl Scenario {
    /// A context over the scenario.
    pub fn context(&self) -> SystemContext {
        self.fixture.context_with(self.runner.clone(), &self.env)
    }
}

    const INTEL_CPUINFO: &str = "processor\t: 0\nvendor_id\t: GenuineIntel\nmodel name\t: Intel(R) Core(TM) i5-12400F\nflags\t\t: fpu vme de pse tsc msr pae mce cx8 apic sep mtrr lm vmx sse4_2 avx2\n";

    /// `bootctl list --json=short` on a systemd-boot machine that also has
    /// Windows.
    pub const BOOTCTL_LIST_WITH_WINDOWS: &str = r#"[{"type":"type1","id":"linux-cachyos.conf","title":"CachyOS Linux","isDefault":true,"isSelected":true},{"type":"auto","id":"auto-windows","title":"Windows Boot Manager","isDefault":false,"isSelected":false},{"type":"auto","id":"auto-reboot-to-firmware-setup","title":"Reboot Into Firmware Interface","isDefault":false,"isSelected":false}]"#;

    fn pci_device(
        fixture: Fixture,
        slot: &str,
        class: &str,
        vendor: &str,
        device: &str,
        driver: Option<&str>,
    ) -> Fixture {
        let base = format!("/sys/bus/pci/devices/{slot}");
        let mut uevent = String::new();
        if let Some(driver) = driver {
            uevent.push_str(&format!("DRIVER={driver}\n"));
        }
        uevent.push_str(&format!("PCI_SLOT_NAME={slot}\n"));
        fixture
            .file(&format!("{base}/class"), &format!("{class}\n"))
            .file(&format!("{base}/vendor"), &format!("{vendor}\n"))
            .file(&format!("{base}/device"), &format!("{device}\n"))
            .file(&format!("{base}/uevent"), &uevent)
    }

    fn secure_boot(fixture: Fixture, enabled: bool) -> Fixture {
        fixture
            .bytes(
                &var_path("SecureBoot", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID),
                &efi_var_u8(u8::from(enabled)),
            )
            .bytes(&var_path("SetupMode", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID), &efi_var_u8(0))
    }

    fn iommu_groups(mut fixture: Fixture, count: usize) -> Fixture {
        for index in 0..count {
            fixture = fixture.dir(&format!("/sys/kernel/iommu_groups/{index}"));
        }
        fixture.dir("/sys/class/iommu/ivhd0")
    }

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn fixture_is_removed_on_drop() {
        let root = {
            let fixture = Fixture::new().file("/etc/hostname", "box\n");
            assert!(fixture.root().join("etc").join("hostname").is_file());
            fixture.root().to_path_buf()
        };
        assert!(!root.exists());
    }

    #[test]
    fn mock_runner_answers_and_records() {
        let runner = MockRunner::new()
            .on("wine --version", "wine-10.0\n")
            .on_interactive("systemctl reboot", 0);
        let output = runner
            .run("wine", &["--version"], Duration::from_secs(1))
            .expect("scripted");
        assert!(output.success());
        assert_eq!(output.stdout, "wine-10.0\n");
        assert_eq!(
            runner.run("missing", &[], Duration::from_secs(1)),
            Err(CommandError::NotFound)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            runner.run_interactive("systemctl", &["reboot"]),
            Ok(Some(0))
        );
        assert_eq!(
            runner.calls(),
            ["wine --version", "missing", "interactive: systemctl reboot"]
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn load_option_layout_is_well_formed() {
        let raw = efi_load_option(
            "Windows Boot Manager",
            Some("\\EFI\\Microsoft\\Boot\\bootmgfw.efi"),
        );
        // 4 attribute bytes, then the load option attributes and path length.
        assert_eq!(&raw[4..8], &1u32.to_le_bytes());
        let path_length = usize::from(u16::from_le_bytes([raw[8], raw[9]]));
        // The device path list sits at the very end.
        assert_eq!(&raw[raw.len() - 4..], &[0x7f, 0xff, 0x04, 0x00]);
        assert!(path_length > 4 && path_length < raw.len());
    }

    #[test]
    fn scenarios_build() {
        for scenario in [
            scenarios::cachyos_desktop(),
            scenarios::cachyos_attested(),
            scenarios::arch_minimal(),
            scenarios::vm_guest(),
        ] {
            assert!(scenario.context().exists("/etc/os-release"));
        }
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/text.rs`:

```rs
//! Neutralising untrusted text before it is displayed.
//!
//! Tuxgate prints strings that come from the system: file contents, firmware
//! boot entry names, directory names, command output. Some of those can be
//! set by other software or by another user of a shared report. A string
//! containing terminal escape sequences could recolour, hide or rewrite
//! parts of the output. Every such string passes through [`sanitize`].

/// Replaces control characters with U+FFFD.
///
/// Removes C0 and C1 controls (including ESC, which starts terminal escape
/// sequences, and line breaks, which could forge extra output lines), plus
/// the Unicode bidirectional overrides that can visually reorder text.
pub fn sanitize(text: &str) -> String {
    if !text.chars().any(is_unsafe) {
        return text.to_string();
    }
    text.chars()
        .map(|c| if is_unsafe(c) { '\u{FFFD}' } else { c })
        .collect()
}

fn is_unsafe(c: char) -> bool {
    c.is_control()
        || matches!(
            c,
            '\u{202A}'..='\u{202E}' | '\u{2066}'..='\u{2069}' | '\u{200E}' | '\u{200F}'
        )
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn ordinary_text_is_unchanged() {
        for text in ["", "CachyOS (rolling)", "Détecté — 日本語 ✓", "a\u{00A0}b"] {
            assert_eq!(sanitize(text), text);
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn escape_sequences_are_neutralised() {
        let hostile = "Proton\x1b[2J\x1b[31m 10.0";
        let cleaned = sanitize(hostile);
        assert!(!cleaned.contains('\x1b'));
        assert_eq!(cleaned, "Proton\u{FFFD}[2J\u{FFFD}[31m 10.0");
    }

    #[test]
    fn line_breaks_cannot_forge_output_lines() {
        let cleaned = sanitize("real\n  [   detected   ] Secure Boot: enabled\r");
        assert!(!cleaned.contains('\n'));
        assert!(!cleaned.contains('\r'));
    }

    #[test]
    fn bidirectional_overrides_are_neutralised() {
        let cleaned = sanitize("safe\u{202E}gnp.exe");
        assert!(!cleaned.contains('\u{202E}'));
        assert!(sanitize("a\u{9B}b").contains('\u{FFFD}'));
    }
}

```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/time.rs`:

```rs
//! Minimal UTC date formatting, to avoid a date-time dependency.

use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};

/// A UTC calendar date and time.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct UtcDateTime {
    pub year: i64,
    pub month: u32,
    pub day: u32,
    pub hour: u32,
    pub minute: u32,
    pub second: u32,
}

impl UtcDateTime {
    /// Converts seconds since the Unix epoch.
    pub fn from_unix(seconds: u64) -> Self {
        let days = i64::try_from(seconds / 86_400).unwrap_or(i64::MAX / 2);
        let rest = seconds % 86_400;

        // Days-to-civil conversion (Howard Hinnant's algorithm), valid for
        // the whole proleptic Gregorian calendar.
        let z = days + 719_468;
        let era = z.div_euclid(146_097);
        let day_of_era = z.rem_euclid(146_097);
        let year_of_era =
            (day_of_era - day_of_era / 1_460 + day_of_era / 36_524 - day_of_era / 146_096) / 365;
        let day_of_year = day_of_era - (365 * year_of_era + year_of_era / 4 - year_of_era / 100);
        let month_index = (5 * day_of_year + 2) / 153;
        let day = day_of_year - (153 * month_index + 2) / 5 + 1;
        let month = if month_index < 10 {
            month_index + 3
        } else {
            month_index - 9
        };
        let year = year_of_era + era * 400 + i64::from(month <= 2);

        Self {
            year,
            month: month as u32,
            day: day as u32,
            hour: (rest / 3_600) as u32,
            minute: (rest % 3_600 / 60) as u32,
            second: (rest % 60) as u32,
        }
    }

    /// The current time.
    pub fn now() -> Self {
        let seconds = SystemTime::now()
            .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)
            .map(|d| d.as_secs())
            .unwrap_or(0);
        Self::from_unix(seconds)
    }

    /// `YYYY-MM-DD`.
    pub fn date(&self) -> String {
        format!("{:04}-{:02}-{:02}", self.year, self.month, self.day)
    }

    /// `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SSZ`.
    pub fn iso8601(&self) -> String {
        format!(
            "{}T{:02}:{:02}:{:02}Z",
            self.date(),
            self.hour,
            self.minute,
            self.second
        )
    }
}

/// True when `text` looks like `YYYY-MM-DD` with plausible month and day.
pub fn is_iso_date(text: &str) -> bool {
    let bytes = text.as_bytes();
    if bytes.len() != 10 || bytes[4] != b'-' || bytes[7] != b'-' {
        return false;
    }
    let digits = |range: std::ops::Range<usize>| -> Option<u32> {
        text.get(range)
            .filter(|s| s.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit()))
            .and_then(|s| s.parse().ok())
    };
    matches!(
        (digits(0..4), digits(5..7), digits(8..10)),
        (Some(_), Some(1..=12), Some(1..=31))
    )
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn converts_the_epoch() {
        assert_eq!(UtcDateTime::from_unix(0).iso8601(), "1970-01-01T00:00:00Z");
    }

    #[test]
    fn converts_known_timestamps() {
        // 2000-02-29 is a leap day in a century year divisible by 400.
        assert_eq!(UtcDateTime::from_unix(951_782_400).date(), "2000-02-29");
        assert_eq!(
            UtcDateTime::from_unix(1_700_000_000).iso8601(),
            "2023-11-14T22:13:20Z"
        );
        assert_eq!(
            UtcDateTime::from_unix(1_795_824_000).iso8601(),
            "2026-11-28T00:00:00Z"
        );
        // Last second of a year, first second of the next.
        assert_eq!(
            UtcDateTime::from_unix(1_798_761_599).iso8601(),
            "2026-12-31T23:59:59Z"
        );
        assert_eq!(UtcDateTime::from_unix(1_798_761_600).date(), "2027-01-01");
    }

    #[test]
    fn validates_iso_dates() {
        assert!(is_iso_date("2026-11-28"));
        assert!(!is_iso_date("2026-13-01"));
        assert!(!is_iso_date("2026-11-00"));
        assert!(!is_iso_date("2026-1-28"));
        assert!(!is_iso_date("28/11/2026"));
        assert!(!is_iso_date("2026-11-2x"));
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-core/src/tools.rs`:

```rs
//! External programs Tuxgate can use when they are installed.
//!
//! None of them is required: every feature degrades to an `Unverifiable`
//! finding or an explanation when its tool is missing. The list is the
//! single source of truth for the diagnostics, the documentation and the
//! package's optional dependencies.

/// A program Tuxgate may call.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ExternalTool {
    /// Executable name.
    pub program: &'static str,
    /// Arch Linux package providing it.
    pub package: &'static str,
    /// What Tuxgate uses it for.
    pub purpose: &'static str,
    /// True when the tool is only used after an explicit user confirmation
    /// and may request elevated privileges.
    pub privileged_use: bool,
}

/// Every external program Tuxgate knows how to use.
pub const EXTERNAL_TOOLS: &[ExternalTool] = &[
    ExternalTool {
        program: "bootctl",
        package: "systemd",
        purpose: "list boot loader entries",
        privileged_use: false,
    },
    ExternalTool {
        program: "systemctl",
        package: "systemd",
        purpose: "reboot into a chosen boot entry, after confirmation",
        privileged_use: true,
    },
    ExternalTool {
        program: "efibootmgr",
        package: "efibootmgr",
        purpose: "select Windows for the next boot only, after confirmation",
        privileged_use: true,
    },
    ExternalTool {
        program: "pkexec",
        package: "polkit",
        purpose: "ask for administrator rights for the single efibootmgr call",
        privileged_use: true,
    },
    ExternalTool {
        program: "lsblk",
        package: "util-linux",
        purpose: "look for Windows partitions",
        privileged_use: false,
    },
    ExternalTool {
        program: "vulkaninfo",
        package: "vulkan-tools",
        purpose: "confirm that a Vulkan driver actually loads",
        privileged_use: false,
    },
    ExternalTool {
        program: "wine",
        package: "wine",
        purpose: "report the installed Wine version",
        privileged_use: false,
    },
    ExternalTool {
        program: "systemd-detect-virt",
        package: "systemd",
        purpose: "identify a hypervisor or container",
        privileged_use: false,
    },
];

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn programs_are_unique() {
        let mut names: Vec<_> = EXTERNAL_TOOLS.iter().map(|t| t.program).collect();
        names.sort_unstable();
        let before = names.len();
        names.dedup();
        assert_eq!(names.len(), before);
    }

    #[test]
    fn privileged_tools_say_they_need_confirmation() {
        for tool in EXTERNAL_TOOLS.iter().filter(|t| t.privileged_use) {
            assert!(
                tool.purpose.contains("confirmation") || tool.program == "pkexec",
                "{} must state that it runs only after confirmation",
                tool.program
            );
        }
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-diagnostics/src/boot.rs`:

```rs
//! The boot manager.

use tuxgate_core::efi::{self, LOADER_GUID};
use tuxgate_core::{Category, Finding, FsError, Probe, Status, SystemContext};

/// Boot managers Tuxgate can recognise from their files.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum BootManager {
    SystemdBoot,
    Limine,
    Grub,
    Refind,
}

impl BootManager {
    /// Display name.
    pub fn name(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            BootManager::SystemdBoot => "systemd-boot",
            BootManager::Limine => "Limine",
            BootManager::Grub => "GRUB",
            BootManager::Refind => "rEFInd",
        }
    }

    /// Files whose presence indicates the boot manager is installed.
    fn markers(self) -> &'static [&'static str] {
        match self {
            BootManager::SystemdBoot => &[
                "/boot/loader/loader.conf",
                "/efi/loader/loader.conf",
                "/boot/efi/loader/loader.conf",
            ],
            BootManager::Limine => &[
                "/boot/limine.conf",
                "/boot/limine/limine.conf",
                "/boot/EFI/limine/limine.conf",
                "/boot/EFI/BOOT/limine.conf",
                "/etc/default/limine",
            ],
            BootManager::Grub => &["/boot/grub/grub.cfg", "/boot/grub2/grub.cfg"],
            BootManager::Refind => &[
                "/boot/EFI/refind/refind.conf",
                "/boot/efi/EFI/refind/refind.conf",
                "/efi/EFI/refind/refind.conf",
            ],
        }
    }

    const ALL: [BootManager; 4] = [
        BootManager::SystemdBoot,
        BootManager::Limine,
        BootManager::Grub,
        BootManager::Refind,
    ];
}

/// The description the running boot loader left in the `LoaderInfo` EFI
/// variable, for loaders that implement the systemd Boot Loader Interface.
pub fn loader_info(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Option<String> {
    efi::read_var(ctx, "LoaderInfo", LOADER_GUID)
        .ok()
        .map(|data| efi::utf16le_to_string(&data))
        .filter(|text| !text.is_empty())
}

/// Boot managers whose configuration files are present.
pub fn installed_boot_managers(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<(BootManager, &'static str)> {
    BootManager::ALL
        .iter()
        .filter_map(|manager| {
            manager
                .markers()
                .iter()
                .find(|path| ctx.is_file(path))
                .map(|path| (*manager, *path))
        })
        .collect()
}

const MEASUREMENT_NOTE: &str = "According to the CachyOS wiki, systemd-boot and GRUB record \
     TPM boot measurements, while Limine and rEFInd do not produce measurements that verify.";

/// Reports which boot manager started the system.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct BootManagerProbe;

impl Probe for BootManagerProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "boot_manager"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Boot manager in use, from EFI variables and configuration files"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        const ID: &str = "boot.manager";
        const TITLE: &str = "Boot manager";

        if let Some(info) = loader_info(ctx) {
            return vec![Finding::new(ID, Category::Boot, TITLE, Status::Detected)
                .value(info)
                .file(efi::var_path("LoaderInfo", LOADER_GUID))
                .note("Reported by the boot loader that started this session.")
                .note(MEASUREMENT_NOTE)];
        }

        let installed = installed_boot_managers(ctx);
        if !installed.is_empty() {
            let names: Vec<_> = installed.iter().map(|(m, _)| m.name()).collect();
            let mut finding =
                Finding::new(ID, Category::Boot, TITLE, Status::Detected).value(names.join(", "));
            for (_, path) in &installed {
                finding = finding.file(*path);
            }
            if installed.len() > 1 {
                finding = finding.note(
                    "Several boot managers have files installed; which one starts the system \
                     could not be determined.",
                );
            }
            return vec![finding
                .note("Identified from configuration files, not from the running session.")
                .note(MEASUREMENT_NOTE)];
        }

        // Nothing found. The ESP is commonly mounted so that only root can
        // list it; say "cannot tell" rather than "absent" in that case.
        let unreadable = ["/boot", "/boot/efi", "/efi"]
            .iter()
            .any(|dir| matches!(ctx.list_dir(dir), Err(FsError::PermissionDenied)));
        let has_boot_dir = ctx.is_dir("/boot") || ctx.is_dir("/efi");
        let finding = if unreadable {
            Finding::new(ID, Category::Boot, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note(
                "The boot partition is not readable without administrator rights on this \
                 system, and the boot loader did not identify itself through EFI variables.",
            )
        } else if has_boot_dir {
            Finding::new(ID, Category::Boot, TITLE, Status::NotDetected)
                .note("No known boot manager configuration was found.")
        } else {
            Finding::new(ID, Category::Boot, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                .note("No boot directory is visible.")
        };
        vec![finding.file("/boot")]
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::{scenarios, Fixture};

    #[test]
    fn prefers_the_running_loader_identity() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = BootManagerProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(
            findings[0].value.as_deref(),
            Some("systemd-boot 258.2-1-arch")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn falls_back_to_configuration_files() {
        let scenario = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = BootManagerProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("GRUB"));
        assert!(findings[0]
            .notes
            .iter()
            .any(|n| n.contains("configuration files")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn reports_ambiguity_when_several_are_installed() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new()
            .file("/boot/limine.conf", "timeout: 5\n")
            .file("/boot/grub/grub.cfg", "");
        let findings = BootManagerProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("Limine, GRUB"));
        assert!(findings[0].notes.iter().any(|n| n.contains("Several")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn empty_boot_directory_is_not_detected() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new().dir("/boot");
        let findings = BootManagerProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::NotDetected);
    }

    #[test]
    fn no_boot_directory_is_unverifiable() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let findings = BootManagerProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Unverifiable);
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-diagnostics/src/gaming.rs`:

```rs
//! Wine, Steam and Proton.

use tuxgate_core::{Category, CommandError, Finding, Probe, Status, SystemContext};

/// Appended to every compatibility-layer finding, because a working Wine
/// or Proton is routinely mistaken for a path to Vanguard-protected games.
pub const KERNEL_ANTICHEAT_NOTE: &str = "Wine and Proton cannot run kernel-mode anti-cheat \
     drivers such as Riot Vanguard's, whatever their version.";

/// Reports the system Wine installation.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct WineProbe;

impl Probe for WineProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "wine"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "System Wine installation and version"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        const ID: &str = "gaming.wine";
        const TITLE: &str = "Wine";
        const COMMAND: &str = "wine --version";
        let opt_wine = "/opt/wine-cachyos/bin/wine";

        let mut finding = match ctx.run("wine", &["--version"]) {
            Ok(output) if output.success() && !output.stdout.trim().is_empty() => {
                Finding::new(ID, Category::Gaming, TITLE, Status::Detected)
                    .value(output.stdout.trim())
                    .command(COMMAND)
            }
            Ok(_) => Finding::new(ID, Category::Gaming, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                .command(COMMAND)
                .note("wine is installed but did not report a version."),
            Err(CommandError::NotFound) => {
                Finding::new(ID, Category::Gaming, TITLE, Status::NotDetected).command(COMMAND)
            }
            Err(error) => match ctx.find_program("wine") {
                // The command could not be run (offline tree, timeout) but the
                // program file is there.
                Some(path) => Finding::new(ID, Category::Gaming, TITLE, Status::Detected)
                    .value("installed, version not queried")
                    .file(path)
                    .note(format!("wine --version: {error}")),
                None => Finding::new(ID, Category::Gaming, TITLE, Status::NotDetected)
                    .note(format!("wine --version: {error}")),
            },
        };

        if ctx.is_file(opt_wine) {
            if finding.status == Status::NotDetected {
                finding.status = Status::Detected;
                finding.value = Some("wine-cachyos-opt".to_string());
            }
            finding = finding
                .file(opt_wine)
                .note("wine-cachyos-opt is installed under /opt/wine-cachyos.");
        }
        vec![finding.note(KERNEL_ANTICHEAT_NOTE)]
    }
}

/// A Proton build found on disk.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ProtonBuild {
    pub name: String,
    pub version: Option<String>,
    pub directory: String,
}

fn steam_roots(home: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    vec![
        format!("{home}/.local/share/Steam"),
        format!("{home}/.steam/root"),
        format!("{home}/.var/app/com.valvesoftware.Steam/.local/share/Steam"),
    ]
}

fn read_proton_version(ctx: &SystemContext, directory: &str) -> Option<String> {
    // The file holds "<timestamp> <version>".
    let text = ctx.read_to_string(&format!("{directory}/version")).ok()?;
    text.split_whitespace().last().map(str::to_string)
}

/// Finds Proton builds in the Steam library and in the compatibility tool
/// directories.
pub fn find_proton_builds(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<ProtonBuild> {
    // (directory, keep only entries named "Proton...")
    let mut locations: Vec<(String, bool)> =
        vec![("/usr/share/steam/compatibilitytools.d".to_string(), false)];
    if let Some(home) = ctx.env("HOME") {
        for root in steam_roots(home) {
            locations.push((format!("{root}/steamapps/common"), true));
            locations.push((format!("{root}/compatibilitytools.d"), false));
        }
    }

    let mut builds: Vec<ProtonBuild> = Vec::new();
    for (location, proton_only) in locations {
        let Ok(names) = ctx.list_dir(&location) else {
            continue;
        };
        for name in names {
            if proton_only && !name.starts_with("Proton") {
                continue;
            }
            let directory = format!("{location}/{name}");
            if !ctx.is_dir(&directory) || builds.iter().any(|b| b.name == name) {
                continue;
            }
            builds.push(ProtonBuild {
                version: read_proton_version(ctx, &directory),
                name,
                directory,
            });
        }
    }
    builds
}

/// Reports Steam and the Proton builds available to it.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ProtonProbe;

impl Probe for ProtonProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "proton"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Steam installation and available Proton builds"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        let home = ctx.env("HOME");
        let steam_program = ctx.find_program("steam");
        let steam_root =
            home.and_then(|home| steam_roots(home).into_iter().find(|root| ctx.is_dir(root)));

        let steam = match (&steam_program, &steam_root) {
            (None, None) if home.is_none() => Finding::new(
                "gaming.steam",
                Category::Gaming,
                "Steam",
                Status::Unverifiable,
            )
            .note("HOME is not known, so the user's Steam library could not be searched."),
            (None, None) => Finding::new(
                "gaming.steam",
                Category::Gaming,
                "Steam",
                Status::NotDetected,
            ),
            (program, root) => {
                let mut finding =
                    Finding::new("gaming.steam", Category::Gaming, "Steam", Status::Detected)
                        .value("installed");
                if let Some(program) = program {
                    finding = finding.file(program.clone());
                }
                if let Some(root) = root {
                    finding = finding.file(root.clone());
                }
                finding
            }
        };

        let builds = find_proton_builds(ctx);
        let proton = if builds.is_empty() {
            let status = if home.is_none() {
                Status::Unverifiable
            } else {
                Status::NotDetected
            };
            Finding::new("gaming.proton", Category::Gaming, "Proton", status)
        } else {
            let value = builds
                .iter()
                .map(|b| match &b.version {
                    Some(version) => format!("{} ({version})", b.name),
                    None => b.name.clone(),
                })
                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
                .join(", ");
            let mut finding = Finding::new(
                "gaming.proton",
                Category::Gaming,
                "Proton",
                Status::Detected,
            )
            .value(value);
            for build in &builds {
                finding = finding.file(build.directory.clone());
            }
            finding
        };

        vec![steam, proton.note(KERNEL_ANTICHEAT_NOTE)]
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::finding::find;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::{scenarios, Fixture, MockRunner};

    #[test]
    fn reports_wine_version() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = WineProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("wine-10.19 (Staging)"));
        assert!(findings[0].notes.iter().any(|n| n == KERNEL_ANTICHEAT_NOTE));
    }

    #[test]
    fn missing_wine_is_not_detected() {
        let scenario = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = WineProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::NotDetected);
        // The warning is shown even when Wine is absent.
        assert!(findings[0].notes.iter().any(|n| n == KERNEL_ANTICHEAT_NOTE));
    }

    #[test]
    fn offline_tree_detects_wine_by_file() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new().file("/usr/bin/wine", "");
        let findings = WineProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(
            findings[0].value.as_deref(),
            Some("installed, version not queried")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn detects_wine_cachyos_opt() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new().file("/opt/wine-cachyos/bin/wine", "");
        let findings = WineProbe.run(&fixture.context_with(MockRunner::new(), &[]));
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("wine-cachyos-opt"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn finds_proton_in_library_and_system_directory() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let ctx = scenario.context();
        let builds = find_proton_builds(&ctx);
        let names: Vec<_> = builds.iter().map(|b| b.name.as_str()).collect();
        assert_eq!(names, ["proton-cachyos-slr", "Proton 10.0"]);
        assert_eq!(builds[1].version.as_deref(), Some("proton-10.0-3"));

        let findings = ProtonProbe.run(&ctx);
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "gaming.steam").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::Detected)
        );
        let proton = find(&findings, "gaming.proton").expect("proton");
        assert_eq!(
            proton.value.as_deref(),
            Some("proton-cachyos-slr (proton-cachyos-10.0-20261120-slr), Proton 10.0 (proton-10.0-3)")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn ignores_games_in_the_steam_library() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new()
            .dir("/home/u/.local/share/Steam/steamapps/common/Some Game")
            .dir("/home/u/.local/share/Steam/steamapps/common/Proton - Experimental");
        let ctx = fixture.context_with(MockRunner::new(), &[("HOME", "/home/u")]);
        let builds = find_proton_builds(&ctx);
        assert_eq!(builds.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(builds[0].name, "Proton - Experimental");
        assert_eq!(builds[0].version, None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn nothing_installed_is_not_detected() {
        let scenario = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = ProtonProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "gaming.steam").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "gaming.proton").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn unknown_home_is_unverifiable() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let findings = ProtonProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "gaming.steam").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::Unverifiable)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "gaming.proton").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::Unverifiable)
        );
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-diagnostics/src/graphics.rs`:

```rs
//! Graphics adapters, their kernel drivers and the Vulkan stack.

use tuxgate_core::{Category, CommandError, Finding, FsError, Probe, Status, SystemContext};

use crate::parse;

const PCI_DEVICES: &str = "/sys/bus/pci/devices";
const NVIDIA_VENDOR: u32 = 0x10de;

/// Names a PCI vendor commonly seen on display adapters.
pub fn vendor_name(vendor: u32) -> &'static str {
    match vendor {
        0x10de => "NVIDIA",
        0x1002 => "AMD",
        0x8086 => "Intel",
        0x1af4 => "virtio (virtual)",
        0x15ad => "VMware (virtual)",
        0x1234 => "QEMU (virtual)",
        0x80ee => "VirtualBox (virtual)",
        0x1414 => "Hyper-V (virtual)",
        0x1b36 => "QXL (virtual)",
        _ => "unknown vendor",
    }
}

/// A display adapter found on the PCI bus.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Gpu {
    pub slot: String,
    pub vendor: u32,
    pub device: u32,
    pub driver: Option<String>,
}

/// Lists display-class PCI devices (base class 0x03).
pub fn list_gpus(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Result<Vec<Gpu>, FsError> {
    let mut gpus = Vec::new();
    for slot in ctx.list_dir(PCI_DEVICES)? {
        let base = format!("{PCI_DEVICES}/{slot}");
        let class = ctx
            .read_to_string(&format!("{base}/class"))
            .ok()
            .and_then(|text| parse::hex_id(&text));
        if class.map(|c| c >> 16) != Some(0x03) {
            continue;
        }
        let read_id = |name: &str| {
            ctx.read_to_string(&format!("{base}/{name}"))
                .ok()
                .and_then(|text| parse::hex_id(&text))
                .unwrap_or(0)
        };
        let driver = ctx
            .read_to_string(&format!("{base}/uevent"))
            .ok()
            .and_then(|text| parse::key_values(&text).remove("DRIVER"))
            .filter(|d| !d.is_empty());
        gpus.push(Gpu {
            vendor: read_id("vendor"),
            device: read_id("device"),
            driver,
            slot,
        });
    }
    Ok(gpus)
}

/// Reports graphics adapters and the NVIDIA driver version.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct GpuProbe;

impl Probe for GpuProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "gpu"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Graphics adapters and the kernel drivers bound to them, from sysfs"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        let gpus = match list_gpus(ctx) {
            Ok(gpus) => gpus,
            Err(error) => {
                return vec![Finding::new(
                    "graphics.gpu",
                    Category::Graphics,
                    "Graphics adapter",
                    Status::Unverifiable,
                )
                .file(PCI_DEVICES)
                .note(format!("{PCI_DEVICES}: {error}"))];
            }
        };

        if gpus.is_empty() {
            return vec![Finding::new(
                "graphics.gpu",
                Category::Graphics,
                "Graphics adapter",
                Status::NotDetected,
            )
            .file(PCI_DEVICES)
            .note("No display-class PCI device was found.")];
        }

        let mut findings: Vec<Finding> = gpus
            .iter()
            .enumerate()
            .map(|(index, gpu)| {
                let driver = gpu.driver.as_deref().unwrap_or("none");
                let mut finding = Finding::new(
                    format!("graphics.gpu.{index}"),
                    Category::Graphics,
                    "Graphics adapter",
                    Status::Detected,
                )
                .value(format!(
                    "{} [{:04x}:{:04x}], driver: {driver}",
                    vendor_name(gpu.vendor),
                    gpu.vendor,
                    gpu.device
                ))
                .file(format!("{PCI_DEVICES}/{}", gpu.slot));
                if gpu.driver.is_none() {
                    finding = finding.note("No kernel driver is bound to this adapter.");
                }
                if gpu.driver.as_deref() == Some("nouveau") {
                    finding = finding.note(
                        "The nouveau driver is in use. Games generally need the NVIDIA driver \
                         (nvidia-open or nvidia packages).",
                    );
                }
                finding
            })
            .collect();

        if gpus.iter().any(|g| g.vendor == NVIDIA_VENDOR) {
            findings.push(nvidia_driver(ctx, &gpus));
        }
        findings
    }
}

fn nvidia_driver(ctx: &SystemContext, gpus: &[Gpu]) -> Finding {
    const ID: &str = "graphics.nvidia_driver";
    const TITLE: &str = "NVIDIA driver";
    let path = "/proc/driver/nvidia/version";

    match ctx.read_to_string(path) {
        Ok(text) => {
            let first_line = text.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
            match parse::first_version(first_line) {
                Some(version) => {
                    let kind = if first_line.contains("Open Kernel Module") {
                        "open kernel module"
                    } else {
                        "proprietary kernel module"
                    };
                    Finding::new(ID, Category::Graphics, TITLE, Status::Detected)
                        .value(format!("{version} ({kind})"))
                        .file(path)
                }
                None => Finding::new(ID, Category::Graphics, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                    .file(path)
                    .note("The version could not be read from the driver's version file."),
            }
        }
        Err(FsError::NotFound) => {
            let bound = gpus
                .iter()
                .any(|g| g.vendor == NVIDIA_VENDOR && g.driver.as_deref() == Some("nvidia"));
            let status = if bound {
                Status::Unverifiable
            } else {
                Status::NotDetected
            };
            Finding::new(ID, Category::Graphics, TITLE, status)
                .file(path)
                .note("The NVIDIA kernel module is not loaded.")
        }
        Err(error) => Finding::new(ID, Category::Graphics, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
            .file(path)
            .note(format!("{path}: {error}")),
    }
}

const ICD_DIRS: [&str; 3] = [
    "/usr/share/vulkan/icd.d",
    "/etc/vulkan/icd.d",
    "/usr/local/share/vulkan/icd.d",
];

/// Reports installed Vulkan drivers and whether one actually loads.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct VulkanProbe;

impl Probe for VulkanProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "vulkan"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Installed Vulkan drivers (ICD manifests) and a vulkaninfo check when available"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        vec![vulkan_drivers(ctx), vulkan_runtime(ctx)]
    }
}

fn vulkan_drivers(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Finding {
    const ID: &str = "graphics.vulkan_drivers";
    const TITLE: &str = "Vulkan drivers";

    let mut manifests = Vec::new();
    let mut searched = Vec::new();
    let mut denied = false;
    for dir in ICD_DIRS {
        match ctx.list_dir(dir) {
            Ok(names) => {
                searched.push(dir);
                manifests.extend(
                    names
                        .into_iter()
                        .filter(|n| n.ends_with(".json"))
                        .map(|n| n.trim_end_matches(".json").to_string()),
                );
            }
            Err(FsError::NotFound) => {}
            Err(_) => denied = true,
        }
    }
    manifests.sort();
    manifests.dedup();

    let mut finding = if !manifests.is_empty() {
        Finding::new(ID, Category::Graphics, TITLE, Status::Detected).value(manifests.join(", "))
    } else if denied {
        Finding::new(ID, Category::Graphics, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
            .note("A Vulkan driver directory could not be read.")
    } else {
        Finding::new(ID, Category::Graphics, TITLE, Status::NotDetected).note(
            "No Vulkan driver manifest is installed. On Arch and CachyOS the packages are \
             vulkan-radeon (AMD), nvidia-utils (NVIDIA) and vulkan-intel (Intel).",
        )
    };
    for dir in if searched.is_empty() {
        ICD_DIRS.to_vec()
    } else {
        searched
    } {
        finding = finding.file(dir);
    }
    finding
}

fn vulkan_runtime(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Finding {
    const ID: &str = "graphics.vulkan_runtime";
    const TITLE: &str = "Vulkan device";
    const COMMAND: &str = "vulkaninfo --summary";
    let base = |status| Finding::new(ID, Category::Graphics, TITLE, status).command(COMMAND);

    match ctx.run("vulkaninfo", &["--summary"]) {
        Ok(output) if output.success() => {
            let field = |name: &str| {
                output
                    .stdout
                    .lines()
                    .filter_map(|line| line.split_once('='))
                    .find(|(key, _)| key.trim() == name)
                    .map(|(_, value)| value.trim().to_string())
            };
            match field("deviceName") {
                Some(device) => {
                    let value = match field("apiVersion") {
                        Some(api) => format!("{device} (Vulkan {api})"),
                        None => device,
                    };
                    base(Status::Detected).value(value)
                }
                None => base(Status::NotDetected).note("vulkaninfo ran but listed no device."),
            }
        }
        Ok(output) => {
            let reason = output
                .stderr
                .lines()
                .chain(output.stdout.lines())
                .map(str::trim)
                .find(|line| !line.is_empty())
                .unwrap_or("no output");
            base(Status::NotDetected).note(format!("vulkaninfo failed: {reason}"))
        }
        Err(CommandError::NotFound) => {
            base(Status::Unverifiable).note("vulkaninfo is not installed (package vulkan-tools).")
        }
        Err(error) => base(Status::Unverifiable).note(format!("vulkaninfo: {error}")),
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::finding::find;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::{scenarios, Fixture, MockRunner};

    #[test]
    fn lists_only_display_adapters() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let gpus = list_gpus(&scenario.context()).expect("gpus");
        // The audio controller in the scenario must be ignored.
        assert_eq!(gpus.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(gpus[0].vendor, 0x10de);
        assert_eq!(gpus[0].device, 0x2684);
        assert_eq!(gpus[0].driver.as_deref(), Some("nvidia"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn reports_nvidia_adapter_and_driver() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = GpuProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        let gpu = find(&findings, "graphics.gpu.0").expect("gpu");
        assert_eq!(
            gpu.value.as_deref(),
            Some("NVIDIA [10de:2684], driver: nvidia")
        );
        let driver = find(&findings, "graphics.nvidia_driver").expect("driver");
        assert_eq!(driver.status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(
            driver.value.as_deref(),
            Some("580.95.05 (open kernel module)")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn amd_adapter_has_no_nvidia_finding() {
        let scenario = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = GpuProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert!(find(&findings, "graphics.gpu.0")
            .and_then(|f| f.value.as_deref())
            .is_some_and(|v| v.starts_with("AMD") && v.ends_with("amdgpu")));
        assert!(find(&findings, "graphics.nvidia_driver").is_none());
    }

    #[test]
    fn flags_unbound_and_nouveau_adapters() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new()
            .file("/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:01:00.0/class", "0x030000\n")
            .file("/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:01:00.0/vendor", "0x10de\n")
            .file("/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:01:00.0/device", "0x1c03\n")
            .file(
                "/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:01:00.0/uevent",
                "DRIVER=nouveau\n",
            )
            .file("/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:02:00.0/class", "0x030200\n")
            .file("/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:02:00.0/vendor", "0x8086\n")
            .file("/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:02:00.0/device", "0x56a0\n")
            .file(
                "/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:02:00.0/uevent",
                "PCI_SLOT_NAME=x\n",
            );
        let findings = GpuProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        let nouveau = find(&findings, "graphics.gpu.0").expect("first");
        assert!(nouveau.notes.iter().any(|n| n.contains("nouveau")));
        let unbound = find(&findings, "graphics.gpu.1").expect("second");
        assert!(unbound
            .value
            .as_deref()
            .is_some_and(|v| v.ends_with("driver: none")));
        assert!(unbound.notes.iter().any(|n| n.contains("No kernel driver")));
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "graphics.nvidia_driver").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn no_pci_bus_is_unverifiable() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let findings = GpuProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Unverifiable);
    }

    #[test]
    fn reports_vulkan_drivers_and_device() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = VulkanProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "graphics.vulkan_drivers").and_then(|f| f.value.as_deref()),
            Some("nvidia_icd")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "graphics.vulkan_runtime").and_then(|f| f.value.as_deref()),
            Some("NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090 (Vulkan 1.4.312)")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn missing_vulkaninfo_is_unverifiable_not_absent() {
        let scenario = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = VulkanProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        let runtime = find(&findings, "graphics.vulkan_runtime").expect("runtime");
        assert_eq!(runtime.status, Status::Unverifiable);
        assert!(runtime.notes.iter().any(|n| n.contains("vulkan-tools")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn failing_vulkaninfo_is_not_detected() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let runner = MockRunner::new().on_exit(
            "vulkaninfo --summary",
            1,
            "",
            "ERROR: [Loader Message] Code 0 : no ICD found\n",
        );
        let findings = VulkanProbe.run(&fixture.context_with(runner, &[]));
        let runtime = find(&findings, "graphics.vulkan_runtime").expect("runtime");
        assert_eq!(runtime.status, Status::NotDetected);
        assert!(runtime.notes.iter().any(|n| n.contains("no ICD found")));
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "graphics.vulkan_drivers").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-diagnostics/src/lib.rs`:

```rs
//! Read-only system probes.
//!
//! Each probe looks at one subject and reports [`tuxgate_core::Finding`]s.
//! Probes never modify the system and never ask for privileges: what cannot
//! be read is reported as unverifiable.

pub mod boot;
pub mod gaming;
pub mod graphics;
pub mod parse;
pub mod readiness;
pub mod security;
pub mod system;
pub mod tools;

use tuxgate_core::{Finding, Probe, ProbeRegistry, SystemContext};

/// Every probe, in display order.
fn all_probes() -> Vec<Box<dyn Probe>> {
    vec![
        Box::new(system::DistroProbe),
        Box::new(system::KernelProbe),
        Box::new(system::CpuProbe),
        Box::new(graphics::GpuProbe),
        Box::new(graphics::VulkanProbe),
        Box::new(gaming::WineProbe),
        Box::new(gaming::ProtonProbe),
        Box::new(security::SecureBootProbe),
        Box::new(security::TpmProbe),
        Box::new(security::IommuProbe),
        Box::new(security::HardeningProbe),
        Box::new(security::VirtualizationProbe),
        Box::new(boot::BootManagerProbe),
        Box::new(tools::ToolsProbe),
    ]
}

/// The registry holding every built-in probe.
pub fn default_registry() -> ProbeRegistry {
    let mut registry = ProbeRegistry::new();
    for probe in all_probes() {
        registry
            .register(probe)
            .expect("built-in probe identifiers are unique");
    }
    registry
}

/// Runs every built-in probe except the disabled ones.
pub fn run_all(ctx: &SystemContext, disabled: &[String]) -> Vec<Finding> {
    default_registry().run(ctx, disabled)
}

/// True when the tree looks like a Linux system with sysfs mounted. Lets
/// probes tell "feature absent" from "nothing to look at".
pub(crate) fn sysfs_available(ctx: &SystemContext) -> bool {
    ctx.is_dir("/sys/class") || ctx.is_dir("/sys/kernel") || ctx.is_dir("/sys/bus")
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::finding::find;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::{scenarios, Fixture};
    use tuxgate_core::Status;

    #[test]
    fn registry_has_unique_documented_probes() {
        let registry = default_registry();
        let described = registry.describe();
        assert_eq!(described.len(), 14);
        for (id, description) in described {
            assert!(!id.is_empty());
            assert!(!description.is_empty(), "probe {id} has no description");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn finding_identifiers_are_unique_in_every_scenario() {
        for scenario in [
            scenarios::cachyos_desktop(),
            scenarios::cachyos_attested(),
            scenarios::arch_minimal(),
            scenarios::vm_guest(),
        ] {
            let findings = run_all(&scenario.context(), &[]);
            let mut ids: Vec<_> = findings.iter().map(|f| f.id.clone()).collect();
            ids.sort();
            let total = ids.len();
            ids.dedup();
            assert_eq!(ids.len(), total, "duplicate finding identifiers");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn an_empty_tree_produces_no_false_detection() {
        // Nothing to read: no finding may claim something was detected.
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let findings = run_all(&fixture.context(), &[]);
        assert!(!findings.is_empty());
        for finding in &findings {
            assert_ne!(
                finding.status,
                Status::Detected,
                "{} claims a detection on an empty tree",
                finding.id
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn security_findings_carry_the_attestation_disclaimer() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = run_all(&scenario.context(), &[]);
        for id in [
            "security.secure_boot",
            "security.tpm",
            "security.iommu",
            "security.lockdown",
            "security.module_signatures",
        ] {
            let finding = find(&findings, id).expect(id);
            assert!(
                finding
                    .notes
                    .iter()
                    .any(|n| n == tuxgate_core::finding::ATTESTATION_DISCLAIMER),
                "{id} lacks the disclaimer"
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn disabled_probes_are_skipped() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = run_all(
            &scenario.context(),
            &["wine".to_string(), "tools".to_string()],
        );
        assert!(find(&findings, "gaming.wine").is_none());
        assert!(findings.iter().all(|f| !f.id.starts_with("tools.")));
        assert!(find(&findings, "system.kernel").is_some());
    }
}
```
