`crates/tuxgate-diagnostics/src/parse.rs`:

```rs
//! Small parsers for the text formats found under `/etc`, `/proc` and `/sys`.

use std::collections::BTreeMap;

/// Parses `KEY=value` lines as found in `/etc/os-release` and sysfs `uevent`
/// files. Surrounding quotes are removed; comments and blank lines ignored.
pub fn key_values(text: &str) -> BTreeMap<String, String> {
    text.lines()
        .map(str::trim)
        .filter(|line| !line.is_empty() && !line.starts_with('#'))
        .filter_map(|line| line.split_once('='))
        .map(|(key, value)| {
            let value = value.trim();
            let unquoted = value
                .strip_prefix('"')
                .and_then(|v| v.strip_suffix('"'))
                .or_else(|| value.strip_prefix('\'').and_then(|v| v.strip_suffix('\'')))
                .unwrap_or(value);
            (key.trim().to_string(), unquoted.to_string())
        })
        .collect()
}

/// Returns the first value of a `key : value` field in `/proc/cpuinfo`.
pub fn cpuinfo_field(text: &str, key: &str) -> Option<String> {
    text.lines()
        .filter_map(|line| line.split_once(':'))
        .find(|(name, _)| name.trim() == key)
        .map(|(_, value)| value.trim().to_string())
        .filter(|value| !value.is_empty())
}

/// Returns the CPU feature flags of the first processor.
pub fn cpu_flags(text: &str) -> Vec<String> {
    cpuinfo_field(text, "flags")
        .or_else(|| cpuinfo_field(text, "Features"))
        .map(|flags| flags.split_whitespace().map(str::to_string).collect())
        .unwrap_or_default()
}

/// Returns the bracketed choice of a sysfs selector such as
/// `none [integrity] confidentiality`.
pub fn bracketed(text: &str) -> Option<&str> {
    let start = text.find('[')?;
    let end = text[start..].find(']')? + start;
    let inner = text[start + 1..end].trim();
    (!inner.is_empty()).then_some(inner)
}

/// Returns the value of a `key=value` kernel command line parameter. The
/// last occurrence wins, as in the kernel.
pub fn cmdline_value<'a>(cmdline: &'a str, key: &str) -> Option<&'a str> {
    cmdline
        .split_whitespace()
        .filter_map(|param| param.split_once('='))
        .filter(|(name, _)| *name == key)
        .map(|(_, value)| value)
        .next_back()
}

/// Parses a sysfs hexadecimal identifier such as `0x10de`.
pub fn hex_id(text: &str) -> Option<u32> {
    let trimmed = text.trim();
    let digits = trimmed
        .strip_prefix("0x")
        .or_else(|| trimmed.strip_prefix("0X"))
        .unwrap_or(trimmed);
    u32::from_str_radix(digits, 16).ok()
}

/// Finds the first dotted version number (`580.95.05`, `10.0`) in a text.
pub fn first_version(text: &str) -> Option<&str> {
    text.split(|c: char| c.is_whitespace())
        .map(|token| token.trim_matches(|c: char| !c.is_ascii_digit()))
        .find(|token| {
            token.contains('.')
                && token.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_digit() || c == '.')
                && token.split('.').all(|part| !part.is_empty())
        })
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn parses_os_release() {
        let map = key_values(
            "# comment\nNAME=\"CachyOS Linux\"\nID=cachyos\nID_LIKE='arch'\n\nBROKEN LINE\nEMPTY=\n",
        );
        assert_eq!(map.get("NAME").map(String::as_str), Some("CachyOS Linux"));
        assert_eq!(map.get("ID").map(String::as_str), Some("cachyos"));
        assert_eq!(map.get("ID_LIKE").map(String::as_str), Some("arch"));
        assert_eq!(map.get("EMPTY").map(String::as_str), Some(""));
        assert!(!map.contains_key("BROKEN LINE"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn reads_cpuinfo_fields() {
        let text = "processor\t: 0\nmodel name\t: AMD Ryzen 7\nflags\t\t: fpu svm\n\nprocessor\t: 1\nmodel name\t: other\n";
        assert_eq!(
            cpuinfo_field(text, "model name").as_deref(),
            Some("AMD Ryzen 7")
        );
        assert_eq!(cpuinfo_field(text, "missing"), None);
        assert_eq!(cpu_flags(text), ["fpu", "svm"]);
        assert!(cpu_flags("processor: 0\n").is_empty());
    }

    #[test]
    fn finds_the_bracketed_choice() {
        assert_eq!(
            bracketed("none [integrity] confidentiality\n"),
            Some("integrity")
        );
        assert_eq!(bracketed("[none] integrity confidentiality"), Some("none"));
        assert_eq!(bracketed("none integrity"), None);
        assert_eq!(bracketed("[] x"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn reads_command_line_parameters() {
        let cmdline = "root=/dev/sda2 rw iommu=off lockdown=integrity iommu=pt quiet";
        assert_eq!(cmdline_value(cmdline, "lockdown"), Some("integrity"));
        assert_eq!(cmdline_value(cmdline, "iommu"), Some("pt"));
        assert_eq!(cmdline_value(cmdline, "quiet"), None);
        assert_eq!(cmdline_value(cmdline, "root"), Some("/dev/sda2"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn parses_hex_identifiers() {
        assert_eq!(hex_id("0x10de\n"), Some(0x10de));
        assert_eq!(hex_id("030000"), Some(0x03_0000));
        assert_eq!(hex_id("nope"), None);
    }

    #[test]
    fn extracts_versions() {
        assert_eq!(
            first_version(
                "NVRM version: NVIDIA UNIX Open Kernel Module for x86_64  580.95.05  Release Build"
            ),
            Some("580.95.05")
        );
        assert_eq!(first_version("wine-10.19 (Staging)"), Some("10.19"));
        assert_eq!(first_version("no version here"), None);
        assert_eq!(first_version("x86_64 build"), None);
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-diagnostics/src/readiness.rs`:

```rs
//! A view of the system against the attested-boot model.
//!
//! In October 2026 Epic announced Easy Anti-Cheat Linux v2, which accepts a
//! Linux system only when its boot chain can be verified. CachyOS documents
//! the properties that model requires. This module lists which of those
//! properties are observable on the machine.
//!
//! It is informational. It is not a validation by Epic, and it has nothing
//! to do with Riot Vanguard, which has no Linux version.

use serde::Serialize;
use tuxgate_core::finding::find;
use tuxgate_core::{Finding, Status};

/// Shown with every readiness view.
pub const READINESS_DISCLAIMER: &str = "This compares local observations with the properties \
     documented for Easy Anti-Cheat Linux v2 on CachyOS. It is not a validation by any \
     anti-cheat vendor, and it does not apply to Riot Vanguard, which has no Linux version.";

/// Whether a property was observed.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
pub enum Observed {
    Yes,
    No,
    Unknown,
}

/// One property of the model.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct ReadinessItem {
    pub property: &'static str,
    /// The finding the observation comes from.
    pub finding_id: &'static str,
    pub observed: Observed,
    /// The observed value, or why it is unknown.
    pub detail: String,
}

/// The whole view.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct Readiness {
    pub items: Vec<ReadinessItem>,
    /// Number of properties observed.
    pub observed: usize,
    pub total: usize,
    /// Requirements of the model that cannot be checked from the machine.
    pub not_locally_checkable: Vec<&'static str>,
    pub disclaimer: &'static str,
}

struct Property {
    name: &'static str,
    finding_id: &'static str,
    /// The status the finding must have for the property to hold.
    wanted: Status,
    /// When set, the finding's value must also equal this.
    wanted_value: Option<&'static str>,
}

const PROPERTIES: &[Property] = &[
    Property {
        name: "Secure Boot enabled",
        finding_id: "security.secure_boot",
        wanted: Status::Detected,
        wanted_value: None,
    },
    Property {
        name: "TPM 2.0 present",
        finding_id: "security.tpm",
        wanted: Status::Detected,
        wanted_value: Some("TPM 2.0"),
    },
    Property {
        name: "IOMMU active",
        finding_id: "security.iommu",
        wanted: Status::Detected,
        wanted_value: None,
    },
    Property {
        name: "Kernel lockdown in integrity mode",
        finding_id: "security.lockdown",
        wanted: Status::Detected,
        wanted_value: None,
    },
    Property {
        name: "Only signed kernel modules load",
        finding_id: "security.module_signatures",
        wanted: Status::Detected,
        wanted_value: None,
    },
    Property {
        name: "kexec disabled",
        finding_id: "security.kexec",
        wanted: Status::Detected,
        wanted_value: None,
    },
    Property {
        name: "BPF security module active",
        finding_id: "security.bpf_lsm",
        wanted: Status::Detected,
        wanted_value: None,
    },
    Property {
        name: "Not a virtual machine",
        finding_id: "security.virtualization",
        wanted: Status::NotDetected,
        wanted_value: None,
    },
];

const NOT_LOCALLY_CHECKABLE: &[&str] = &[
    "The kernel and boot manager must be the builds signed by a key the anti-cheat vendor trusts.",
    "The boot measurements recorded in the TPM must match what the vendor expects.",
    "The vendor's servers make the final decision.",
];

fn describe(finding: &Finding) -> String {
    match &finding.value {
        Some(value) => format!("{} ({value})", finding.status),
        None => finding.status.to_string(),
    }
}

/// Builds the view from a set of findings.
pub fn attested_boot(findings: &[Finding]) -> Readiness {
    let items: Vec<ReadinessItem> = PROPERTIES
        .iter()
        .map(|property| {
            let (observed, detail) = match find(findings, property.finding_id) {
                None => (
                    Observed::Unknown,
                    "not checked (probe disabled)".to_string(),
                ),
                Some(finding) => {
                    let observed = if finding.status == Status::Unverifiable {
                        Observed::Unknown
                    } else if finding.status == property.wanted
                        && property
                            .wanted_value
                            .is_none_or(|wanted| finding.value.as_deref() == Some(wanted))
                    {
                        Observed::Yes
                    } else {
                        Observed::No
                    };
                    (observed, describe(finding))
                }
            };
            ReadinessItem {
                property: property.name,
                finding_id: property.finding_id,
                observed,
                detail,
            }
        })
        .collect();

    Readiness {
        observed: items.iter().filter(|i| i.observed == Observed::Yes).count(),
        total: items.len(),
        items,
        not_locally_checkable: NOT_LOCALLY_CHECKABLE.to_vec(),
        disclaimer: READINESS_DISCLAIMER,
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::run_all;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::scenarios;

    fn item<'a>(readiness: &'a Readiness, finding_id: &str) -> &'a ReadinessItem {
        readiness
            .items
            .iter()
            .find(|i| i.finding_id == finding_id)
            .expect(finding_id)
    }

    #[test]
    fn attested_kernel_shows_every_property() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_attested();
        let readiness = attested_boot(&run_all(&scenario.context(), &[]));
        assert_eq!(readiness.total, 8);
        assert_eq!(readiness.observed, 8);
        // Even then, the parts only the vendor can verify stay listed.
        assert_eq!(readiness.not_locally_checkable.len(), 3);
        assert!(readiness.disclaimer.contains("not a validation"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn default_desktop_shows_a_partial_picture() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let readiness = attested_boot(&run_all(&scenario.context(), &[]));
        assert_eq!(
            item(&readiness, "security.secure_boot").observed,
            Observed::Yes
        );
        assert_eq!(item(&readiness, "security.tpm").observed, Observed::Yes);
        assert_eq!(item(&readiness, "security.iommu").observed, Observed::Yes);
        assert_eq!(item(&readiness, "security.lockdown").observed, Observed::No);
        assert_eq!(item(&readiness, "security.kexec").observed, Observed::No);
        assert_eq!(item(&readiness, "security.bpf_lsm").observed, Observed::Yes);
        assert_eq!(
            item(&readiness, "security.virtualization").observed,
            Observed::Yes
        );
        assert_eq!(readiness.observed, 5);
    }

    #[test]
    fn virtual_machine_fails_the_bare_metal_property() {
        let scenario = scenarios::vm_guest();
        let readiness = attested_boot(&run_all(&scenario.context(), &[]));
        assert_eq!(
            item(&readiness, "security.virtualization").observed,
            Observed::No
        );
        // Unverifiable findings are unknown, not failures.
        assert_eq!(
            item(&readiness, "security.lockdown").observed,
            Observed::Unknown
        );
        // Not applicable is a plain "no": the property does not hold.
        assert_eq!(
            item(&readiness, "security.secure_boot").observed,
            Observed::No
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn tpm_1_2_does_not_satisfy_the_tpm_property() {
        let findings = vec![Finding::new(
            "security.tpm",
            tuxgate_core::Category::Security,
            "TPM",
            Status::Detected,
        )
        .value("TPM 1.2")];
        let readiness = attested_boot(&findings);
        assert_eq!(item(&readiness, "security.tpm").observed, Observed::No);
    }

    #[test]
    fn disabled_probes_give_unknown() {
        let readiness = attested_boot(&[]);
        assert_eq!(readiness.observed, 0);
        assert!(readiness
            .items
            .iter()
            .all(|i| i.observed == Observed::Unknown));
    }
}

```

`crates/tuxgate-diagnostics/src/security.rs`:

```rs
//! Platform security features: firmware, Secure Boot, TPM, IOMMU, kernel
//! hardening and virtualisation.
//!
//! Everything here is a local observation. None of it is an attestation,
//! and none of it is recognised by any anti-cheat; every finding says so.

use tuxgate_core::efi::{self, EFIVARS_DIR, EFI_DIR, EFI_GLOBAL_GUID};
use tuxgate_core::{Category, CommandError, Finding, FsError, Probe, Status, SystemContext};

use crate::{parse, sysfs_available};

fn security(id: &str, title: &str, status: Status) -> Finding {
    Finding::new(id, Category::Security, title, status)
}

/// Reports the firmware type and the Secure Boot state.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct SecureBootProbe;

impl Probe for SecureBootProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "secure_boot"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "UEFI firmware and Secure Boot state, from EFI variables"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        const SB_ID: &str = "security.secure_boot";
        const SB_TITLE: &str = "Secure Boot";

        if !sysfs_available(ctx) && !ctx.is_dir(EFI_DIR) {
            return vec![
                security("security.uefi", "UEFI firmware", Status::Unverifiable)
                    .file(EFI_DIR)
                    .note("sysfs is not available."),
                security(SB_ID, SB_TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                    .note("sysfs is not available.")
                    .not_an_attestation(),
            ];
        }

        if !efi::booted_with_uefi(ctx) {
            return vec![
                security("security.uefi", "UEFI firmware", Status::NotDetected)
                    .file(EFI_DIR)
                    .note("The system was booted in legacy BIOS mode, or the kernel does not expose EFI runtime services."),
                security(SB_ID, SB_TITLE, Status::NotApplicable)
                    .note("Secure Boot requires UEFI boot.")
                    .not_an_attestation(),
            ];
        }

        let uefi = security("security.uefi", "UEFI firmware", Status::Detected)
            .value("UEFI")
            .file(EFI_DIR);

        let path = efi::var_path("SecureBoot", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID);
        let mut secure_boot = match efi::read_u8(ctx, "SecureBoot", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID) {
            Ok(1) => security(SB_ID, SB_TITLE, Status::Detected)
                .value("enabled")
                .note(
                    "Secure Boot being enabled does not tell a remote party which kernel was \
                     booted. An anti-cheat would have to verify the boot chain itself.",
                ),
            Ok(0) => security(SB_ID, SB_TITLE, Status::NotDetected).value("disabled"),
            Ok(other) => security(SB_ID, SB_TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                .note(format!("Unexpected SecureBoot value {other}.")),
            Err(FsError::NotFound) if !ctx.is_dir(EFIVARS_DIR) => {
                security(SB_ID, SB_TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                    .note("efivarfs is not mounted, so EFI variables cannot be read.")
            }
            Err(FsError::NotFound) => security(SB_ID, SB_TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                .note("The firmware does not expose a SecureBoot variable."),
            Err(error) => security(SB_ID, SB_TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                .note(format!("SecureBoot variable: {error}")),
        }
        .file(path);

        if efi::read_u8(ctx, "SetupMode", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID) == Ok(1) {
            secure_boot = secure_boot.note(
                "The firmware is in Setup Mode: Secure Boot keys can be enrolled and Secure \
                 Boot is not enforcing.",
            );
        }

        vec![uefi, secure_boot.not_an_attestation()]
    }
}

/// Reports the Trusted Platform Module.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct TpmProbe;

impl Probe for TpmProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "tpm"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Trusted Platform Module presence and specification version, from sysfs"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        const ID: &str = "security.tpm";
        const TITLE: &str = "TPM";
        let class_dir = "/sys/class/tpm";
        let absent_note = "No TPM device is exposed by the kernel. A firmware TPM (AMD fTPM, \
             Intel PTT) may be present but disabled in the firmware settings.";

        let finding = match ctx.list_dir(class_dir) {
            Ok(devices) => match devices.iter().find(|d| d.starts_with("tpm")) {
                Some(device) => {
                    let version_path = format!("{class_dir}/{device}/tpm_version_major");
                    match ctx.read_trimmed(&version_path).as_deref() {
                        Ok("2") => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Detected)
                            .value("TPM 2.0")
                            .file(version_path),
                        Ok("1") => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Detected)
                            .value("TPM 1.2")
                            .file(version_path)
                            .note("Riot requires TPM 2.0 on Windows; TPM 1.2 does not meet that."),
                        Ok(other) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                            .file(version_path)
                            .note(format!("Unexpected TPM version '{other}'.")),
                        Err(_) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                            .file(version_path)
                            .note(
                                "A TPM device exists but its version is not exposed (the \
                                 attribute exists from Linux 5.5).",
                            ),
                    }
                }
                None => security(ID, TITLE, Status::NotDetected)
                    .file(class_dir)
                    .note(absent_note),
            },
            Err(FsError::NotFound) if sysfs_available(ctx) => {
                security(ID, TITLE, Status::NotDetected)
                    .file(class_dir)
                    .note(absent_note)
            }
            Err(error) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                .file(class_dir)
                .note(format!("{class_dir}: {error}")),
        };

        vec![finding
            .note(
                "A TPM being present is not measured boot: nothing here shows that boot \
                 measurements were taken or verified.",
            )
            .not_an_attestation()]
    }
}

/// Reports whether an IOMMU is active.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct IommuProbe;

impl Probe for IommuProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "iommu"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "IOMMU (Intel VT-d, AMD-Vi) activity, from sysfs and the kernel command line"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        const ID: &str = "security.iommu";
        const TITLE: &str = "IOMMU";
        let groups_dir = "/sys/kernel/iommu_groups";
        let units_dir = "/sys/class/iommu";

        if !sysfs_available(ctx) {
            return vec![security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                .file(groups_dir)
                .note("sysfs is not available.")
                .not_an_attestation()];
        }

        let groups = ctx.list_dir(groups_dir).map(|g| g.len());
        let units = ctx.list_dir(units_dir).unwrap_or_default();

        let mut finding = match groups {
            Ok(count) if count > 0 => {
                let value = if units.is_empty() {
                    format!("active, {count} groups")
                } else {
                    format!("active, {count} groups ({})", units.join(", "))
                };
                security(ID, TITLE, Status::Detected).value(value)
            }
            Ok(_) | Err(FsError::NotFound) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::NotDetected).note(
                "No IOMMU group exists. Enable VT-d (Intel) or AMD-Vi / IOMMU (AMD) in the \
                 firmware; Intel systems may also need intel_iommu=on on the kernel command line.",
            ),
            Err(error) => {
                security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note(format!("{groups_dir}: {error}"))
            }
        }
        .file(groups_dir);

        if let Ok(cmdline) = ctx.read_to_string("/proc/cmdline") {
            let passthrough = parse::cmdline_value(&cmdline, "iommu.passthrough") == Some("1")
                || parse::cmdline_value(&cmdline, "iommu") == Some("pt");
            if passthrough {
                finding = finding.file("/proc/cmdline").note(
                    "The kernel command line selects IOMMU passthrough: devices are not \
                     restricted by default, which weakens DMA protection.",
                );
            }
        }

        vec![finding.not_an_attestation()]
    }
}

/// Reports kernel hardening settings that attested-boot models rely on.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct HardeningProbe;

impl Probe for HardeningProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "hardening"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Kernel lockdown, module signature enforcement, kexec and active security modules"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        vec![
            lockdown(ctx).not_an_attestation(),
            module_signatures(ctx).not_an_attestation(),
            kexec(ctx).not_an_attestation(),
            bpf_lsm(ctx).not_an_attestation(),
        ]
    }
}

fn lockdown(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Finding {
    const ID: &str = "security.lockdown";
    const TITLE: &str = "Kernel lockdown";
    let path = "/sys/kernel/security/lockdown";

    match ctx.read_to_string(path) {
        Ok(text) => match parse::bracketed(&text) {
            Some("none") => security(ID, TITLE, Status::NotDetected).value("none"),
            Some(mode) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Detected).value(mode),
            None => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
                .note("The lockdown file has an unexpected format."),
        },
        Err(FsError::NotFound) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note(
            "securityfs is not mounted, or the kernel was built without the lockdown \
             security module.",
        ),
        Err(error) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note(format!("{path}: {error}")),
    }
    .file(path)
}

fn module_signatures(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Finding {
    const ID: &str = "security.module_signatures";
    const TITLE: &str = "Module signature enforcement";
    let path = "/sys/module/module/parameters/sig_enforce";

    match ctx.read_trimmed(path).as_deref() {
        Ok("Y") | Ok("1") => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Detected).value("enforced"),
        Ok("N") | Ok("0") => security(ID, TITLE, Status::NotDetected)
            .value("not enforced")
            .note(
                "Unsigned kernel modules can be loaded (the kernel is then marked tainted). \
                 Kernel lockdown, when active, also refuses unsigned modules.",
            ),
        Ok(other) => {
            security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note(format!("Unexpected value '{other}'."))
        }
        Err(FsError::NotFound) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable)
            .note("Not exposed: the kernel may be built without module signature support."),
        Err(error) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note(format!("{path}: {error}")),
    }
    .file(path)
}

fn kexec(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Finding {
    const ID: &str = "security.kexec";
    const TITLE: &str = "kexec disabled";
    let path = "/proc/sys/kernel/kexec_load_disabled";

    match ctx.read_trimmed(path).as_deref() {
        Ok("0") => security(ID, TITLE, Status::NotDetected)
            .value("kexec allowed")
            .note("A new kernel can be loaded at runtime without going through the firmware."),
        Ok(value) if value.parse::<u32>().is_ok() => {
            security(ID, TITLE, Status::Detected).value("kexec disabled")
        }
        Ok(other) => {
            security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note(format!("Unexpected value '{other}'."))
        }
        Err(FsError::NotFound) if ctx.is_dir("/proc/sys/kernel") => {
            security(ID, TITLE, Status::NotApplicable)
                .note("The kernel was built without kexec support.")
        }
        Err(error) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note(format!("{path}: {error}")),
    }
    .file(path)
}

fn bpf_lsm(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Finding {
    const ID: &str = "security.bpf_lsm";
    const TITLE: &str = "BPF security module";
    let path = "/sys/kernel/security/lsm";

    match ctx.read_trimmed(path) {
        Ok(list) => {
            let active = list.split(',').any(|lsm| lsm.trim() == "bpf");
            let status = if active {
                Status::Detected
            } else {
                Status::NotDetected
            };
            security(ID, TITLE, status).value(format!("active modules: {list}"))
        }
        Err(FsError::NotFound) => {
            security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note("securityfs is not mounted.")
        }
        Err(error) => security(ID, TITLE, Status::Unverifiable).note(format!("{path}: {error}")),
    }
    .file(path)
}

/// Text shown whenever a hypervisor is found.
pub const VM_NOTE: &str = "Riot Vanguard refuses virtual machines (errors VAN 9100 and VAN 138). \
     Hiding a hypervisor from an anti-cheat is circumvention; Tuxgate does not help with it.";

fn dmi_hypervisor(vendor: &str, product: &str) -> Option<&'static str> {
    let haystack = format!("{vendor} {product}").to_ascii_lowercase();
    let table = [
        ("qemu", "QEMU"),
        ("kvm", "KVM"),
        ("vmware", "VMware"),
        ("virtualbox", "VirtualBox"),
        ("innotek", "VirtualBox"),
        ("xen", "Xen"),
        ("parallels", "Parallels"),
        ("bochs", "Bochs"),
        ("microsoft corporation virtual machine", "Hyper-V"),
    ];
    table
        .iter()
        .find(|(needle, _)| haystack.contains(needle))
        .map(|(_, name)| *name)
}

/// Reports whether the system runs inside a virtual machine.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct VirtualizationProbe;

impl Probe for VirtualizationProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "virtualization"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Whether the system is a virtual machine, from systemd-detect-virt, CPU flags and DMI"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        const ID: &str = "security.virtualization";
        const TITLE: &str = "Virtual machine";
        let mut evidence_seen = false;
        let mut finding = security(ID, TITLE, Status::NotDetected);
        let mut detected: Vec<String> = Vec::new();

        // systemd-detect-virt prints the technology and exits 0, or prints
        // "none" and exits non-zero.
        match ctx.run("systemd-detect-virt", &[]) {
            Ok(output) => {
                evidence_seen = true;
                finding = finding.command("systemd-detect-virt");
                let name = output.stdout.trim();
                if output.success() && !name.is_empty() && name != "none" {
                    detected.push(name.to_string());
                }
            }
            Err(CommandError::NotFound | CommandError::Disabled) => {}
            Err(error) => {
                finding = finding.note(format!("systemd-detect-virt: {error}"));
            }
        }

        if let Ok(cpuinfo) = ctx.read_to_string("/proc/cpuinfo") {
            evidence_seen = true;
            finding = finding.file("/proc/cpuinfo");
            if parse::cpu_flags(&cpuinfo).iter().any(|f| f == "hypervisor") {
                detected.push("CPU hypervisor flag".to_string());
            }
        }

        let vendor = ctx.read_trimmed("/sys/class/dmi/id/sys_vendor");
        let product = ctx.read_trimmed("/sys/class/dmi/id/product_name");
        if vendor.is_ok() || product.is_ok() {
            evidence_seen = true;
            finding = finding.file("/sys/class/dmi/id");
            let vendor = vendor.unwrap_or_default();
            let product = product.unwrap_or_default();
            if let Some(name) = dmi_hypervisor(&vendor, &product) {
                detected.push(format!("{name} (DMI)"));
            }
        }

        if let Ok(kind) = ctx.read_trimmed("/sys/hypervisor/type") {
            evidence_seen = true;
            finding = finding.file("/sys/hypervisor/type");
            if !kind.is_empty() {
                detected.push(format!("{kind} (sysfs)"));
            }
        }

        if !evidence_seen {
            finding.status = Status::Unverifiable;
            return vec![finding.note("No source of virtualisation information could be read.")];
        }

        if detected.is_empty() {
            finding = finding.value("bare metal");
        } else {
            detected.dedup();
            finding.status = Status::Detected;
            finding = finding.value(detected.join(", ")).note(VM_NOTE);
        }
        vec![finding]
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::finding::find;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::{efi_var_u8, scenarios, Fixture};

    #[test]
    fn secure_boot_enabled() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = SecureBootProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "security.uefi").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::Detected)
        );
        let sb = find(&findings, "security.secure_boot").expect("sb");
        assert_eq!(sb.status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(sb.value.as_deref(), Some("enabled"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn secure_boot_disabled() {
        let scenario = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = SecureBootProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        let sb = find(&findings, "security.secure_boot").expect("sb");
        assert_eq!(sb.status, Status::NotDetected);
        assert_eq!(sb.value.as_deref(), Some("disabled"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn secure_boot_not_applicable_on_bios() {
        let scenario = scenarios::vm_guest();
        let findings = SecureBootProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "security.uefi").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "security.secure_boot").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotApplicable)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn secure_boot_unverifiable_without_efivarfs() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new().dir("/sys/firmware/efi").dir("/sys/class");
        let findings = SecureBootProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        let sb = find(&findings, "security.secure_boot").expect("sb");
        assert_eq!(sb.status, Status::Unverifiable);
        assert!(sb
            .notes
            .iter()
            .any(|n| n.contains("efivarfs is not mounted")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn setup_mode_is_flagged() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new()
            .bytes(
                &efi::var_path("SecureBoot", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID),
                &efi_var_u8(0),
            )
            .bytes(&efi::var_path("SetupMode", EFI_GLOBAL_GUID), &efi_var_u8(1));
        let findings = SecureBootProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        let sb = find(&findings, "security.secure_boot").expect("sb");
        assert!(sb.notes.iter().any(|n| n.contains("Setup Mode")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn tpm_versions() {
        let v2 = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = TpmProbe.run(&v2.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("TPM 2.0"));

        let v1 = Fixture::new().file("/sys/class/tpm/tpm0/tpm_version_major", "1\n");
        let findings = TpmProbe.run(&v1.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("TPM 1.2"));
        assert!(findings[0]
            .notes
            .iter()
            .any(|n| n.contains("does not meet")));

        let old_kernel = Fixture::new().dir("/sys/class/tpm/tpm0");
        let findings = TpmProbe.run(&old_kernel.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Unverifiable);
    }

    #[test]
    fn tpm_absent() {
        let scenario = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = TpmProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::NotDetected);
        assert!(findings[0].notes.iter().any(|n| n.contains("fTPM")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn iommu_states() {
        let on = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = IommuProbe.run(&on.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(
            findings[0].value.as_deref(),
            Some("active, 24 groups (ivhd0)")
        );

        let off = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = IommuProbe.run(&off.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::NotDetected);
    }

    #[test]
    fn iommu_passthrough_is_flagged() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new()
            .dir("/sys/kernel/iommu_groups/0")
            .file("/proc/cmdline", "root=/dev/sda1 iommu=pt\n");
        let findings = IommuProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Detected);
        assert!(findings[0].notes.iter().any(|n| n.contains("passthrough")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn hardening_on_default_kernel() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = HardeningProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "security.lockdown").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "security.module_signatures").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "security.kexec").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "security.bpf_lsm").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::Detected)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn hardening_on_attested_kernel() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_attested();
        let findings = HardeningProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        let lockdown = find(&findings, "security.lockdown").expect("lockdown");
        assert_eq!(lockdown.status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(lockdown.value.as_deref(), Some("integrity"));
        for id in [
            "security.module_signatures",
            "security.kexec",
            "security.bpf_lsm",
        ] {
            assert_eq!(
                find(&findings, id).map(|f| f.status),
                Some(Status::Detected),
                "{id}"
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn hardening_unverifiable_without_securityfs() {
        let scenario = scenarios::vm_guest();
        let findings = HardeningProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "security.lockdown").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::Unverifiable)
        );
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "security.kexec").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotApplicable)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn bare_metal_is_not_a_vm() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = VirtualizationProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::NotDetected);
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("bare metal"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn vm_is_detected_with_a_warning() {
        let scenario = scenarios::vm_guest();
        let findings = VirtualizationProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Detected);
        let value = findings[0].value.as_deref().expect("value");
        assert!(value.contains("kvm"));
        assert!(value.contains("CPU hypervisor flag"));
        assert!(value.contains("QEMU (DMI)"));
        assert!(findings[0].notes.iter().any(|n| n == VM_NOTE));
    }

    #[test]
    fn vm_detection_without_any_source_is_unverifiable() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let findings = VirtualizationProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Unverifiable);
    }

    #[test]
    fn recognises_dmi_vendors() {
        assert_eq!(
            dmi_hypervisor("innotek GmbH", "VirtualBox"),
            Some("VirtualBox")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            dmi_hypervisor("Microsoft Corporation", "Virtual Machine"),
            Some("Hyper-V")
        );
        assert_eq!(
            dmi_hypervisor("Microsoft Corporation", "Surface Laptop"),
            None
        );
        assert_eq!(dmi_hypervisor("ASUS", "System Product Name"), None);
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-diagnostics/src/system.rs`:

```rs
//! Distribution, kernel and processor.

use tuxgate_core::{Category, Finding, FsError, Probe, Status, SystemContext};

use crate::parse;

fn unreadable(id: &str, title: &str, path: &str, error: &FsError) -> Finding {
    let status = match error {
        FsError::NotFound => Status::NotDetected,
        _ => Status::Unverifiable,
    };
    Finding::new(id, Category::System, title, status)
        .file(path)
        .note(format!("{path}: {error}"))
}

/// Identifies the distribution from `os-release`.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct DistroProbe;

impl Probe for DistroProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "distro"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Linux distribution and version, from /etc/os-release"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        const ID: &str = "system.distribution";
        const TITLE: &str = "Distribution";
        let primary = "/etc/os-release";
        let fallback = "/usr/lib/os-release";

        let (path, text) = match ctx.read_to_string(primary) {
            Ok(text) => (primary, text),
            Err(FsError::NotFound) => match ctx.read_to_string(fallback) {
                Ok(text) => (fallback, text),
                Err(error) => return vec![unreadable(ID, TITLE, fallback, &error)],
            },
            Err(error) => return vec![unreadable(ID, TITLE, primary, &error)],
        };

        let fields = parse::key_values(&text);
        let get = |key: &str| {
            fields
                .get(key)
                .map(String::as_str)
                .filter(|v| !v.is_empty())
        };
        let name = get("PRETTY_NAME").or(get("NAME")).unwrap_or("unknown");
        let id = get("ID").unwrap_or("unknown");
        let like = get("ID_LIKE").unwrap_or("");
        let version = get("VERSION_ID").or(get("BUILD_ID"));

        let mut value = name.to_string();
        if let Some(version) = version {
            value.push_str(&format!(" ({version})"));
        }

        let family = if id == "cachyos" {
            "CachyOS: the distribution Tuxgate is developed for."
        } else if id == "arch" || like.split_whitespace().any(|l| l == "arch") {
            "Arch-based: the Tuxgate package builds here with makepkg."
        } else {
            "Not Arch-based: the diagnostics work, but no package is provided for this distribution."
        };

        vec![Finding::new(ID, Category::System, TITLE, Status::Detected)
            .value(value)
            .file(path)
            .note(format!("ID={id}"))
            .note(family)]
    }
}

/// The CachyOS kernel package a release string corresponds to, if any.
///
/// `6.17.9-2-cachyos-bore` gives `linux-cachyos-bore`.
pub fn cachyos_kernel_package(release: &str) -> Option<String> {
    let start = release.find("cachyos")?;
    let suffix = &release[start..];
    let valid = suffix
        .chars()
        .all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '-' || c == '_');
    valid.then(|| format!("linux-{suffix}"))
}

/// Reports the running kernel and whether it is a CachyOS build.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct KernelProbe;

impl Probe for KernelProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "kernel"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Kernel release, CachyOS kernel variant and machine architecture"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        let mut findings = Vec::new();
        let release_path = "/proc/sys/kernel/osrelease";

        match ctx.read_trimmed(release_path) {
            Ok(release) if !release.is_empty() => {
                findings.push(
                    Finding::new(
                        "system.kernel",
                        Category::System,
                        "Kernel",
                        Status::Detected,
                    )
                    .value(release.clone())
                    .file(release_path),
                );

                let flavour = match cachyos_kernel_package(&release) {
                    Some(package) => {
                        let mut finding = Finding::new(
                            "system.kernel_flavour",
                            Category::System,
                            "CachyOS kernel",
                            Status::Detected,
                        )
                        .value(package.clone())
                        .derived(release_path);
                        if package.contains("-eac") {
                            finding = finding.note(
                                "This is the CachyOS kernel built for attested boot (Easy \
                                 Anti-Cheat Linux v2). It has no connection with Riot Vanguard.",
                            );
                        }
                        finding
                    }
                    None => Finding::new(
                        "system.kernel_flavour",
                        Category::System,
                        "CachyOS kernel",
                        Status::NotDetected,
                    )
                    .derived(release_path)
                    .note("The running kernel is not a CachyOS build."),
                };
                findings.push(flavour.note(
                    "Tuxgate is a user-space program and loads no kernel module, so it works \
                     the same on every kernel variant.",
                ));
            }
            Ok(_) => findings.push(
                Finding::new(
                    "system.kernel",
                    Category::System,
                    "Kernel",
                    Status::Unverifiable,
                )
                .file(release_path)
                .note("the file is empty"),
            ),
            Err(error) => {
                findings.push(unreadable("system.kernel", "Kernel", release_path, &error));
            }
        }

        let arch_path = "/proc/sys/kernel/arch";
        findings.push(match ctx.read_trimmed(arch_path) {
            Ok(arch) if !arch.is_empty() => {
                let finding = Finding::new(
                    "system.architecture",
                    Category::System,
                    "Architecture",
                    Status::Detected,
                )
                .value(arch.clone())
                .file(arch_path);
                if arch == "x86_64" {
                    finding
                } else {
                    finding.note("Riot's PC games are x86_64 Windows programs.")
                }
            }
            Ok(_) | Err(_) => Finding::new(
                "system.architecture",
                Category::System,
                "Architecture",
                Status::Unverifiable,
            )
            .file(arch_path)
            .note("Not exposed by this kernel (the file exists from Linux 6.1)."),
        });

        findings
    }
}

/// Reports the processor model and its virtualisation extensions.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct CpuProbe;

impl Probe for CpuProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "cpu"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Processor model and hardware virtualisation extensions, from /proc/cpuinfo"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        let path = "/proc/cpuinfo";
        let text = match ctx.read_to_string(path) {
            Ok(text) => text,
            Err(error) => return vec![unreadable("system.cpu", "Processor", path, &error)],
        };

        let model = parse::cpuinfo_field(&text, "model name")
            .or_else(|| parse::cpuinfo_field(&text, "Model"))
            .or_else(|| parse::cpuinfo_field(&text, "Hardware"));
        let cpu = match model {
            Some(model) => Finding::new(
                "system.cpu",
                Category::System,
                "Processor",
                Status::Detected,
            )
            .value(model),
            None => Finding::new(
                "system.cpu",
                Category::System,
                "Processor",
                Status::Unverifiable,
            )
            .note("no model name field"),
        }
        .file(path);

        let flags = parse::cpu_flags(&text);
        let has = |flag: &str| flags.iter().any(|f| f == flag);
        let extension = if has("vmx") {
            Some("Intel VT-x")
        } else if has("svm") {
            Some("AMD-V")
        } else {
            None
        };
        let virtualization = match extension {
            Some(name) => Finding::new(
                "system.cpu_virtualization",
                Category::System,
                "CPU virtualisation extensions",
                Status::Detected,
            )
            .value(name),
            None => Finding::new(
                "system.cpu_virtualization",
                Category::System,
                "CPU virtualisation extensions",
                Status::NotDetected,
            )
            .note(
                "Neither vmx nor svm is advertised. They may be disabled in the firmware, or \
                 hidden because this system is itself a virtual machine.",
            ),
        }
        .file(path);

        vec![cpu, virtualization]
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::finding::find;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::{scenarios, Fixture};

    #[test]
    fn recognises_cachyos() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = DistroProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(findings.len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::Detected);
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("CachyOS (rolling)"));
        assert!(findings[0]
            .notes
            .iter()
            .any(|n| n.contains("developed for")));
    }

    #[test]
    fn recognises_arch_and_foreign_distributions() {
        let arch = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = DistroProbe.run(&arch.context());
        assert!(findings[0].notes.iter().any(|n| n.contains("Arch-based")));

        let fedora = Fixture::new().file(
            "/usr/lib/os-release",
            "NAME=\"Fedora Linux\"\nID=fedora\nVERSION_ID=43\n",
        );
        let findings = DistroProbe.run(&fedora.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("Fedora Linux (43)"));
        assert!(findings[0]
            .notes
            .iter()
            .any(|n| n.contains("Not Arch-based")));
        assert_eq!(findings[0].evidence[0].source, "/usr/lib/os-release");
    }

    #[test]
    fn missing_os_release_is_not_detected() {
        let fixture = Fixture::new();
        let findings = DistroProbe.run(&fixture.context());
        assert_eq!(findings[0].status, Status::NotDetected);
    }

    #[test]
    fn maps_release_strings_to_kernel_packages() {
        let cases = [
            ("6.17.9-2-cachyos", Some("linux-cachyos")),
            ("6.17.9-2-cachyos-bore", Some("linux-cachyos-bore")),
            ("6.12.58-1-cachyos-lts", Some("linux-cachyos-lts")),
            ("6.17.9-2-cachyos-eac", Some("linux-cachyos-eac")),
            (
                "6.17.9-2-cachyos-rt-bore-lto",
                Some("linux-cachyos-rt-bore-lto"),
            ),
            ("6.17.8-arch1-1", None),
            ("6.8.0-45-generic", None),
        ];
        for (release, expected) in cases {
            assert_eq!(
                cachyos_kernel_package(release).as_deref(),
                expected,
                "{release}"
            );
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn reports_kernel_and_flavour() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_attested();
        let findings = KernelProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "system.kernel").and_then(|f| f.value.as_deref()),
            Some("6.17.9-2-cachyos-eac")
        );
        let flavour = find(&findings, "system.kernel_flavour").expect("flavour");
        assert_eq!(flavour.value.as_deref(), Some("linux-cachyos-eac"));
        assert!(flavour
            .notes
            .iter()
            .any(|n| n.contains("no connection with Riot Vanguard")));
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "system.architecture").and_then(|f| f.value.as_deref()),
            Some("x86_64")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn non_cachyos_kernel_is_reported_as_such() {
        let scenario = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = KernelProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "system.kernel_flavour").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn reports_cpu_and_extensions() {
        let amd = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = CpuProbe.run(&amd.context());
        assert!(find(&findings, "system.cpu")
            .and_then(|f| f.value.as_deref())
            .is_some_and(|v| v.contains("Ryzen")));
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "system.cpu_virtualization").and_then(|f| f.value.as_deref()),
            Some("AMD-V")
        );

        let intel = scenarios::cachyos_attested();
        let findings = CpuProbe.run(&intel.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "system.cpu_virtualization").and_then(|f| f.value.as_deref()),

        let vm = scenarios::vm_guest();
        let findings = CpuProbe.run(&vm.context());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "system.cpu_virtualization").map(|f| f.status),
            Some(Status::NotDetected)
        );
    }
```

```rs
//! Optional external programs.

use tuxgate_core::tools::EXTERNAL_TOOLS;
use tuxgate_core::{Category, Finding, Probe, Status, SystemContext};

/// Reports which optional programs are installed.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ToolsProbe;

impl Probe for ToolsProbe {
    fn id(&self) -> &'static str {
        "tools"
    }

    fn description(&self) -> &'static str {
        "Optional programs Tuxgate can use when present"
    }

    fn run(&self, ctx: &SystemContext) -> Vec<Finding> {
        EXTERNAL_TOOLS
            .iter()
            .map(|tool| {
                let id = format!("tools.{}", tool.program);
                match ctx.find_program(tool.program) {
                    Some(path) => Finding::new(id, Category::Tools, tool.program, Status::Detected)
                        .value(path.clone())
                        .file(path),
                    None => Finding::new(id, Category::Tools, tool.program, Status::NotDetected)
                        .note(format!(
                            "Optional. Used to {} (package {}).",
                            tool.purpose, tool.package
                        )),
                }
            })
            .collect()
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::finding::find;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::scenarios;

    #[test]
    fn reports_every_known_tool() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let findings = ToolsProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        assert_eq!(findings.len(), EXTERNAL_TOOLS.len());
        assert_eq!(
            find(&findings, "tools.efibootmgr").and_then(|f| f.value.as_deref()),
            Some("/usr/bin/efibootmgr")
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn missing_tools_name_their_package() {
        let scenario = scenarios::arch_minimal();
        let findings = ToolsProbe.run(&scenario.context());
        let vulkaninfo = find(&findings, "tools.vulkaninfo").expect("vulkaninfo");
        assert_eq!(vulkaninfo.status, Status::NotDetected);
        assert!(vulkaninfo.notes[0].contains("vulkan-tools"));
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-reporting/src/anonymize.rs`:

```rs
//! Removal of personal identifiers from report data.
//!
//! The first line of defence is not collecting them: Tuxgate never reads
//! the host name, serial numbers, MAC addresses, disk identifiers or the
//! full kernel command line into a finding. This module is the second
//! line. It rewrites what could still identify a person or a machine:
//!
//! - the home directory, replaced by `~`;
//! - any other `/home/<name>` path, replaced by `/home/<user>`;
//! - UUIDs, replaced by `<uuid>` (well-known UEFI vendor GUIDs excepted);
//! - MAC addresses, replaced by `<mac>`.

use tuxgate_bridge::WindowsDetection;
use tuxgate_core::efi::{EFI_GLOBAL_GUID, LOADER_GUID};
use tuxgate_core::{Finding, SystemContext};

/// GUIDs that identify a specification, not a machine.
const PUBLIC_GUIDS: [&str; 2] = [EFI_GLOBAL_GUID, LOADER_GUID];

/// Rewrites strings to remove personal identifiers.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Anonymizer {
    home: Option<String>,
}

fn is_hex_run(text: &str, lengths: &[usize], separator: char) -> bool {
    let mut parts = text.split(separator);
    lengths.iter().all(|length| {
        parts.next().is_some_and(|part| {
            part.len() == *length && part.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_hexdigit())
        })
    }) && parts.next().is_none()
}

fn is_uuid(text: &str) -> bool {
    text.len() == 36 && is_hex_run(text, &[8, 4, 4, 4, 12], '-')
}

fn is_mac(text: &str) -> bool {
    text.len() == 17 && is_hex_run(text, &[2, 2, 2, 2, 2, 2], ':')
}

/// Replaces UUIDs and MAC addresses that stand as separate tokens.
fn redact_identifiers(text: &str) -> String {
    let chars: Vec<(usize, char)> = text.char_indices().collect();
    let mut output = String::with_capacity(text.len());
    let mut index = 0;

    while index < chars.len() {
        let (offset, current) = chars[index];
        let at_boundary = index == 0 || !chars[index - 1].1.is_ascii_alphanumeric();

        if at_boundary && current.is_ascii_hexdigit() {
            let mut replaced = false;
            for (length, placeholder, matches) in [
                (36, "<uuid>", is_uuid as fn(&str) -> bool),
                (17, "<mac>", is_mac as fn(&str) -> bool),
            ] {
                let Some(candidate) = text.get(offset..offset + length) else {
                    continue;
                };
                let followed_by_more = text[offset + length..]
                    .chars()
                    .next()
                    .is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric());
                if matches(candidate) && !followed_by_more {
                    if PUBLIC_GUIDS.contains(&candidate.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str()) {
                        output.push_str(candidate);
                    } else {
                        output.push_str(placeholder);
                    }
                    // Candidates are ASCII, so bytes and characters coincide.
                    index += length;
                    replaced = true;
                    break;
                }
            }
            if replaced {
                continue;
            }
        }
        output.push(current);
        index += 1;
    }
    output
}

/// Replaces the user segment of every `/home/<name>` path.
fn redact_home_paths(text: &str) -> String {
    const PREFIX: &str = "/home/";
    let mut output = String::with_capacity(text.len());
    let mut rest = text;
    while let Some(position) = rest.find(PREFIX) {
        let after = &rest[position + PREFIX.len()..];
        let name_length = after
            .find(|c: char| c == '/' || c.is_whitespace() || matches!(c, ',' | ')' | '"' | '\''))
            .unwrap_or(after.len());
        output.push_str(&rest[..position]);
        if name_length == 0 {
            output.push_str(PREFIX);
        } else {
            output.push_str("/home/<user>");
        }
        rest = &after[name_length..];
    }
    output.push_str(rest);
    output
}

impl Anonymizer {
    /// Builds an anonymiser knowing the given home directory.
    pub fn new(home: Option<&str>) -> Self {
        Self {
            home: home
                .map(|h| h.trim_end_matches('/').to_string())
                // Never treat "/" or a one-character path as a home directory.
                .filter(|h| h.len() > 1),
        }
    }

    /// Builds an anonymiser from the inspected session's environment.
    pub fn from_context(ctx: &SystemContext) -> Self {
        Self::new(ctx.env("HOME"))
    }

    /// Returns the text with personal identifiers removed.
    pub fn scrub(&self, text: &str) -> String {
        let mut scrubbed = text.to_string();
        if let Some(home) = &self.home {
            scrubbed = scrubbed.replace(home.as_str(), "~");
        }
        scrubbed = redact_home_paths(&scrubbed);
        redact_identifiers(&scrubbed)
    }

    fn scrub_in_place(&self, text: &mut String) {
        *text = self.scrub(text);
    }

    /// Scrubs every free-text field of the findings.
    pub fn scrub_findings(&self, findings: &mut [Finding]) {
        for finding in findings {
            if let Some(value) = &mut finding.value {
                self.scrub_in_place(value);
            }
            for evidence in &mut finding.evidence {
                self.scrub_in_place(&mut evidence.source);
            }
            for note in &mut finding.notes {
                self.scrub_in_place(note);
            }
        }
    }

    /// Scrubs every free-text field of the Windows detection.
    pub fn scrub_windows(&self, windows: &mut WindowsDetection) {
        for text in windows
            .evidence
            .iter_mut()
            .chain(windows.notes.iter_mut())
            .chain(windows.partition_hints.iter_mut())
            .chain(windows.esp_loader.iter_mut())
            .chain(windows.systemd_boot_entry.iter_mut())
        {
            self.scrub_in_place(text);
        }
        for entry in &mut windows.efi_entries {
            self.scrub_in_place(&mut entry.description);
            if let Some(path) = &mut entry.file_path {
                self.scrub_in_place(path);
            }
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::{Category, Status};

    #[test]
    fn replaces_the_home_directory() {
        let anonymizer = Anonymizer::new(Some("/home/ada/"));
        assert_eq!(
            anonymizer.scrub("/home/ada/.local/share/Steam/steamapps/common/Proton 10.0"),
            "~/.local/share/Steam/steamapps/common/Proton 10.0"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn replaces_other_users_home_paths() {
        let anonymizer = Anonymizer::new(Some("/home/ada"));
        assert_eq!(
            anonymizer.scrub("found in /home/grace/games and /home/linus/.steam, see /home/"),
            "found in /home/<user>/games and /home/<user>/.steam, see /home/"
        );
        // Works without knowing any home directory.
        assert_eq!(
            Anonymizer::new(None).scrub("\"/home/ada\""),
            "\"/home/<user>\""
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn a_root_home_is_not_used_as_a_replacement_pattern() {
        let anonymizer = Anonymizer::new(Some("/"));
        assert_eq!(anonymizer.scrub("/usr/bin/wine"), "/usr/bin/wine");
    }

    #[test]
    fn redacts_uuids_but_keeps_public_guids() {
        let anonymizer = Anonymizer::new(None);
        assert_eq!(
            anonymizer.scrub("root=UUID=3f1c9a52-8d7e-4b1a-9c55-0a6d2f4e7b10 rw"),
            "root=UUID=<uuid> rw"
        );
        let efi_path = "/sys/firmware/efi/efivars/SecureBoot-8be4df61-93ca-11d2-aa0d-00e098032b8c";
        assert_eq!(anonymizer.scrub(efi_path), efi_path);
        assert_eq!(
            anonymizer.scrub("LoaderInfo-4A67B082-0A4C-41CF-B6C7-440B29BB8C4F"),
            "LoaderInfo-4A67B082-0A4C-41CF-B6C7-440B29BB8C4F"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn redacts_mac_addresses_only() {
        let anonymizer = Anonymizer::new(None);
        assert_eq!(
            anonymizer.scrub("link/ether 3c:7c:3f:1a:2b:4d brd"),
            "link/ether <mac> brd"
        );
        // A PCI address and a clock time are not MAC addresses.
        assert_eq!(
            anonymizer.scrub("0000:01:00.0 at 12:34:56"),
            "0000:01:00.0 at 12:34:56"
        );
        // Hex runs embedded in longer tokens are left alone.
        assert_eq!(
            anonymizer.scrub("x3c:7c:3f:1a:2b:4d and 3c:7c:3f:1a:2b:4dz"),
            "x3c:7c:3f:1a:2b:4d and 3c:7c:3f:1a:2b:4dz"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn handles_non_ascii_text() {
        let anonymizer = Anonymizer::new(Some("/home/zoé"));
        assert_eq!(
            anonymizer.scrub("Détecté dans /home/zoé/jeux — 3c:7c:3f:1a:2b:4d"),
            "Détecté dans ~/jeux — <mac>"
        );
        assert_eq!(anonymizer.scrub("é"), "é");
        assert_eq!(anonymizer.scrub(""), "");
    }

    #[test]
    fn scrubs_every_field_of_a_finding() {
        let mut findings = vec![Finding::new(
            "gaming.proton",
            Category::Gaming,
            "Proton",
            Status::Detected,
        )
        .value("build in /home/ada/x")
        .file("/home/ada/.steam/root")
        .note("disk 3f1c9a52-8d7e-4b1a-9c55-0a6d2f4e7b10")];
        Anonymizer::new(Some("/home/ada")).scrub_findings(&mut findings);
        assert_eq!(findings[0].value.as_deref(), Some("build in ~/x"));
        assert_eq!(findings[0].evidence[0].source, "~/.steam/root");
        assert_eq!(findings[0].notes[0], "disk <uuid>");
    }
}
```

`crates/tuxgate-reporting/src/lib.rs`:

```rs
//! Local technical reports.
//!
//! A report gathers the diagnostics, the readiness view, the Windows
//! detection and the games database into one document that can be rendered
//! as text, Markdown or JSON. Reports are written to local files only: this
//! crate has no way to send anything anywhere.

pub mod anonymize;
pub mod render;

use std::io::Write;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};

use serde::Serialize;
use tuxgate_bridge::WindowsDetection;
use tuxgate_compatibility::{Compatibility, GameStatus};
use tuxgate_core::{Finding, SystemContext};
use tuxgate_diagnostics::readiness::{attested_boot, Readiness};

pub use anonymize::Anonymizer;
pub use render::{render_json, render_markdown, render_text, TextOptions};

/// Schema version of the JSON report.
pub const REPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION: u32 = 1;

/// Printed at the end of every report.
pub const REPORT_DISCLAIMER: &str =
    "Tuxgate is an independent project. It is not affiliated with, \
     endorsed by or sponsored by Riot Games or CachyOS. It does not bypass, emulate or \
     interact with any anti-cheat. This report was produced locally and has not been sent \
     anywhere.";

/// Errors raised when writing a report.
#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum ReportError {
    #[error("{0} already exists; choose another name or pass --force to overwrite it")]
    Exists(PathBuf),

    #[error("cannot write {path}: {source}")]
    Io {
        path: PathBuf,
        #[source]
        source: std::io::Error,
    },

    #[error("cannot serialise the report: {0}")]
    Serialise(String),
}

/// Output format of a report.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Format {
    Text,
    Markdown,
    Json,
}

/// A game as it appears in a report.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct GameSummary {
    pub id: String,
    pub name: String,
    pub status: GameStatus,
    pub status_label: &'static str,
    pub summary: String,
}

/// Everything Tuxgate knows about the system at one moment.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize)]
pub struct Report {
    pub schema_version: u32,
    pub tuxgate_version: &'static str,
    /// UTC time of generation, ISO 8601.
    pub generated_at: String,
    /// True when personal identifiers were removed.
    pub anonymized: bool,
    /// True when an offline tree was inspected instead of the running system.
    pub offline: bool,
    pub findings: Vec<Finding>,
    pub readiness: Readiness,
    pub windows: WindowsDetection,
    /// Date of the games database.
    pub games_updated: String,
    pub games: Vec<GameSummary>,
    pub disclaimer: &'static str,
}

/// Parameters of a report collection.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct CollectOptions<'a> {
    /// Probe identifiers to skip.
    pub disabled_probes: &'a [String],
    /// Remove personal identifiers.
    pub anonymize: bool,
    /// Timestamp to record.
    pub generated_at: String,
}

impl Report {
    /// Inspects the system and assembles a report. Read-only.
    pub fn collect(
        ctx: &SystemContext,
        compatibility: &Compatibility,
        options: &CollectOptions<'_>,
    ) -> Self {
        let mut findings = tuxgate_diagnostics::run_all(ctx, options.disabled_probes);
        let mut windows = tuxgate_bridge::detect_windows(ctx);

        if options.anonymize {
            let anonymizer = Anonymizer::from_context(ctx);
            anonymizer.scrub_findings(&mut findings);
            anonymizer.scrub_windows(&mut windows);
        }

        // Derived after scrubbing, so nothing unscrubbed can reach it.
        let readiness = attested_boot(&findings);

        let games = compatibility
            .games
            .iter()
            .map(|game| GameSummary {
                id: game.id.clone(),
                name: game.name.clone(),
                status: game.status,
                status_label: game.status.label(),
                summary: game.summary.clone(),
            })
            .collect();

        Self {
            schema_version: REPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION,
            tuxgate_version: tuxgate_core::VERSION,
            generated_at: options.generated_at.clone(),
            anonymized: options.anonymize,
            offline: ctx.is_offline(),
            findings,
            readiness,
            windows,
            games_updated: compatibility.updated.clone(),
            games,
            disclaimer: REPORT_DISCLAIMER,
        }
    }

    /// Renders the report without colour, with evidence.
    pub fn render(&self, format: Format) -> Result<String, ReportError> {
        match format {
            Format::Text => Ok(render_text(
                self,
                &TextOptions {
                    color: false,
                    evidence: true,
                },
            )),
            Format::Markdown => Ok(render_markdown(self)),
            Format::Json => render_json(self),
        }
    }
}

/// Writes a report to a local file.
///
/// An existing file is never overwritten unless `force` is set. On Unix the
/// file is created readable by its owner only, since even an anonymised
/// report describes the machine.
pub fn write_report(path: &Path, content: &str, force: bool) -> Result<(), ReportError> {
    let mut options = std::fs::OpenOptions::new();
    options.write(true);
    if force {
        options.create(true).truncate(true);
    } else {
        options.create_new(true);
    }
    #[cfg(unix)]
    {
        use std::os::unix::fs::OpenOptionsExt;
        options.mode(0o600);
    }

    let mut file = options.open(path).map_err(|source| {
        if source.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists {
            ReportError::Exists(path.to_path_buf())
        } else {
            ReportError::Io {
                path: path.to_path_buf(),
                source,
            }
        }
    })?;
    file.write_all(content.as_bytes())
        .map_err(|source| ReportError::Io {
            path: path.to_path_buf(),
            source,
        })
}

#[cfg(test)]
pub(crate) mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use tuxgate_core::testing::scenarios;

    pub(crate) fn sample(anonymize: bool) -> Report {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let compatibility = Compatibility::builtin().expect("database");
        Report::collect(
            &scenario.context(),
            &compatibility,
            &CollectOptions {
                disabled_probes: &[],
                anonymize,
                generated_at: "2026-11-28T10:00:00Z".to_string(),
            },
        )
    }

    #[test]
    fn collects_every_section() {
        let report = sample(true);
        assert_eq!(report.schema_version, REPORT_SCHEMA_VERSION);
        assert!(report.anonymized);
        assert!(!report.offline);
        assert!(report.findings.len() > 20);
        assert_eq!(report.readiness.total, 8);
        assert_eq!(report.windows.status, tuxgate_core::Status::Detected);
        assert!(report.games.iter().any(|g| g.id == "valorant"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn anonymised_report_contains_no_user_path() {
        let report = sample(true);
        for format in [Format::Text, Format::Markdown, Format::Json] {
            let text = report.render(format).expect("render");
            assert!(
                !text.contains("/home/ada"),
                "{format:?} leaks the home path"
            );
            assert!(!text.contains("ada/"), "{format:?} leaks the user name");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn raw_report_keeps_paths_and_says_so() {
        let report = sample(false);
        assert!(!report.anonymized);
        let text = report.render(Format::Text).expect("render");
        assert!(text.contains("/home/ada/.local/share/Steam"));
        assert!(text.contains("not anonymised"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn disabled_probes_are_absent_from_the_report() {
        let scenario = scenarios::cachyos_desktop();
        let compatibility = Compatibility::builtin().expect("database");
        let disabled = vec!["tools".to_string(), "proton".to_string()];
        let report = Report::collect(
            &scenario.context(),
            &compatibility,
            &CollectOptions {
                disabled_probes: &disabled,
                anonymize: true,
                generated_at: "2026-11-28T10:00:00Z".to_string(),
            },
        );
        assert!(report.findings.iter().all(|f| !f.id.starts_with("tools.")));
        assert!(report.findings.iter().all(|f| f.id != "gaming.proton"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn refuses_to_overwrite_without_force() {
        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
            "tuxgate-report-test-{}-{}",
            std::process::id(),
            std::time::SystemTime::now()
                .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
                .map(|d| d.subsec_nanos())
                .unwrap_or(0)
        ));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).expect("dir");
        let path = dir.join("report.txt");

        write_report(&path, "first", false).expect("first write");
        assert!(matches!(
            write_report(&path, "second", false),
            Err(ReportError::Exists(_))
        ));
        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_to_string(&path).expect("read"), "first");

        write_report(&path, "third", true).expect("forced write");
        assert_eq!(std::fs::read_to_string(&path).expect("read"), "third");

        #[cfg(unix)]
        {
            use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
            let mode = std::fs::metadata(&path)
                .expect("metadata")
                .permissions()
                .mode();
            assert_eq!(mode & 0o777, 0o600);
        }

        let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
    }

    #[test]
    fn writing_into_a_missing_directory_is_an_io_error() {
        let path = std::env::temp_dir()
            .join("tuxgate-missing-directory-for-test")
            .join("report.txt");
        assert!(matches!(
            write_report(&path, "x", false),
            Err(ReportError::Io { .. })
        ));
    }
}
```
