`crates/tuxgate-reporting/src/render.rs`:

```rs
//! Rendering of a [`Report`] as text, Markdown or JSON.

use tuxgate_core::{Category, EvidenceKind, Finding, Status};
use tuxgate_diagnostics::readiness::Observed;

use crate::{Report, ReportError};

/// Options of the text renderer.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct TextOptions {
    /// Use ANSI colours.
    pub color: bool,
    /// List the files read and commands run under each finding.
    pub evidence: bool,
}

const RESET: &str = "\x1b[0m";
const BOLD: &str = "1";
const DIM: &str = "2";

/// Wraps text in an ANSI style when colours are enabled.
pub fn paint(enabled: bool, code: &str, text: &str) -> String {
    if enabled {
        format!("\x1b[{code}m{text}{RESET}")
    } else {
        text.to_string()
    }
}

/// ANSI colour of a status.
///
/// "Detected" is cyan rather than green on purpose: detecting something is
/// not always good news (a hypervisor, for instance).
pub fn status_color(status: Status) -> &'static str {
    match status {
        Status::Detected => "36",
        Status::NotDetected => "33",
        Status::Unverifiable => "35",
        Status::NotApplicable => DIM,
        Status::BlockedExternal => "31",
    }
}

/// Fixed-width status tag.
pub fn status_tag(status: Status, color: bool) -> String {
    let label = match status {
        Status::Detected => "detected",
        Status::NotDetected => "not detected",
        Status::Unverifiable => "unverifiable",
        Status::NotApplicable => "n/a",
        Status::BlockedExternal => "blocked",
    };
    paint(color, status_color(status), &format!("[{label:^14}]"))
}

fn observed_tag(observed: Observed, color: bool) -> String {
    let (label, code) = match observed {
        Observed::Yes => ("yes", "36"),
        Observed::No => ("no", "33"),
        Observed::Unknown => ("unknown", "35"),
    };
    paint(color, code, &format!("[{label:^9}]"))
}

const INDENT: &str = "                   ";

fn push_finding(out: &mut String, finding: &Finding, options: &TextOptions) {
    let headline = match &finding.value {
        Some(value) => format!("{}: {value}", finding.title),
        None => finding.title.clone(),
    };
    out.push_str(&format!(
        "  {} {headline}\n",
        status_tag(finding.status, options.color)
    ));
    for note in &finding.notes {
        out.push_str(&format!(
            "{INDENT}{}\n",
            paint(options.color, DIM, &format!("- {note}"))
        ));
    }
    if options.evidence {
        for evidence in &finding.evidence {
            let kind = match evidence.kind {
                EvidenceKind::File => "read",
                EvidenceKind::Command => "ran",
                EvidenceKind::Derived => "from",
            };
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "{INDENT}{}\n",
                paint(options.color, DIM, &format!("{kind}: {}", evidence.source))
            ));
        }
    }
}

fn heading(out: &mut String, title: &str, color: bool) {
    out.push('\n');
    out.push_str(&paint(color, BOLD, title));
    out.push('\n');
}

/// Renders a report as plain or coloured text.
pub fn render_text(report: &Report, options: &TextOptions) -> String {
    let color = options.color;
    let mut out = String::new();

    out.push_str(&paint(
        color,
        BOLD,
        &format!("Tuxgate {} system report", report.tuxgate_version),
    ));
    out.push('\n');
    let privacy = if report.anonymized {
        "anonymised"
    } else {
        "not anonymised: contains user paths"
    };
    out.push_str(&format!("Generated {} ({privacy})\n", report.generated_at));
    if report.offline {
        out.push_str("Offline tree inspected: no command was run.\n");
    }

    for category in Category::ALL {
        let findings: Vec<&Finding> = report
            .findings
            .iter()
            .filter(|f| f.category == category)
            .collect();
        if findings.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }
        heading(&mut out, category.title(), color);
        for finding in findings {
            push_finding(&mut out, finding, options);
        }
    }

    heading(&mut out, "Attested-boot model (informational)", color);
    out.push_str(&format!(
        "  {} of {} properties observed.\n",
        report.readiness.observed, report.readiness.total
    ));
    for item in &report.readiness.items {
        out.push_str(&format!(
            "  {} {}: {}\n",
            observed_tag(item.observed, color),
            item.property,
            item.detail
        ));
    }
    out.push_str("  Cannot be checked from this machine:\n");
    for line in &report.readiness.not_locally_checkable {
        out.push_str(&format!("    - {line}\n"));
    }
    out.push_str(&format!(
        "  {}\n",
        paint(color, DIM, report.readiness.disclaimer)
    ));

    heading(&mut out, "Windows (dual boot)", color);
    out.push_str(&format!(
        "  {} {}\n",
        status_tag(report.windows.status, color),
        report.windows.summary()
    ));
    for note in &report.windows.notes {
        out.push_str(&format!(
            "{INDENT}{}\n",
            paint(color, DIM, &format!("- {note}"))
        ));
    }
    if options.evidence {
        for evidence in &report.windows.evidence {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "{INDENT}{}\n",
                paint(color, DIM, &format!("source: {evidence}"))
            ));
        }
    }

    heading(
        &mut out,
        &format!("Riot titles (database of {})", report.games_updated),
        color,
    );
    for game in &report.games {
        out.push_str(&format!(
            "  {} {}: {}\n",
            status_tag(game.status.as_status(), color),
            game.name,
            game.status_label
        ));
    }
    out.push_str(&format!(
        "  {}\n",
        paint(
            color,
            DIM,
            "Statuses are documented from sources, not tested by Tuxgate."
        )
    ));

    out.push('\n');
    out.push_str(&paint(color, DIM, report.disclaimer));
    out.push('\n');
    out
}

/// Escapes text for use inside a Markdown table cell.
fn cell(text: &str) -> String {
    text.replace('|', "\\|").replace('\n', " ")
}

/// Renders a report as Markdown, suitable for an issue or a forum post.
pub fn render_markdown(report: &Report) -> String {
    let mut out = String::new();
    out.push_str(&format!(
        "# Tuxgate {} system report\n\n",
        report.tuxgate_version
    ));
    out.push_str(&format!("- Generated: {}\n", report.generated_at));
    out.push_str(&format!(
        "- Anonymised: {}\n",
        if report.anonymized { "yes" } else { "no" }
    ));
    if report.offline {
        out.push_str("- Offline tree inspected: no command was run\n");
    }

    for category in Category::ALL {
        let findings: Vec<&Finding> = report
            .findings
            .iter()
            .filter(|f| f.category == category)
            .collect();
        if findings.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }
        out.push_str(&format!("\n## {}\n\n", category.title()));
        out.push_str("| Check | Status | Value |\n|---|---|---|\n");
        for finding in &findings {
            out.push_str(&format!(
                "| {} | {} | {} |\n",
                cell(&finding.title),
                finding.status.label(),
                cell(finding.value.as_deref().unwrap_or(""))
            ));
        }
        let noted: Vec<&&Finding> = findings.iter().filter(|f| !f.notes.is_empty()).collect();
        if !noted.is_empty() {
            out.push('\n');
            for finding in noted {
                for note in &finding.notes {
                    out.push_str(&format!("- **{}**: {note}\n", finding.title));
                }
            }
        }
    }

    out.push_str("\n## Attested-boot model (informational)\n\n");
    out.push_str(&format!(
        "{} of {} properties observed.\n\n",
        report.readiness.observed, report.readiness.total
    ));
    out.push_str("| Property | Observed | Detail |\n|---|---|---|\n");
    for item in &report.readiness.items {
        let observed = match item.observed {
            Observed::Yes => "yes",
            Observed::No => "no",
            Observed::Unknown => "unknown",
        };
        out.push_str(&format!(
            "| {} | {observed} | {} |\n",
            cell(item.property),
            cell(&item.detail)
        ));
    }
    out.push_str("\nCannot be checked from this machine:\n\n");
    for line in &report.readiness.not_locally_checkable {
        out.push_str(&format!("- {line}\n"));
    }
    out.push_str(&format!("\n> {}\n", report.readiness.disclaimer));

    out.push_str("\n## Windows (dual boot)\n\n");
    out.push_str(&format!(
        "**{}** — {}\n",
        report.windows.status.label(),
        report.windows.summary()
    ));
    if !report.windows.notes.is_empty() {
        out.push('\n');
        for note in &report.windows.notes {
            out.push_str(&format!("- {note}\n"));
        }
    }

    out.push_str(&format!(
        "\n## Riot titles (database of {})\n\n",
        report.games_updated
    ));
    out.push_str("| Title | Documented status |\n|---|---|\n");
    for game in &report.games {
        out.push_str(&format!(
            "| {} | {} |\n",
            cell(&game.name),
            game.status_label
        ));
    }
    out.push_str("\nStatuses are documented from sources, not tested by Tuxgate.\n");

    out.push_str(&format!("\n---\n\n{}\n", report.disclaimer));
    out
}

/// Renders a report as pretty-printed JSON.
pub fn render_json(report: &Report) -> Result<String, ReportError> {
    serde_json::to_string_pretty(report)
        .map(|mut json| {
            json.push('\n');
            json
        })
        .map_err(|e| ReportError::Serialise(e.to_string()))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::tests::sample;
    use crate::REPORT_DISCLAIMER;

    #[test]
    fn text_has_every_section_and_the_disclaimers() {
        let text = render_text(
            &sample(true),
            &TextOptions {
                color: false,
                evidence: true,
            },
        );
        for expected in [
            "Tuxgate ",
            "(anonymised)",
            "\nSystem\n",
            "\nGraphics\n",
            "\nSecurity features\n",
            "Attested-boot model (informational)",
            "5 of 8 properties observed.",
            "Windows (dual boot)",
            "Riot titles (database of ",
            "VALORANT: blocked by an external requirement",
            "not tested by Tuxgate",
            "read: /sys/class/tpm/tpm0/tpm_version_major",
            "ran: wine --version",
            REPORT_DISCLAIMER,
        ] {
            assert!(text.contains(expected), "missing {expected:?} in:\n{text}");
        }
        assert!(
            !text.contains('\x1b'),
            "plain text must not contain escapes"
        );
    }

    #[test]
    fn evidence_can_be_hidden() {
        let text = render_text(
            &sample(true),
            &TextOptions {
                color: false,
                evidence: false,
            },
        );
        assert!(!text.contains("read: /sys/class/tpm"));
        assert!(text.contains("TPM: TPM 2.0"));
    }

    #[test]
    fn colour_adds_escapes_only_when_asked() {
        let coloured = render_text(
            &sample(true),
            &TextOptions {
                color: true,
                evidence: false,
            },
        );
        assert!(coloured.contains("\x1b[36m"));
        assert!(coloured.contains(RESET));
        assert_eq!(paint(false, "31", "x"), "x");
    }

    #[test]
    fn status_tags_have_a_constant_width() {
        let widths: Vec<usize> = Status::ALL
            .iter()
            .map(|s| status_tag(*s, false).chars().count())
            .collect();
        assert!(widths.iter().all(|w| *w == widths[0]), "{widths:?}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn markdown_is_well_formed() {
        let markdown = render_markdown(&sample(true));
        assert!(markdown.starts_with("# Tuxgate "));
        assert!(markdown.contains("## Security features"));
        assert!(markdown.contains("| Secure Boot | detected | enabled |"));
        assert!(markdown.contains("| VALORANT | blocked by an external requirement |"));
        assert!(markdown.contains(REPORT_DISCLAIMER));
        // Every table row has the same number of cells as its header.
        for block in markdown.split("\n\n") {
            let rows: Vec<&str> = block.lines().filter(|l| l.starts_with('|')).collect();
            if let Some(header) = rows.first() {
                let columns = header.matches('|').count();
                for row in &rows {
                    let unescaped = row.replace("\\|", "");
                    assert_eq!(unescaped.matches('|').count(), columns, "row: {row}");
                }
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn markdown_cells_escape_pipes() {
        assert_eq!(cell("a|b\nc"), "a\\|b c");
    }

    #[test]
    fn json_is_valid_and_complete() {
        let json = render_json(&sample(true)).expect("json");
        let value: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(&json).expect("valid json");
        assert_eq!(value["schema_version"], 1);
        assert_eq!(value["anonymized"], true);
        assert_eq!(value["windows"]["status"], "detected");
        assert_eq!(value["readiness"]["total"], 8);
        assert!(value["findings"].as_array().is_some_and(|f| f.len() > 20));
        let valorant = value["games"]
            .as_array()
            .and_then(|games| games.iter().find(|g| g["id"] == "valorant"))
            .expect("valorant");
        assert_eq!(valorant["status"], "blocked");
    }
}

`deny.toml`:

```toml
# Supply-chain policy, enforced in CI with `cargo deny check`.

[graph]
targets = ["x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu"]
all-features = true

[advisories]
# Fail on any RustSec advisory, including unmaintained crates.
version = 2
yanked = "deny"

[licenses]
version = 2
# Licences compatible with distributing Tuxgate under GPL-3.0-or-later.
allow = [
    "MIT",
    "Apache-2.0",
    "Unicode-3.0",
    "Unlicense",
    "GPL-3.0-or-later",
]
confidence-threshold = 0.9

[bans]
multiple-versions = "warn"
wildcards = "deny"
# Path dependencies between workspace crates are allowed to omit a version.
allow-wildcard-paths = true

# Tuxgate must not be able to talk to the network. These crates are the
# usual ways a Rust program opens a connection; none may enter the tree.
deny = [
    { crate = "reqwest", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "hyper", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "ureq", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "curl", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "curl-sys", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "isahc", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "attohttpc", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "surf", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "h2", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "tokio", reason = "no async runtime or socket layer is needed" },
    { crate = "async-std", reason = "no async runtime or socket layer is needed" },
    { crate = "mio", reason = "no socket layer is needed" },
    { crate = "socket2", reason = "no socket layer is needed" },
    { crate = "rustls", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "native-tls", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "openssl", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "openssl-sys", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "tungstenite", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "trust-dns-resolver", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
    { crate = "hickory-resolver", reason = "Tuxgate makes no network request" },
]

[sources]
# Crates may only come from crates.io.
unknown-registry = "deny"
unknown-git = "deny"
allow-registry = ["https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"]
allow-git = []

`docs/DISTRIBUTION.md`:

```md
# Distribution

This document describes how Tuxgate can be delivered to users. It separates what **exists today** from what is **planned**. No repository, AUR package or signing key mentioned as planned exists yet; do not copy a planned command expecting it to work.

## What exists today

One way to install: build the package from a checkout of the source tree.

```sh
./packaging/arch/make-source.sh
cd packaging/arch && makepkg -si
```

`make-source.sh` archives the current commit; `packaging/arch/PKGBUILD` builds from that archive. Details are in `INSTALLATION.md`.

There is **no** `pacman -S tuxgate` and **no** AUR package at the time of writing.

## Option 1: the AUR (planned)

The Arch User Repository hosts build recipes, not binaries. Users build locally with `makepkg` or an AUR helper.

Two packages would be published:

| Package | Builds | For |
|---|---|---|
| `tuxgate` | a tagged release tarball, verified by checksum | most users |
| `tuxgate-git` | the latest commit of the default branch | testers |

### Prerequisites

1. The source repository is public. Done: it has been public since 28 November 2026.
2. A release is tagged (`v0.1.0`), so that a tarball with a stable checksum exists.
3. The maintainer has an AUR account with an SSH key registered.
4. The names `tuxgate` and `tuxgate-git` are free on the AUR and in the official repositories. Checked on 28 November 2026: both were free.

### Release recipe (template)

It is the in-tree recipe with two lines changed: `source` points at a tagged tarball and `sha256sums` pins it. The tag and the checksum do not exist yet.

```sh
pkgname=tuxgate
pkgver=0.1.0
pkgrel=1
pkgdesc="Unofficial companion for Riot Games titles on Linux: diagnostics, compatibility facts, dual-boot help"
arch=('x86_64')
url="https://github.com/LoanPleurmeauBonnin/tuxgate"
license=('GPL-3.0-or-later')
depends=('glibc' 'libgcc')
makedepends=('cargo')
# optdepends and options: as in packaging/arch/PKGBUILD
source=("$pkgname-$pkgver.tar.gz::$url/archive/refs/tags/v$pkgver.tar.gz")
sha256sums=('<sha256 of the release tarball>')

# prepare(), build(), check() and package(): exactly as in packaging/arch/PKGBUILD.
```

The `-git` variant uses `source=("git+$url.git")`, `sha256sums=('SKIP')`, a `pkgver()` function derived from `git describe`, and declares `provides=('tuxgate')` and `conflicts=('tuxgate')`.

### Publishing

```sh
git clone ssh://aur@aur.archlinux.org/tuxgate.git
# add PKGBUILD, then:
makepkg --printsrcinfo > .SRCINFO
git add PKGBUILD .SRCINFO && git commit && git push
```

### Source verification

- The release tarball is pinned by `sha256sums`.
- Every Rust dependency is pinned by `Cargo.lock`, which records a checksum per crate; `cargo fetch --locked` and `cargo build --frozen` refuse anything else.
- Signed tags (`git tag -s`) plus a `validpgpkeys` entry would add authenticity on top of integrity. This is worth doing once a maintainer key exists.

### Trade-offs

- Advantages: the standard community channel on Arch and CachyOS; users can read the recipe; no infrastructure to run.
- Drawbacks: users compile (about a minute); installation needs an AUR helper or a manual `makepkg`; the AUR is unreviewed, so users must trust the recipe.

## Option 2: a signed pacman repository (planned, later)

A custom repository would allow `sudo pacman -S tuxgate` with prebuilt, signed packages.

### What it requires

1. A dedicated **signing key**, kept offline or in a hardware token, with a published fingerprint.
2. Reproducible builds in CI, producing `tuxgate-<ver>-<rel>-x86_64.pkg.tar.zst`.
3. Signing each package (`makepkg --sign` or `gpg --detach-sign`) and the database (`repo-add --sign --verify tuxgate.db.tar.gz *.pkg.tar.zst`).
4. Static hosting of the packages, their `.sig` files and the database.
5. User-side setup:

```sh
# Illustrative only. No such key or server exists.
sudo pacman-key --recv-keys <FINGERPRINT>
sudo pacman-key --lsign-key <FINGERPRINT>
```

```ini
# /etc/pacman.conf, illustrative only
[tuxgate]
SigLevel = Required DatabaseOptional
Server = https://<host>/$arch
```

### Trade-offs

- Advantages: one-command installation; no compilation; signatures verified by pacman on every install and upgrade.
- Drawbacks: the signing key becomes a high-value secret, since whoever holds it can ship code that pacman installs as root; hosting and key rotation must be maintained; users add a third-party repository to a root-level trust store.

### Recommendation

Start with the AUR. A signed repository is only justified once there is a release cadence and a maintainer able to protect a signing key properly. Shipping an unsigned or carelessly signed repository would be worse than shipping none.

## Option 3: inclusion in the CachyOS repositories

This is the CachyOS team's decision. It would require the project to be public, stable and of interest to them. Nothing has been requested or agreed. Tuxgate is not endorsed by CachyOS.

## Compatibility with CachyOS kernels

Tuxgate is a user-space program. It ships no kernel module, no DKMS package and no kernel hook, so there is nothing to rebuild or sign per kernel. It runs unchanged on every CachyOS kernel variant, including `linux-cachyos-eac`, where out-of-tree modules are refused.

## What the package installs

| Path | Content |
|---|---|
| `/usr/bin/tuxgate` | the program |
| `/usr/share/bash-completion/completions/tuxgate` | bash completion |
| `/usr/share/zsh/site-functions/_tuxgate` | zsh completion |
| `/usr/share/fish/vendor_completions.d/tuxgate.fish` | fish completion |
| `/usr/share/man/man1/tuxgate.1` | manual page |
| `/usr/share/doc/tuxgate/` | README, research, security and installation notes, changelog, example configuration |

No install script runs. Removing the package removes all of the above. Per-user files (`~/.config/tuxgate`, `~/.local/share/tuxgate`) are created only when the user records a test or writes a configuration, and, as is the convention for pacman packages, are left in place on removal.

```

`docs/TESTING.md`:

```md
# Testing

This document says what is tested automatically, what must be tested by hand, and **what has actually been run**. A green build means the code does what its tests say. It does not mean any game runs.

## Automated tests

Run everything with:

```sh
cargo test --workspace
```

| Crate | Tests | What they cover |
|---|---|---|
| `tuxgate-core` | 44 | Status model, configuration parsing and validation, XDG paths, rooted file access, command runner, EFI variable decoding, neutralisation of control characters, dates |
| `tuxgate-diagnostics` | 62 | Each probe on four simulated machines and on hand-built edge cases; the readiness view; no false detection on an empty tree |
| `tuxgate-compatibility` | 20 | Database validity and honesty rules; history round trip; regression and divergence detection |
| `tuxgate-bridge` | 30 | Boot entry decoding including malformed and hostile data; Windows detection; reboot planning and execution with a scripted runner; `PATH` hijack resistance |
| `tuxgate-reporting` | 20 | Anonymisation; leak checks on all formats; rendering; file writing |
| `tuxgate-cli` | 9 + 24 | Grammar, verdict wording; the real binary driven end to end against simulated machines; packaging consistency |

Total: 209.

### Simulated machines

Defined in `crates/tuxgate-core/src/testing.rs`:

| Scenario | Description |
|---|---|
| `cachyos_desktop` | CachyOS, NVIDIA, Secure Boot on, TPM 2.0, IOMMU, systemd-boot, dual boot with Windows, Steam with two Proton builds |
| `cachyos_attested` | CachyOS on the `-eac` kernel: lockdown integrity, enforced module signatures, kexec off, BPF LSM |
| `arch_minimal` | Arch, AMD graphics, Secure Boot off, no TPM, no IOMMU, GRUB, no Windows |
| `vm_guest` | QEMU/KVM guest, legacy BIOS, virtio display |

These are file trees and scripted command answers written from the documented formats of `/proc`, `/sys` and efivarfs. They are not captures of real machines.

### Continuous integration

`.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on every push and pull request:

| Job | What it does |
|---|---|
| `test` | `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy -D warnings`, `cargo test`, and a run of the binary on the CI machine |
| `msrv` | `cargo check` with Rust 1.85 |
| `supply-chain` | `cargo deny check`: advisories, licences, sources and the ban on networking crates |
| `package` | In an Arch Linux container and in a CachyOS container: `makepkg` (including the test suite), `namcap`, install, run every command as an ordinary user, upgrade to a higher `pkgrel`, remove, and check that no installed file is left behind |

A CI container has no real firmware, TPM or boot manager. The `package` job validates the package, not the diagnostics.

## What has been run

| Check | Status | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Unit and end-to-end tests | Run, passing | Windows 10 development machine; Linux in CI |
| Cross-check for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` | Run, passing | Windows 10 development machine |
| Package build, install, upgrade, removal | Run by CI on every push; see the latest workflow run for the current result | Arch Linux and CachyOS containers |
| Every command on a real Linux system | Run by CI on every push: a virtual machine (Ubuntu on Hyper-V), where the diagnostics correctly report the hypervisor, Secure Boot state and boot manager | GitHub-hosted runner |
| Diagnostics on real CachyOS hardware | **Not run** | — |
| `windows detect` on a real dual-boot machine | **Not run** | — |
| `windows reboot` on a real machine | **Not run** | — |
| Any Riot title | **Not run, and not planned by the project** | — |

Until the "not run" rows are filled in by someone with the hardware, treat the corresponding features as unvalidated.

## Manual checklist for real hardware

For anyone with a CachyOS or Arch machine. Record the outcome in the table at the end and open a pull request.

### 1. Diagnostics

```sh
tuxgate doctor --verbose
```

Compare each line with what you know about the machine:

- [ ] Distribution and kernel match `cat /etc/os-release` and `uname -r`.
- [ ] The CachyOS kernel package matches `pacman -Qo /usr/lib/modules/$(uname -r)`.
- [ ] Each GPU is listed with the right vendor and driver (`lspci -k`).
- [ ] Secure Boot matches `bootctl status` or `sbctl status`.
- [ ] TPM matches `cat /sys/class/tpm/tpm0/tpm_version_major`.
- [ ] IOMMU matches `ls /sys/kernel/iommu_groups | wc -l`.
- [ ] Lockdown matches `cat /sys/kernel/security/lockdown`.
- [ ] The boot manager is the one you use.
- [ ] Nothing asked for a password, and no file was created outside your home directory.
- [ ] Anything Tuxgate could not read is shown as "unverifiable", not as absent.

### 2. Windows detection

On a dual-boot machine:

- [ ] `tuxgate windows detect` reports Windows, with the same entry number as `efibootmgr`.

On a Linux-only machine:

- [ ] It reports "No Windows installation was found" and exits 0.

### 3. Reboot, dry run first

```sh
tuxgate windows reboot --dry-run
```

- [ ] The plan names the method that fits your boot manager.
- [ ] The commands shown are what you would run by hand.

### 4. Reboot for real

Save your work first.

```sh
tuxgate windows reboot
```

- [ ] Typing anything other than `reboot to windows` cancels, and nothing changes (`efibootmgr` shows no `BootNext`).
- [ ] Dismissing the administrator prompt cancels without restarting.
- [ ] Confirming restarts into Windows.
- [ ] The restart after that returns to Linux: the boot order is unchanged.

### 5. Package

- [ ] `./packaging/arch/make-source.sh` then `makepkg -si` in `packaging/arch` builds and installs.
- [ ] `sudo pacman -R tuxgate` leaves no file from `pacman -Ql tuxgate`.

### Results

| Date | Machine | Kernel | Boot manager | Sections passed | Tester | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| | | | | | | |

`docs/config.example.toml`:

```toml
# Tuxgate configuration. Every key is optional; the values below are the defaults.

[output]
# "auto", "always" or "never"
color = "auto"

[report]
# Strip user names, home paths and hardware identifiers from reports.
anonymize = true

[probes]
# Probe identifiers to skip, for example ["wine", "vulkan"].
disabled = []
# Time limit for each diagnostic command, in seconds (1 to 60).
command_timeout_secs = 5

```

`packaging/arch/PKGBUILD`:

```
# Maintainer: Tuxgate contributors
#
# Builds Tuxgate from a snapshot of the source tree this file lives in.
# From the repository root:
#
#     ./packaging/arch/make-source.sh
#     cd packaging/arch && makepkg -si
#
# make-source.sh archives the current commit next to this file. Apart from
# that archive, the build downloads only the Rust crates pinned, with their
# checksums, in Cargo.lock. The package installs no service, no setuid
# binary, no polkit rule and no install script.
#
# A release recipe differs only in `source` (a tagged tarball) and in
# `sha256sums` (a real checksum). See docs/DISTRIBUTION.md.

pkgname=tuxgate
pkgver=0.1.0
pkgrel=1
pkgdesc="Unofficial companion for Riot Games titles on Linux: diagnostics, compatibility facts, dual-boot help"
arch=('x86_64')
url="https://github.com/LoanPleurmeauBonnin/tuxgate"
license=('GPL-3.0-or-later')
depends=('glibc' 'libgcc')
makedepends=('cargo')
optdepends=(
  'systemd: list boot entries and restart into Windows (bootctl, systemctl)'
  'efibootmgr: select Windows for the next boot on systems without systemd-boot'
  'polkit: ask for administrator rights for the single efibootmgr call (pkexec)'
  'util-linux: look for Windows partitions (lsblk)'
  'vulkan-tools: confirm that a Vulkan driver loads (vulkaninfo)'
  'wine: report the installed Wine version'
)
# The release profile carries no debug information, so a -debug package
# would be empty.
options=('!debug')
# A local snapshot made by make-source.sh: there is no fixed checksum to pin.
source=("$pkgname-$pkgver.tar.gz")
sha256sums=('SKIP')

prepare() {
  cd "$pkgname-$pkgver"
  export RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=stable
  cargo fetch --locked --target "$(rustc -vV | sed -n 's/host: //p')"
}

build() {
  cd "$pkgname-$pkgver"
  export RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=stable
  export CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target
  cargo build --frozen --release --package tuxgate-cli

  mkdir -p generated
  target/release/tuxgate completions bash > generated/tuxgate.bash
  target/release/tuxgate completions zsh > generated/_tuxgate
  target/release/tuxgate completions fish > generated/tuxgate.fish
  target/release/tuxgate man > generated/tuxgate.1
}

check() {
  cd "$pkgname-$pkgver"
  export RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=stable
  export CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target
  cargo test --frozen --workspace
}

package() {
  cd "$pkgname-$pkgver"
  install -Dm755 target/release/tuxgate "$pkgdir/usr/bin/tuxgate"

  install -Dm644 generated/tuxgate.bash \
    "$pkgdir/usr/share/bash-completion/completions/tuxgate"
  install -Dm644 generated/_tuxgate \
    "$pkgdir/usr/share/zsh/site-functions/_tuxgate"
  install -Dm644 generated/tuxgate.fish \
    "$pkgdir/usr/share/fish/vendor_completions.d/tuxgate.fish"
  install -Dm644 generated/tuxgate.1 \
    "$pkgdir/usr/share/man/man1/tuxgate.1"

  install -Dm644 -t "$pkgdir/usr/share/doc/tuxgate" \
    README.md RESEARCH.md SECURITY.md INSTALLATION.md CHANGELOG.md \
    docs/config.example.toml
}

```

`packaging/arch/make-source.sh`:

```sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Creates the source archive that packaging/arch/PKGBUILD builds from:
# a snapshot of the current commit, written next to the PKGBUILD.
#
# Uncommitted changes are not included, so that a package always
# corresponds to a commit.
set -euo pipefail

here="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
repo="$(git -C "$here" rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
version="$(sed -n 's/^pkgver=//p' "$here/PKGBUILD")"

if [ -z "$version" ]; then
  echo "error: cannot read pkgver from $here/PKGBUILD" >&2
  exit 1
fi

if [ -n "$(git -C "$repo" status --porcelain)" ]; then
  echo "warning: uncommitted changes are not part of the archive" >&2
fi

archive="$here/tuxgate-$version.tar.gz"
git -C "$repo" archive --format=tar.gz --prefix="tuxgate-$version/" \
  --output="$archive" HEAD
echo "$archive"

```
